# SwarmAI -- Lessons & Patterns

_This document captures what worked, what failed, and what to watch for when developing SwarmAI. Swarm updates this automatically after significant sessions. You can also edit it directly._

## What Worked
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [pitfall] **修复 skill 引用 drift 前必须先区分两个命名空间,"看起来失效"≠"真失效"(P1 verify-before-assume)。** SwarmAI 有两个正交的 skill 命名空间:(a) **SwarmAI-native**(`backend/skills/` 下,如 s_project-manager/s_persist/s_autonomous-pipeline/s_pollinate)—— SkillRegistry 只扫这里,是运行时可加载的;(b) **DDD-native**(`s_ddd-manager/persist/pipeline/pollinate`,定义在 `backend/templates/ddd-skills/`,由 `swarm_workspace_manager.DDD_NATIVE_SKILLS` 声明)—— 是 ships-INSIDE-each-DDD 的模板命名空间,SwarmAI 运行时**不加载**。权威定义在 `s_project-manager/SKILL.md` 的 "Two distinct namespaces — do not conflate" 表 + 决定性 commit `16ac21bb`。**修 drift 时的两个判断:** (1) 一个 `s_ddd-*` 引用出现在 SwarmAI-native skill(如 s_ddd-distribute)的 NOT-FOR 里 = 真 drift(该 skill 运行时够不到 s_ddd-* 模板),应改成 SwarmAI-native 对应名;但同样的 `s_ddd-*` 出现在 core/(swarm_workspace_manager/ddd_*_registry)= 命名空间的正确定义,**绝不能改**。差点因"全仓 grep 看到 s_ddd-manager 就当失效"而误改 core。(2) **改生产 skill 名会连带打破测试 fixture** —— test_skill_registry.py 硬编码了已删的 save-memory 当 Memory 类样本,改 registry 后测试红;这是 fixture 自身的 drift(committed test 编码了旧行为),把 fixture 同步到 persist 并 mutation-test 证明断言非 vacuous,而不是回退生产改动。(2026-08-23, run_cf3dd08e, bugfix)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **删除一个 SwarmAI skill 的完整 blast radius = 目录本身 + 3 类消费者,且 skill 使用统计不能单独作删除依据。** 删 skill 时必须 grep 三处消费者:(a) 其他 skill 的 SKILL.md/INSTRUCTIONS.md 交叉引用(NOT-FOR/对比块);(b) `skill_registry.py` 的 `SKILL_CATEGORIES` 分类映射(反向映射 `_SKILL_TO_CATEGORY` 在 import 时自动重建,改前者即可);(c) job yaml / hook 的 `skill:` 字段。**但 `.claude/skills/` 的投影副本无需手动清** —— `projection_layer._cleanup_stale_entries`(line 426-475)在每次 `project_skills` 循环末尾 rmtree 掉不在 target_skills 的条目,删源后下次 daemon 启动自愈。**关键判断错误要避免:skill_metrics 的 calls=0 不是删除依据** —— metrics 仅回溯约 11 天且经历过 data.db 重建,大量按需 skill(pdf/docx/translate/image-gen)calls=0 却有价值;删除只能靠硬证据(frontmatter 自称 SUPERSEDED / 功能是另一 skill 的真子集且 TRIGGER 撞车 / 全仓 grep 零引用),靠调用数会误删一批好 skill。同族陷阱:同名函数不是 skill 引用(`main.py::_run_inprocess_scheduler` vs skill `s_scheduler`),grep 判 dangling 时要排除。(2026-08-23, run_2d48419e, bugfix)

- [pitfall] **A product contract change that adds a new field the consumer READS must migrate EVERY consumer — including standalone `scripts/` fault-injection harnesses, not just `tests/`.** run_dcd668a6 added a CPU-liveness gate to `lifecycle_manager._check_streaming_timeout` reading `unit.TOOL_FREE_HARD_CEILING_S` + `_tool_free_hang_verdict`; it R27-migrated the 4 sibling `tests/` but NOT `scripts/fault_inject_dumb_spawn.py` (a golden-case GS_RTH003 driver) → the harness's bare MagicMock unit made `stall(float) <= ceiling(MagicMock)` raise TypeError, silently breaking the eval gate ~a week later. The tell: a new `getattr(unit, "NEW_FIELD")` in a hot path is a contract change — grep for ALL mock/stub constructors of that object across `tests/` AND `scripts/`, not just the obvious test files. Fix mirrored the already-migrated sibling `test_lifecycle_watchdog.py:786` (RP45 reuse-proven-driver); teeth preserved (negative-mode still discriminates at `stall > effective_timeout`, verdict=wedged is non-vacuous). (2026-08-21, run_a8486702, bugfix)

- [pitfall] **When N independent timers/tasks guard ONE invariant (here: 2 tool-free kill** paths — orchestrator wait_for + watchdog backstop), gating only one is split-brain: the bug recurs via the ungated door. Enumerate every terminator, route all through ONE authority. Gate-1 SSA caught this design-level. (2026-08-20, run_dcd668a6, auto-cultivated)

- [decision] **✅ ALREADY-SATISFIED — 「模型可见=必被记录」不变量已实现(pipeline run_026449e5 EVALUATE DEFER,零代码).** 起 pipeline 落地借鉴自 DeepSeek Harness 的这条不变量时,Understanding Gate 判定需求已由 COE 修复自身(run_e47c1cfb)全部实现且测试覆盖:completeness gate=`prompt_builder.py assert_core_sections()`+`required_prompt_sections()` SSOT(`:1372`);故障隔离=`_core_committed` 核心先提交(`:949-954`);fail-loud=ERROR+`_context_degraded`+mirror 进 metadata(`:1397`);R28 恢复路径执行测试=`test_ac4_core_failure_is_loud`(mock load_all 抛错、断言降级路径真执行)等,实测 6/6 绿。**教训:借鉴外部好思路前,先 grep 自己代码——好方案可能已经在跑。**这是 P9(justify before build)+ ALREADY-SATISFIED 的活例:pipeline 的正确价值是 EVALUATE 阶段零代码杀掉一个已实现的需求。设计溯源:`knowledge/designs/2026-08-15-model-visible-means-logged-invariant-proposal.md`;研究报告:`Knowledge/Reports/2026-08-15-deepseek-harness-cordis-research.html`。(2026-08-15, run_026449e5, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Gate-2 meta-review caught a cross-fix the code specialists could not: the same** target-repo mapping was stated in TWO places (per-row label via TARGET_REPO_MAP + a hardcoded prose legend in the guideline) → guaranteed drift. Code review checks each spot in isolation; only the cross-fix lens sees two-sources-of-truth. Fix: render the legend FROM the same map (Object.entries), single source. When a value appears in a row AND a legend, derive both from one constant. (2026-08-15, run_8d2ec26c, auto-cultivated)

- [decision] **NO-GO at THINK killed an unbuildable recall feature in zero code — the recall SCORE is rank-within-set, not a quality signal (run_7187f4b7).** Requirement: "widen the agentic-re-search nudge from `recall EMPTY` → `empty OR top-score < threshold`" so weak hits also prompt re-grep. EVALUATE's first live probe (40 production task-queries) returned top normalized score = **1.0 for ALL 40** — because `_normalize_bm25_scores` min-max normalizes PER RESULT SET, so the best hit is always 1.0 by construction. **The gated-on signal is a constant → the feature is unbuildable (P6).** Two more falsifications from reading source, not inferring: (a) BOTH nudge branches ALREADY inject a passive re-search hint (`session_router.py:902` recalled / `:978` empty) → the "weak hit silently proceeds" gap is already partially covered; (b) `_score_for` returns None for scoreless domains → a naive top-score would falsely fire. Reusable tell: **before building a mechanism over a recall "problem", verify the SIGNAL it needs actually EXISTS in live data** — this is the 3rd recall build-a-mechanism idea killed by evidence (siblings run_5d6a7e83 synonym-miss, run_7741c727 recall-pool), same C042/O030 family. Full architecture note → TECH.md § Recall Architecture ("the recall SCORE is NOT a quality signal"). (2026-08-15, run_7187f4b7, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Gate-2 adversarial caught a HIGH the builder did not: a shallow-copied** verification with a fail-loud try/except at line N still crashes on a truthy NON-dict corpus_source because src.get() runs BEFORE the try. A fail-loud contract must guard the INPUT SHAPE (isinstance) before any .get(), not just wrap the load. The fresh-context spawn earned its keep. (2026-08-15, run_3df6cc61, auto-cultivated)

Both were confident-wrong self-authored code; the adversarial STANCE (not intelligence) caught them. - [pitfall] **When deleting a module, the consumer-grep MUST cover non-.py files —** SKILL/INSTRUCTIONS.md files contain live `from core.X import Y` code blocks that a `grep ...py:` filter silently skips. EVALUATE grepped backend/ but filtered to .py: and missed s_ddd-weekly-report/INSTRUCTIONS.md:87 which imports the deleted module; Gate-2 caught it. Fix: grep the raw symbol across ALL file types (grep -rn symbol backend/), not just .py. (2026-08-14, run_781ffbd9, auto-cultivated)

- [guideline] **Gate-0 M3 skeptic killed a "root-fix" that was a WRONG-FRAME + would have introduced an O030 violation (2026-08-12, run_c3765055).** Asked to "fix the soft-compact subsystem", I framed a bug as "soft-compact timeout leaves the in-flight `/compact` command hung → collides with next send → exit -9, must add `interrupt()` on timeout". The EVALUATE M3 skeptic (fresh-context, read source+logs) refuted it on three grounds: (1) the exit-9 incidents in daemon.log were NOT the timeout path — they were a concurrent user-Refresh killing the subprocess mid-drain, ALREADY fixed by `send_aborted_state_flip` (commit 437bc74c, deployed) + the `compact_aborted` denoise branch; (2) no log evidence ever traced exit-9 to the 300s timeout path; (3) the 300s timeout is a DELIBERATELY-generous hang-guard so a slow-but-progressing ~600K-token summary can COMPLETE — adding `interrupt()` on timeout would guillotine real in-progress work = an O030/STEERING#2 violation (容灾 truncating real work). Net: the 4-item task correctly shrank to 2 real fixes (a lying docstring + an icon-sizing inconsistency); the "root-fix" was dropped as a non-problem. **The tell (same as C048/C049 improve-before-justify): I reached to add a mechanism the moment I heard "subsystem broken", before verifying the problem was real and on a path that matters. Gate-0 is the external P9 check — run the skeptic against the PREMISE, not just the plan.** (2026-08-12, run_c3765055, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **Context 文件"大 ≠ 该压":先判断体量是噪音还是判断密度,再决定动不动 (2026-08-11).** 当 MEMORY/EVOLUTION/KNOWLEDGE 显得大时,先问"大是脂肪还是判断沉淀"再压。实证方法:(1) `s_context-hygiene` scan 出 drift 候选,逐条 read-and-judge——`context_health_hook` 的自主 decay 早已清过真 drift,scanner 抓的残留基本是嵌在 lesson 里的领域事实(data-URL 上限、事件内存规模、R30#4 反面教材本身)或 auto-gen 区(PROJECTS 表 / 文件计数索引 / KNOWLEDGE Index,重启即重写),删了碰 C046 红线或白删;(2) 语义压缩(叙述→pattern+tell)实测:最典型的 blow-by-blow correction 压到极限只省约 1/5 tokens,且已开始删掉支撑 claim 的实证细节。**结论:真 drift 无可清 + 语义压缩不划算时,文件大是判断密度高的证明,压它 = 削判断(P6 拿数字当目标 + C046 gut-judgment 双违规)。** 真要控 loader 体量,方向是让 EVOLUTION/KNOWLEDGE 也走 MEMORY 式 selective injection(按需注入、不砍内容),而非语义压缩——且先问"loader 在 1M context 下真的疼吗"(truncation 从未在生产触发)。(2026-08-11, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [decision] **Goal-run "class-completeness" gate — per-cycle adversarial is structurally blind to untouched sibling paths (2026-08-10, run_9e236074 design + run_1d3df9e6 impl).** When a goal run migrates a CLASS (all callers of a chokepoint / all sibling ingestion paths) across many cycles, each cycle's Gate-2 reviews only THAT cycle's diff — a sibling path no cycle touched (the ungated MEMORY *decisions* path, found only by a whole-flow E2E pass after 7 per-cycle adversarials missed it) is invisible to every cycle. Fix = a mandatory closeout gate that enumerates the WHOLE class and proves each member migrated-or-carved-out; a MISS = BLOCK. Two anti-self-deception rules earned via the design's own adversarial: (1) enumeration MUST grep a **physical chokepoint** (`_run_locked_write` / `apply_to_ddd`), never an echo'd hand-list — a hand-list inherits the same blind spot that caused the miss; (2) a requirement containing migrate/unify/"route all" → **mandatory declaration, enforced at EVALUATE Gate-0** (opt-in enforcement fails exactly when you don't realize you need it). (2026-08-10, run_9e236074, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **A 100%-reject rate is indistinguishable from a broken judge WITHOUT telemetry — separate infra-failure from real judgment (2026-08-10, run_0d60e04e).** "The judge rejected 21/21" read as a harsh-but-working judge; the telemetry gauge revealed **44% were fail-closed** (Bedrock timeout/throttle/unparseable → forced "suspect"), i.e. the judge never actually ran on them. A fail-closed default (safe for the gate) is a SILENT LIAR for calibration: it looks like a verdict but isn't one. Rule: any fail-closed decision path that also feeds a quality metric MUST tag WHY it failed closed, and the metric MUST exclude infra-failures before reading pass/discard rates — else you "measure" a judge that wasn't consulted. (2026-08-10, run_0d60e04e, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **A gate stamp that BOTH mutates a proposal AND persists to disk creates a re-entry hole — the guard must not depend on the field the stamp mutates:** in the unified ingestion gate, the protected-zone check was nested inside `if trust != "passed"`. Once the self_adversarial judge stamps a proposal `passed` (and it persists to disk / is re-read / retried), that branch is skipped → a judge-only stamp silently gains protected-zone (SELF.md) auto-write authority. Fix = an UNCONDITIONAL top-of-function invariant (protected zones require `trust_source == inherited_gate2`), evaluated regardless of the passed flag or entry state. General rule: a guard keyed on a mutable+persisted field must be re-expressed as an invariant that reads the field it TRUSTS (source), not the field the mutation FLIPS. (2026-08-10, run_0d60e04e, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **When a design doc contradicts its own criterion, the CODE breaks the tie — code-trace before flattening:** run_0d60e04e's design classified 2 orchestrator paths as "ingestion → gate" while its OWN criterion said "modifying existing entries is not ingestion". Reading the code (both were `current_text→proposed_text` verbatim-replace of existing doc text) proved them value-refresh carve-outs, not ingestion. Flattening them into an append-oriented gate would have run a "is this a good NEW entry?" judge on a drift-correction diff (wrong-layer). Rule: a spec's classification is a hypothesis; the code's actual behavior is truth — verify the behavior before acting on the label, especially before a structural change. (2026-08-10, run_0d60e04e, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Layer-2 dependency-scoped regression caught a bug MY OWN test introduced:** asyncio.run() in a handler test tore down the thread event loop, poisoning a sibling test's deprecated get_event_loop(). Fix: save->new_event_loop->run->close->restore. asyncio.run() in a test is not hermetic when others use get_event_loop(). (2026-08-10, run_5165013e, auto-cultivated)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [guideline] **The FIRST write path on a read-only router is a security event, not just a** feature: a free-text field (posted_url) that round-trips to an <a href> with no CSP is stored-XSS. The security specialist caught the javascript:/data: vector that spec + correctness review structurally cannot. Rule: any user string that later renders as a URL/href needs a scheme allowlist at BOTH the write (authoritative) and render (defense-in-depth) layers — and the two must AGREE (Gate-2 caught https:x passing the backend scheme-only check but not the frontend /^https?:// guard; fix = require netloc). (2026-08-10, run_b290eb6f, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **Gate-1 SSA(b) earned the run: it flagged that the prune/window cutoff MUST be** local-naive %Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S to match the writer (a UTC-Z cutoff string-compares wrong against suffix-less rows and deletes the wrong set). A regression test (test_prune_cutoff_matches_writer_local_format) + fixing the SAME lying UTC-Z default in the v7 DDL (found in Gate-2) closed the class — timestamp-format parity is a recurring bug family when a table has a writer + range-query readers + a DDL default, all three must agree. (2026-08-10, run_35f42b75, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **A lookup-map keyed by a string that DOWNSTREAM code mutates or that a SECOND** source populates separately is a lossy-re-derivation trap: it silently misses (falls back to the polluted key). Prefer a DETERMINISTIC inverse function over a stored map when the enriched form has a fixed structure — it survives mutation and covers all sources. (Gate-2 caught my enriched->raw map missing JobResults + breaking on the [UNVERIFIED] rewrite — ironic, in the very run fixing lossy re-derivation.) (2026-08-09, run_fdbc0f08, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **An AST 'does this handler communicate' discriminator must NOT ast.walk() the** whole handler — it descends into nested def/class bodies, so a logger/raise inside an UNUSED nested function falsely marks a silent handler as communicating (false-negative gate hole). Scan the directly-executed body only; skip nested FunctionDef/AsyncFunctionDef/ClassDef/Lambda. Caught by Gate-2, not by me. (2026-08-09, run_9af622ee, auto-cultivated)

- [decision] **DEFER at EVALUATE is the correct outcome for a measured-gated run whose gate is unmet — Run 4 (dedicated recall pool) is the exemplar (PIT57).** A run gated on a measured problem must produce an OBSERVATION of that problem at EVALUATE, or DEFER. Two independent falsifications, both from live observation not inference: (1) **premise source-falsified** — `recall_all` runs its 5 legs SEQUENTIALLY within one call and `session_router` dispatches that whole call as a SINGLE `run_in("io",…)` task, so there is no per-leg fan-out to starve the io pool (the thing the pool would "fix" isn't structurally happening; the design keeps legs sequential by intent). (2) **the gate's measurement did not exist** — Run 3's `recall_metrics` substrate was committed but the daemon binary mtime was OLDER than the commit → undeployed → zero starvation data. Building a pool for an unmeasured + source-falsified problem = the C042/O030/STEERING#2 trap the design explicitly deferred against. **Two reusable tells: (a) before building a mechanism over a problem, verify the problem is OBSERVED in real data — not merely plausible; (b) verify deploy-state by binary-mtime-vs-commit, NEVER by inference (R16b) — "committed" ≠ "deployed" ≠ "collecting data".** Same C042/C044 family as the two prior DEFERs (viz over an unpopulated relation) — reaching to build for a condition that isn't there yet. (2026-08-09, run_7741c727, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

The unit for cross-fix review is MULTIPLE fixes composing, distinct from single-fix adversarial. (2026-08-09, run_35f42b75, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **rAF-throttle + memo are COMPLEMENTARY on a drag hot path, not redundant: rAF** cuts the FREQUENCY of the state write (100+Hz→≤1/frame), memo cuts the COST of each surviving re-render (skips the heavy child reconcile). Neither subsumes the other — without memo, ≤60 heavy reconciles/sec still janks; without rAF, the panel box still re-renders 100+/sec. - [guideline] **A seed constant and its mount-computed replacement must share the SAME fallback** assumptions or they diverge on the unhappy path. The seed DEFAULT_MIN_HEIGHT_PX assumed +16 padding; the mount compute fell back to ||0 padding when getComputedStyle read empty → computed 60 < seed 76 (a downward jump). Fix: mirror the lineHeight ||20 fallback with padding ||8 so compute==seed. Adversarial-caught MED — the general rule: when a value is seeded then re-measured, the re-measure fallbacks must reproduce the seed. (2026-08-07, run_17d708f4, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **DDD-card redesign (run_9ada46ae): my first-draft reflex on a UI-design task was "surface every signal" — a DATA DUMP — when the ask was "answer the user's JUDGMENT questions". XG rejected BOTH my first card AND my first layout; the good versions were his, not mine.** The card v1 = 4-question tiles + a 40-line per-section diagnostics WALL + a header total ("N entries") — exactly the "堆叠 function/features 不是 design" failure XG named earlier this session (run_fdeaead8 / R5 Patch-Instinct's UI sibling). It took XG's "这不是我们要的…下面那些小字是干什么的" to reframe it into verdict-first + a legible ontology + only-the-actionable. Same with layout: my v1 (big card spanning a full row + a grid below) wasted space; XG's "左大卡+右竖排两个小卡" was plainly better. **The durable tell (a UI-design-specific instance of measure/understand-before-build): before laying out ANY info-dense card, do NOT start from "what fields does the data have" — start from "what 2-4 decisions does the user make looking at this, in what priority", then every element earns its place by answering one. A card that shows all the fields is the data-dump smell; a card that answers the questions and HIDES the rest (drill-down) is the design. When I catch myself enumerating available signals into tiles, that enumeration IS the smell.** Honest read (per my meta-correction, run_dcce7023): the harness — XG's visual judgment + 3 gates — carried this, NOT improved design instinct on my part. My v1 reflex was unchanged from the data-dump default; do not read the good final card as "I got better at design". (2026-08-05, run_9ada46ae)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **A guard/regression test can be SILENTLY DEFEATED by the DOM shape it asserts against — a green guard ≠ a working guard.** The Principle-1 guard `expect(txt).not.toMatch(/\b\d+\s+entries\b/i)` was meant to forbid a "size" number on the card. I then shipped a header `<span>…7 types</span><span>{total} entries</span>` — adjacent spans render `textContent` as `"…7 types28 entries"`, and `\b\d+` needs a word boundary before the digits, but `s→2` (`types28`) is NOT a boundary, so the regex silently missed the exact string it existed to catch. The guard was GREEN while the violation shipped; the adversary caught it by RENDERING and reading the real text, not by trusting the green. **Durable tells: (1) a regex guard over concatenated-DOM `textContent` must NOT rely on `\b`/whitespace anchors that inter-element concatenation destroys — assert against the SCOPED subtree's text (`getByTestId('ddd-ontology').textContent`) for the bare forbidden word, or use a structural query, not a boundary-anchored pattern over the whole card. (2) When a "forbidden-content" guard is green, prove it's non-vacuous the same way a RED test is proven: temporarily ADD the forbidden thing and confirm the guard goes RED. A guard never seen to fail is theater.** Same family as the CLASS-A test-theater tell ("grep your test for patch(<symbol>)" / "does it import+call prod or recompute") — here: "does the assertion's pattern survive the real DOM concatenation, and have I seen it fail?" (2026-08-05, run_9ada46ae, adversarial-caught)

- [pitfall] **Capability/platform detection MUST be a single SSOT — a local copy silently rots on a platform migration, and one drifted copy usually means two.** `UnsupportedRenderer` hand-rolled `'__TAURI__' in window`; Tauri 1.x→2.x moved the global to `__TAURI_INTERNALS__`, so the local check went permanently false in the packaged app (Open/Reveal buttons hidden, Copy copied the workspace-relative path) while the canonical `isDesktop()` (checks `__TAURI_INTERNALS__ || __TAURI__`) was fine. The durable rule: when you find ONE drifted copy of a detection/config predicate, **grep the WHOLE tree for the pattern** — this run's meta-review found a SECOND hardcoded copy (`FilePreviewModal.tsx:85`, functionally-correct-but-not-SSOT) that changeset-scoped adversarial review never looked at. Fix = both → `isDesktop()` (RP58 single-authority); after, `grep -rn "'__TAURI__' in window" desktop/src` = comments only. Mutation-proven test (revert isDesktop→__TAURI__ → RED). - [decision] **Frontend regressions were RECURRING (not unlucky) because of a DOUBLE gap — cognition + pipeline — and both are now closed structurally.** Meta-analysis over ~60 recent frontend commits (XG prompt, 2026-08-03): nearly every regression fell into FOUR axes — ① per-tab state stored too shallow (cross-tab leak/freeze), ② render-authority duplicated (the OT01 false-spinner/frozen family, ~33×), ③ scroll not re-pinned after a reflow (re-pin ×3), ④ a new input surface listening on the wrong channel (RP56). TWO root causes, not one: (a) **cognition** — we handle frontend less often, so the fragile-zone reflex isn't native; (b) **pipeline** — the pipeline's own gates were backend-shaped (the RP table ran ~2:1 backend-vs-frontend concepts; BUILD's SMOKE step was entirely pytest/import-smoke, `vitest`+jsdom can't see geometry). So the SAME classes kept shipping and getting caught by Gate-2 / external review / XG *after* the fact, never prevented at BUILD. **Structural fix (4 places, this session):** RP57-60 added to REVIEW_PATTERNS (the 4 axes + a jsdom-can't-see-geometry verification gate); a FRONTEND SMOKE step added to build.md (multi-tab isolation + build:all-for-geometry + exercise-every-entry, gated on what the change touches); a "Frontend Fragile Zones 改前对照表" added to TECH.md (BUILD reads it — maps each axis to the existing correct pattern: CanvasTabState / store-sole-authority / re-pin hook / ChatDropZone); and this entry. The meta-heuristic (already held for backend, now explicit for frontend): **recurrence count ≈ "you're fixing the wrong LAYER / the gate is shaped for the wrong DOMAIN" counter** — when a whole domain keeps regressing, audit whether the pipeline's checklist even covers that domain, don't just add one more lesson. (2026-08-03, run_8cf932a0 follow-up)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **A new drop-zone that only handles OS `dataTransfer.files` + text IGNORES the app's PRIMARY internal drag format — and that IS the advertised interaction.** New Brain's dropzone omitted the `application/json` branch that ChatDropZone (the codebase's established drag pattern) uses to receive a FileTreeItem dragged from the Workspace Explorer — so dragging in material already in the workspace was silently dropped, and OS `.path` is `undefined` in the Tauri webview anyway (only basename survives, unresolvable by the agent). Rule: before building ANY drop zone, grep for the existing drag PRODUCER's format (here `application/json` from FileTreeNode) — the reliable path is the app's internal one, not OS files. Caught by post-delivery review, verified against ChatDropZone.tsx, fixed with a Path-1 json branch (accepting directory→SHELF, unlike ChatDropZone's file-only) + 2 tests. (2026-08-03, run_8cf932a0)

New Brain became a 4th `<...Overlay onDispatch={handleDispatchJobPrompt}/>` line + a JobsRunsOverlay-isomorphic component. The tell that saved a whole invented pathway: when the fix reaches for a "new mechanism", first grep for the existing precedent — the sibling overlays were the template. (2026-08-03, run_8cf932a0)

- [pitfall] **A boolean-return guard is NOT a throw guard — Gate-2 HIGH.** NewBrainOverlay.handleCreate gated close() on `onDispatch`'s returned boolean (F4: keep launcher open on false) but assumed onDispatch only ever RETURNS — if it THROWS (addTab/CustomEvent can raise) the exception dead-ends the click: no close, no feedback, stuck button. The authorship blind spot: I designed for the false path I invented and never for the throw path. Rule: any callback whose failure you handle by return value ALSO needs a try/catch — a throwing dependency is a distinct failure mode from a false-returning one. Fixed with try/catch → landed=false + a test that asserts the click doesn't throw and stays open. (2026-08-03, run_8cf932a0)

- [decision] **To kill a hand-copied list that drifts across a language boundary, DERIVE the copy you can + BIND the copy you can't — and expect the drift-DETECTING test to itself need a rewrite.** The agent's UI-action allowlist lived in 3 hand-aligned places: the LeftNav SSOT `ALL_SHOW_EVENTS` (TS), `UI_COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` (Python), `UI_COMMAND_TABLE` (TS) — bound only by a parity test asserting the two tables equal each other, NOT the SSOT (XG: "你靠测试感觉不靠谱啊 没有共享 list 吗"). Fix: (1) frontend IMPORTS `ALL_SHOW_EVENTS` and DERIVES its show-* rows (`Object.fromEntries(ALL_SHOW_EVENTS.map(...))`) → frontend drift structurally impossible; (2) Python can't import TS, so the backend allowlist stays a literal but a test parses `ALL_SHOW_EVENTS` from source and asserts `UI_COMMAND_ALLOWLIST == {derived show-*} + {open-canvas, back-to-chat}` → backend drift fails the build. **The non-obvious cost the risk-probe caught: deriving the FE table BROKE the old parity test's TS-literal regex** (spread rows are no longer `'k': {event:'...'}` literals, and `back-to-chat` now uses the imported `BACK_TO_CHAT_EVENT` identifier, not a string) — so the test had to be rewritten to bind to the SSOT source instead of scraping the derived table. Lesson: a "derive from SSOT" refactor also invalidates any test that parsed the now-derived literal; plan to rewrite that test to anchor on the SSOT, and mutation-verify it RED in BOTH directions (inject a fake SSOT event → backend RED; re-hand-list a show-* literal → derivation-shape test RED). Same family as run_693df058 (SECTION_SCAFFOLD SSOT), extended to a cross-language boundary. (2026-08-02, run_22905625, trivial)

- [pitfall] **A meta/config key that ONLY tests set (never a prod writer) = green-tests-but-dead-in-prod, the exact class golden eval exists to catch.** code_intel's `repo_root` was written to graph_meta only in test fixtures (they `set_meta` by hand); no production build path (`bulk_insert` callers) ever persisted it. So `get_meta("repo_root")` was None in the live db → `_build_context` returned empty → symbol injection was silently dead system-wide for an unknown-long period, while every unit test passed (they seeded the key themselves). Two reusable tells: (1) when a read path requires `get_meta(K)` / a config key, grep whether any NON-TEST writer sets K — `grep -rn 'set_meta("K"' | grep -v test` returning only tests is the smoking gun; (2) the durable fix is to persist K at the single CHOKE-POINT every create/rebuild path funnels through (here `bulk_insert`), not per-caller — then migrate ALL callers (R27: grep found 4, not the 2 I first assumed) and make it idempotent (INSERT OR REPLACE) so it self-heals a pre-fix db on next rebuild. The golden cases GS_COD008/009 (behavior probes against the real hook) caught what unit tests structurally could not, because they DON'T self-seed the meta — they exercise the live db. Test fixtures that construct their own preconditions hide missing production writers; a probe against real state does not. (2026-07-30, run_2f7a0f8f)

- [pitfall] **"Replace N+1 with an aggregate" is NOT automatically faster — Gate-1's live EXPLAIN saved a 2× regression.** Fixing the session-quality sampler's N+1 (200 per-session `list_by_session` reads, ~3.3s), I assumed one GROUP BY aggregate must be faster. Gate-1 forced an EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN on real data: the bare `WHERE role='user' GROUP BY session_id` was a full SCAN at ~7.3s — SLOWER than the N+1 loop it replaced, because `role` had no usable index. The real fix was a composite index `(role, session_id)` (role FIRST = seek prefix) → index-only SEARCH ~100ms; the aggregate alone would have shipped a regression. Reusable rule: an "aggregate replaces N+1" perf fix MUST be validated with `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` on production-shaped data BEFORE claiming the win — an unindexed aggregate can be worse than the bounded per-row loop. And when you add the index, reuse the SAME filter predicate as the code path you're replacing (`_PENDING_EXCLUDE_SQL`) or the counts silently drift across the sampling threshold. (2026-07-29, run_c0d30950)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Shipping a feature is only half-done until every DIAGRAM/DOC that describes the subsystem is re-synced — and the user, not the pipeline, caught the stalest one.** The Layer②③ real-session loop shipped 2026-07-28 (code+tab+job); but the Guide's hand-authored architecture SVG still drew the 2-layer "golden-exam" model, the Evaluator Methodology JSX still rendered only 2 of 3 execution methods (Behavior omitted), AND a separate vs-Rocky scorecard still marked 层②③ as ❌/未做 — actively UNDERSTATING us. Same root as the append-only-cultivation staleness class: a feature-ship must include a sweep of "what docs/diagrams/scorecards reference this subsystem?" Static hand-drawn assets (SVG) and comparison scorecards are the highest-risk because nothing auto-regenerates them. Fold the doc-refresh into the SAME run as the feature, or it silently rots. (2026-07-28, run_b79aab34)

- Write-time injection-rejection gate (run_6af300b3): reject prompt-injection at the WRITE boundary of agent-owned stores (corrections.jsonl / DailyActivity) so poison never enters and can never be replayed into a future agent's context or fed to the live judgment classifier. Reject-at-write beats read-side provenance because the store is a REPLAY surface — the [RECALLED] header was prose, this is a gate. The false-positive defense is line-start anchoring + an adversarial-OBJECT requirement (a bare imperative "from now on"/"do not report" is ALSO how a legit lesson reads; the ATTACK OBJECT — ignore/skip governance, output-no-findings, become-a-role — is what separates poison from advice; line position alone cannot). Empirically 0 FP on 86 real DailyActivity files vs 26 for naive substring. (2026-07-27, run_6af300b3, commits a9467027+21292f9f)

- Gate-2 caught the authorship trap in a security gate: my own 27 tests were GREEN while two real HIGH bugs lived in the code — a marker-lead-in BYPASS ("- ignore all previous instructions" sails through, and "- {x}" is DailyActivity's native lesson rendering) AND an imperative-first FALSE-POSITIVE ("From now on, run tests" wrongly redacted). Root: my test corpus only contained mid-sentence MENTIONS, so it structurally could not surface the imperative-FIRST class. The reusable tell for a pattern-matching security gate: a green suite whose positive+negative cases are all one structural shape is hiding the other shapes — the FP corpus must include payloads that START with a matched token but have a benign object, and attack payloads wearing the store's NATIVE formatting (bullets/quotes/numbers). Mutation-verified both directions. (2026-07-27, run_6af300b3)

- [pitfall] **Read the FULL target-ecosystem contract before fixing to it — I fixed to HALF the AIM name rule and shipped a fix that still failed. (run_05e60d5b supersedes the run_62055da6 lesson below.)** AIM agentskills.io has TWO name rules: (a) `name == dirname` AND (b) name matches `^[a-z0-9-]+$` (lowercase/digit/hyphen, NO underscore). run_62055da6 saw only rule (a) from the first build error and "fixed" it by rewriting the emitted SKILL.md `name` to match the raw `s_`-prefixed dir (`s_ddd-manager`) — which satisfies (a) but VIOLATES (b) (the `_`). The next `brazil-build` surfaced rule (b) ("name has invalid format"), proving the first fix was aimed at half the contract. The real fix: normalize the emitted DIR name itself to a compliant form (`s_ddd-manager` → `ddd-manager`), so name==dir AND charset-legal, and sync every consumer of the emitted name (agent-spec `skillNames`, `_materialize_shared`). **Reusable lessons: (1)** when a build/validator rejects on rule X, DON'T fix only X — read the validator's FULL ruleset (the spec link in the error, or grep the validator) so you don't ship a fix that trips rule Y on the next run; a one-error-at-a-time loop against an external validator is a tell you haven't read its contract. **(2)** My first-round approach choice (rewrite name to match the `s_` dir) was WRONG and I had *explicitly rejected* the correct one (rename the dir) citing "s_ prefix is a SwarmAI invariant" — but that invariant is INTERNAL; a distribution target's namespace rules OVERRIDE internal conventions. When rejecting the option that adapts to the target in favor of preserving an internal form, that's the C042/C044 "wrong frame" tell. **(3)** renaming the emitted dir is a CONTRACT MIGRATION (R27): grep ALL consumers of the old name — `_copy_skill_dirs` (dir), `_materialize_shared` (found+fixed a real silent-skip bug), agent-spec `skillNames` (dangling-ref), 8 test call-sites. **(4)** Gate-2 caught a HIGH I missed: `_compliant_skill_name` returned `""` for a degenerate dir (`s_`/`s___`/pure-unicode) → `out_skills / "" == out_skills` → skill collapses into the skills root, corrupting siblings + empty name; fail-loud on empty is the fix. Normalization fns that strip chars MUST guarantee non-empty output. Mutation-verified (revert→3 RED); E2E on real ai_ready_repo emits 5 compliant dirs. **Honest meta: the fix took TWO pipeline runs because I read the contract incrementally; the harness (2nd build error + Gate-2) caught both my misses, not my own judgment.** (2026-07-23, run_05e60d5b, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **[SUPERSEDED by run_05e60d5b above — this fix was insufficient] DDD distribution emit is a CONTRACT-ADAPTATION layer, not a verbatim copy — the target ecosystem's rules can conflict with SwarmAI's internal conventions.** `ddd_packager._copy_skill_dirs` used `shutil.copytree` to copy skill dirs into a distributed package as-is. But AIM's aim-build (agentskills.io) requires each `SKILL.md` frontmatter `name` to EQUAL its dir name, while SwarmAI deliberately keeps them distinct (dir `s_repo-to-ddd`, frontmatter `name: repo-to-ddd`) — so every emitted skill failed aim-build validation ("name must match directory name"), BUILD FAILED rc=1. Fix: after copytree, rewrite the EMITTED copy's `name` line to `== dst.name` (emit-layer only, source untouched); single-point fix in `_copy_skill_dirs` covers BOTH emit paths (aim@536 + open_plugin@592, shared fn). **Reusable lessons: (1)** `copytree` into a foreign package is a contract blind-spot — always ask "what does the TARGET ecosystem require that our internal form violates?"; **(2)** a regex replacement string must be a CALLABLE (`lambda _m: f"...{x}"`), never an f-string template, when the value comes from a filesystem name — a template interprets `\g<n>`/`\1` as backreferences (PIT15 latent-footgun; Gate-2 caught it, dir names are `s_[a-z0-9-]` today so not yet live); **(3)** tolerate YAML `name :` (ws before colon) — aim-build validates that form too. Mutation-verified (revert → 3 RED); E2E on the real ai_ready_repo emit confirms 5/5 skills name==dirname. (2026-07-23, run_62055da6, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Surgical Edit adjacent to a neighbor test can silently TRUNCATE it (R27 same-file regression).** Adding a new test class right after `test_emits_plugin_json_and_dirs`, my Edit's `old_string` ended mid-test and the replacement dropped that test's last 3 assertions (`rules/tech.md`, `hooks`, `.mcp.json`). Caught by `git diff` self-review (the deletions showed as `-` lines) + a NameError when the orphaned tail referenced an out-of-scope `out`. Lesson: after an Edit that INSERTS between existing code, `git diff` the region and confirm you only ADDED — a large Edit near a boundary is where truncation hides (PIT83 class). (2026-07-23, run_62055da6, source:manual)

- [guideline] **DDD `_shared/` materialization at distribute-time = the RIGHT layer for "local single-source + portable self-contained" (2026-07-22, run_493d964a):** a DDD can now keep its shared code layer single-source at `<capabilities>/_shared/` (skills import via `parents[2]/_shared` sys.path injection) AND still distribute as self-contained packages, because `ddd_packager._materialize_shared` copies the flat `_shared/*.py` into each emitted skill's `scripts/` at package-emit time. The SAME `import client` line resolves from `_shared` locally and from the materialized sibling in the distributed package — zero skill-code divergence. **The lesson (C042-family, right layer): the "make each skill self-contained" responsibility belongs at the ONE packaging moment, NOT smeared across every consumer all year (the cp-distribution N×M model it replaces).** Gate-2 CLASS-A catch worth recording: my flat-copy passed all build-time tests but would SILENTLY ship a broken package if `_shared` had sub-packages (ModuleNotFoundError only at the foreign host, invisible at build) — the fresh-context adversarial reproduced it by execution; fix = detect sub-dirs → LOUD WARN + skip (enforced precondition, not silent assumption) + a test. "Build-green" ≠ "runs at the host" for a distributed artifact. (source:manual)

- [decision] **"别一个一个打补丁"的正解 = 先审计穷尽所有同类腐烂点,再提取共用 SSOT,一次改完全部消费者。** TECH.md Current Roster 是仓库/tier/stars 的真相源,却被抄成多份静态副本:上上轮修了 monitor.py 扫描名单从 TECH.md 派生,这轮发现 report.py 还有两份独立副本(REPO_INFO 死字典 14 仓库过期 40 天,mattpocock 写 tier2/88K 实为 tier1/165K;外加 `_compute_ddd_health` 第 3 个手写 `"| 1 |" in l` 计数器)。**方法论:XG 点破"不能一个一个补丁打"后,正确动作是先跑全目录审计 sub-agent 穷举所有硬编码副本(确认 skill 内共 3 处 + config.yaml 属独立子系统),再提取 `load_source_matrix()` 单一解析器,让 monitor(扫描)、report(Source Matrix tab)、report(DDD health)三个消费者全改吃它——而不是又给 report 单独写一份解析。** 教训:(1) **Gate-1 抓到第 3 个解析器**——原方案只删 REPO_INFO 漏了 _compute_ddd_health 手写计数,不一起改就还是补丁(删一份留一份);"根治"必须审计所有消费点。(2) 薄封装 + 现有测试当回归网 = 零风险改每日 job 核心函数;新测试只覆盖新增能力(tier3 包含、stars int 边界)。(3) `_parse_stars` strip `*`/逗号 + float 覆盖小数 K(25.5K→25500),异常→0 不抛。(4) report 吃共用解析器的空返回要 warn(否则成绕过 fail-loud 的后门)。(5) **注释是腐烂标志**:REPO_INFO 注释 `# from TECH.md — would be parsed in production` 明说该读却写死(R16b:注释是假设,代码是真相)。**backlog(移出本轮):topic_matrix 死数组不能从 hot_topics 派生——T-* 与 HT-* 两套无映射 ID,Primary Repos 又是短名,硬修=新硬编码换旧;需先补 TECH.md 全名+统一 T-* ID 集,单独立项。** E2E: report 现显示全量 39 仓库,mattpocock 88K→165K. (2026-07-19, run_b8306bd8, bugfix)

- [decision] **信号去噪的根治 = 一个统一准入门槛套住 ALL 生产路径,不是过滤某个已知噪音源。** GitHub community monitor 42% 信号是噪音(dependabot/自动release PR/eks-distro-pr-bot)。根因不是"eks 该过滤",而是 dashboard discovery 分支把"仓库陌生"当价值无条件放行。修法:`is_valuable_signal()` 分层门槛(非PR→topics非空→非bot→有活跃度)挂到全部 4 条生产路径(scan issues/disc、dashboard、hot_topics)。**Gate-1 BLOCK 最大价值:抓到我只挂 3 条、漏了 hot_topics 第4条——号称根治却留一条 bot 能进的路;"从根上"=枚举所有生产者,不是修最显眼那条。** 真实数据验出的具体教训:(1) bot 判定 `[bot]` 后缀不够,`eks-distro-pr-bot`(19%噪音)是普通 machine-user 名,需 `-bot` 结尾+标题 `Bump/Update...to latest` 模式三判;(2) PR 混入真实路径是 dashboard 的 IssueCommentEvent(url 是 `/pull/`),非 issues endpoint;(3) 门槛顺序 bot 必须排活跃度前,否则新 bot PR 从 is_new 漏;(4) `_is_new` 必须防空值,`fromisoformat("")` 会崩整个 monitor;(5) "发现新仓库"与"零噪音"本质冲突,移除 discovery 豁免后发现力 17→~1(有意取舍,发现由 github-trending 承担)。live 48→12 噪音清零。**遗留待办(LOW,非本轮):`fetch_recent_issues` 的 `since` 用 naive local time 却带 Z 后缀,时间窗偏本地 UTC 偏移——预存 bug,单独修。** (2026-07-19, run_ca49f00b, bugfix)

- [decision] **The right fix for "two hand-maintained lists drift" is to derive one from the other at runtime — but only after a skeptic confirms the two lists AREN'T meant to be independent.** The GitHub_Community engine had monitor.py scanning a hardcoded 19-repo list while TECH.md's Source Matrix (its own documented source of truth) tracked ~40 — 21 engagement targets never scanned. The user's initial framing ("merge the config.yaml signal feeds into the track list") was WRONG-FRAME: TECH.md:6 explicitly says "3 separate matrices — DO NOT MERGE THEM" (signal feeds = dep-watch, a different axis). Gate-0 skeptic caught it; real fix was `load_tracked_repos()` deriving monitor.py's scan list from the TECH.md table at runtime (19→26 repos). **Parser design lessons, all caught pre-code by the risk-probe + Gate-1:** (1) section-scope to the "### Current Roster" heading→next-heading — a naive `| N |` row-grep also matches the Hot Topics table (topic prose, not repos); (2) extract from **column-2 cell only**, not row-wide — descriptions contain embedded `[text](url)` links that leak with a row regex; (3) **URL-first** extraction survives truncated link TEXT (full name lives in the href); (4) fail-loud RuntimeError if <5 parsed (never silently scan an empty set). Gate-2 attacked the live parse (26 repos), separator/header-row skip, embedded-pipe column shift, stale-import regression — 0 real bugs. (2026-07-19, run_242c12b2, full)

- [decision] **Privacy leak in SwarmWS provisioning fixed at the right layer — template (new users) + migration-with-rm--cached (existing users) — with both adversarial gates earning their keep.** Product invariant: SwarmWS + the default `Projects/SwarmAI` sample DDD ship publicly; every user-created Project + the 5 personal `.context` files (MEMORY/USER/EVOLUTION/STEERING/TOOLS) are private and must never enter the public repo. Root fix in `swarm_workspace_manager.py`: extend `GITIGNORE_CONTENT` (new-user template) with `Projects/*` + `!Projects/SwarmAI/` + the 5 `.context` excludes; extend the existing `verify_integrity` migration to append those rules to pre-fix workspaces AND a new `_untrack_private_content()` that `git rm --cached` (index-only, disk-safe, marker-gated, fail-open, `git ls-files | filter-out SwarmAI` never a glob) untracks already-committed private content. **Gate-1 caught a CRITICAL I would have shipped: it CLAIMED `Projects/*` breaks the `!SwarmAI` re-inclusion — but a live /tmp git experiment REFUTED the skeptic (Projects/* globs child ENTRIES so the negation IS honored; the skeptic-recommended `Projects/` form actually IGNORES SwarmAI). Verifying the skeptic's claim against reality, not obeying it, was the win (C041: a sub-agent claim is an input to verify, not a conclusion).** **Gate-2 caught a REAL CRITICAL: `if entry not in content` is a SUBSTRING check — a privacy rule mentioned in a `.gitignore` COMMENT would false-satisfy it and the real rule would never append → private files stay committable. Fixed to line-level match against stripped non-comment lines; regression test mutation-verified RED-on-revert.** All fixes mutation-tested. (2026-07-19, run_6be16622, bugfix)

- [guideline] Verify-first is a TWO-WAY gate on a "fix all findings" sweep — a bug report can be WRONG-FRAME (fixing it is negative work) just as often as real. Of 8 audit findings, Gate-0 skeptics closed 3 as verified-non-bug BEFORE any code: A3 "double-init race" was impossible (sync no-await initialize() is atomic on a single event loop — a lock would be dead defense); A4 was already-fixed in a prior session (audit read the pre-flight, missed the runtime-path fix); A1-contract-gate was by-design (no versioned FE/BE contract exists, and coupling would reintroduce a prior regression). Blind-fixing all 8 would have added a useless lock + an over-engineered contract mechanism. The audit found the surface; the skeptic decided real-vs-non-bug. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [pitfall] Python except-clause ORDER is a data-loss trap when the narrow exception is a SUBCLASS of the broad one: my DB-integrity probe wrote `except sqlite3.DatabaseError: return False` (corrupt→re-seed) BEFORE `except sqlite3.OperationalError: return True` (locked→keep). But OperationalError IS a subclass of DatabaseError, so `except DatabaseError` catches "database is locked" first → the OperationalError branch is DEAD CODE → a valid-but-momentarily-locked data.db gets classified corrupt → unlink()+re-seed → USER DATA DESTROYED. The fix's own recovery logic was inverted by ordering. Rule: when catching a subclass and its parent, the SUBCLASS clause MUST come first; and any "detect-corrupt→destroy-and-recover" path needs a test that holds a real lock and asserts the data survives. Adversarial-caught HIGH; I verified via `issubclass(sqlite3.OperationalError, sqlite3.DatabaseError)`. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [pitfall] A "delete the corrupt DB and re-seed" recovery must also delete the -wal/-shm sidecars — a malformed DB from a crash-mid-write is EXACTLY when a hot -wal exists, and unlinking only the main file leaves a stale foreign WAL that the fresh seed copy replays into → re-corruption. Recover the whole DB unit (db + -wal + -shm), not just the .db file. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [guideline] Single-axis completeness analysis is a false "all clear": I reviewed "will startup block?" only along the BACKEND-readiness axis (daemon/health/onboarding) and declared "防呆做得很好" — while a deterministic every-launch boot crash sat on the FRONTEND-render axis I never walked (a root-mounted component calling useLayout() outside its provider). When claiming a GLOBAL property ("won't block"), enumerate the AXES first (backend-ready, frontend-render/context-boundaries, deploy-state) and state which were actually checked — an unwalked axis is not a passing axis. Worse: I had the clue (I listed CredentialBanner as a root-mounted useLayout consumer when drawing the startup diagram) and didn't connect "root node + useLayout = provider not upstream = throws". (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] Structural class-defense &gt; instance fix, and the loud/silent axis is load-bearing: to stop a recurring compile-time-invisible crash class, a Gate-0 skeptic flipped my design from "wrap every root component in ErrorBoundary fallback=null" (silent — MASKS the next violation, and fatal on the startup overlay: swallowing its crash strands isBackendReady=false = permanent boot hang) to "static source-scan test (LOUD, catches the class at its source) as primary + isolate ONLY passive banners as defense-in-depth, never the functional startup gate". A guard that hides violations is worse than the crash it prevents. (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] A guard/regression test needs its OWN mutation + adversarial pass — my class-guard had 3 real bugs my first draft missed, each caught by forcing it: (1) fallback={null} didn't isolate (ErrorBoundary guarded on truthiness → fell through to full-screen; R28 forced-exec test caught it), (2) the guard only forbade 1 of 6 shell-only hooks (meta-review coverage check found useTerminal/useTreeData/useSessionMeta unguarded — same crash class), (3) the list-drift self-check truncated its scan at the first inner `</ErrorBoundary>` (indexOf vs lastIndexOf) so it silently missed half the root block. The tell: a guard test that passes on your first try is under-tested — mutate it (inject the exact bug it guards) and adversarially probe its coverage. (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] The Gate-0 skeptic is a two-way filter, not just a pass-gate — run it on EVERY reported "latent/bug" including my own prior notes. Same session: it killed a proposed fix (WRONG-FRAME: merging the two 300s startup windows would regress run_e3dbc009's anti-false-kill) AND confirmed a real one (AppRoutes blank-screen dead-end). My own TECH.md "latent" note was the input that most needed challenging. (2026-07-18, run_834e952c + run_fdf5129c, source:manual)
- [guideline] react-query error state = no-exit dead-end pattern: a useQuery with `retry:N` and no `isError` branch renders null forever after retries exhaust (status=undefined, isLoading=false). Fix at the pure decision fn: add `isError` param, check it BEFORE the `status===undefined` clause but AFTER `isLoading` (so loading wins during retries, cached-status falls through, no flash). Default the param false for backward-compat. (2026-07-18, run_fdf5129c, source:manual)

- [guideline] **A fail-closed generation gate surfaces the real coverage gap you'd otherwise never confront** — regenerating the code-intel v3 domain layer via `finalize_v3` (which REQUIRES every route be either flow-classified or parked in `unclassified[]` with a substantive reason) forced backfilling 178→0 unaccounted routes to 100% accounted. The gate's whole value: you structurally cannot ship a "domain understanding" layer that silently ignores most of the codebase. The honest 4.8% classified_ratio stays REPORTED (never gated) so you're not rewarded for padding trivial routes into fake flows. (2026-07-18, run_89e28075, source:manual)

- [guideline] **Dog-fooding the coverage mechanism on real data found two wiring gaps unit tests couldn't** — (1) `compute_subsystem_coverage` credits a subsystem as "covered" ONLY via `steps[].file_path` / `entry_ref` route files, NOT `business_rules[].anchor` — so internal (non-HTTP) subsystems must anchor a flow to a real RELATED route (e.g. services-jobs→`/api/jobs/run`) to be counted. (2) The recall domain leg (`_recall_ddd` leg ③) was crowded OUT of the ddd bucket at the default `max_sections=3` because whole-doc BM25 normalizes to ~1.0 while a domain hit scores ≤0.85 → the DoD "recall 命中其独有业务词" failed in real usage; fixed with a reserved slot for the best domain/spec hit (re-sorted to keep score-desc). Both found only by running the SUT against the live `code-intel.json`, not fixtures (O009). (2026-07-18, run_89e28075, source:manual)

- executescript() in sqlite3 IMPLICITLY COMMITS any open transaction before running — a BEGIN+executescript+commit does NOT make a multi-statement migration atomic (verified: rows survive a rollback). - [pitfall] **A self-authored anti-hallucination gate had its OWN escape hatch (CLASS A, caught by Gate-2)** — I built `check_mermaid_node_anchoring` (ai_ready_helpers.py) specifically to close the mermaid-node hole, and its docstring literally asserted "repo_root is NOT an escape hatch" — but Gate-2 proved it WAS: `(_root / tok).is_file()` accepts an ABSOLUTE token `/tmp/x.py` (pathlib discards the left operand when rhs is absolute) and `../` traversal, so the gate meant to block hallucinated nodes would accept ANY real `.py`/`.ts` file anywhere on the machine. The felt-completeness of a self-authored security check is indistinguishable from actual completeness — the exact authorship trap, surfacing in the very gate designed to ENFORCE anti-hallucination. Fix: `resolve()` + `relative_to(repo_root)` containment (the same discipline `_safe_file_read` ALREADY implements in the same file — I re-derived instead of reusing it). Mutation-verified (revert containment → 2 escape tests RED). **Durable tell: a path-based disk check with `is_file()` alone is NEVER containment — always `resolve()`+`relative_to()`, and grep the same file for an existing safe-path helper to reuse before writing a new one.** (2026-07-16, run_3026ef31)

- [pitfall] **Referential-integrity ("does the ref resolve?") is necessary but NOT sufficient — dog-food found a semantic-mismatch bug the gate structurally could not** — `flow:ws-file-write` declared `entry_ref` = the POST `create_file` route, but its mermaid AND all its steps described the PUT `put_workspace_file` path (step line-ranges land inside the PUT handler). `check_domain_referential_integrity` PASSED because the POST route id is a *valid* id — the gate only checks `entry_ref` resolves to *some* route, never that the entry_ref's method/path/file AGREES with the flow's own mermaid/steps. A human reading the spec would be pointed at the wrong entry point. Fixed by hand (correct PUT route id + spec regenerated). **Lesson: a "does X resolve?" gate never catches "does X resolve to the RIGHT thing?" — candidate future gate: assert a flow's `entry_ref` file_path is consistent with its steps' `file_path`.** Dog-fooding on real SwarmAI data surfaced this; fixtures never would. (2026-07-16, run_3026ef31)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- A "fix" for a timeout false-fatal can RE-INTRODUCE the same false-fatal one layer down — and only the Gate-2 meta-review caught it. The new-user first-launch false-fatal (fixed 60s startup cap killing a slow-but-alive daemon) was correctly re-based on launchd process-liveness (`launchctl pid present` = keep waiting), but the fail-fast leg (N consecutive no-pid checks → give up) would kill a daemon still inside the launchd **bootstrap→spawn window** — where the service is registered but `pid=` isn't populated yet on a disk-pressured 500MB cold start. That is the EXACT false-fatal being fixed, just at 16s instead of 60s. Fix: gate fail-fast on `ever_alive` — a never-yet-seen-alive process waits to the absolute ceiling (O030 backstop); only was-alive-then-gone (real crash/respawn-loop) fails fast. Lesson: when replacing a guillotine timeout with an adaptive one, enumerate EVERY "give up" leg and ask "does this fire during the exact slow-but-healthy scenario I'm fixing?" — the fail-fast optimization is where the regression hides. (2026-07-15, run_e3dbc009)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- The diagnose-before-build gate (THINK risk-probe) paid for the whole run by FALSIFYING my own EVALUATE root-cause sub-claim BEFORE any code. I framed the bug as "the gate discards the backend's `{status:initializing}` HTTP signal" — but `grep` of ~15k daemon-log lines showed `initializing` is served 0 times: uvicorn only binds the port AFTER lifespan startup completes (`_startup_complete=True` at main.py:1049, before the yield), so during the slow window the client gets connection-refused, never `initializing`. An HTTP-status-based fix would have been a NO-OP. The real alive/dead signal is the OS process (launchctl pid), not HTTP. Lesson: for any "the code ignores signal X" bug, VERIFY signal X is actually emitted at runtime (grep real logs) before building the consumer for it — a runtime-unreachable branch makes the fix theater. (2026-07-15, run_e3dbc009)

- A prune/migration that unlinks by PATH is a data-loss bug waiting to fire; content-gate it. Gate-2 reproduced live: `_prune_legacy_scaffold` deleted a human-authored `gates/README.md` because it matched a legacy PATH — no content check. Fix: unlink only if the file still carries the shipped-stub markers (a human edit diverges → kept). Same class: a `.gitkeep`-only dir must count as empty (else migrate leaves a half-state that violates the very invariant it enforces). Rule: destructive migration deletes by CONTENT-SIGNATURE, never by path alone; and "empty enough to remove" must exclude dotfile markers. (2026-07-12, run_693df058)
- DDD-native skill decoupling proved shippable in one goal run by keeping the MOAT and dropping the MACHINE ROOM: s_ddd-pipeline/s_ddd-pollinate rewrite s_autonomous-pipeline/s_pollinate to file-based `.artifacts/` state (no data.db/artifact_cli) while retaining Gate-2 adversarial-before-commit + the 养成 ladder. The test asserts BOTH halves — decoupled (uses run.json, states "no data.db") AND moat-intact (adversarial + ladder + BLOCKING) — so a future "port" that keeps the coupling OR drops the moat goes RED. Portability is "carries the brain + method, never the machine room." (2026-07-12, run_693df058)
- SwarmWS-native DDD ≠ AIM-export form: the recurring over-build (agents/, agent-sops/, per-section READMEs) came from conflating the §555 EXPORT layout with the §3.6 CANONICAL structure. When a spec has both a "canonical" and a "physical realization" section, provision the canonical one — the physical members are an export concern. (2026-07-12, run_693df058)

Added mattpocock/skills to GitHub_Community tracking. (2026-07-12)

- [pitfall] **Authorship-trap recurred through a self-authored test — Gate-2 caught my INCOMPLETE fix (run_9290f4ff, full)** — Built `scripts/sync_discussions.py` (number-keyed docs/discussions mirror sync). During BUILD I hit a header-duplication bug on live data, "fixed" `extract_editorial_header` with an xref-regex guard, wrote a test (`> **In one line**` body, NO `#N`), watched it pass. **The fix was incomplete AND my test proved nothing:** the regex's `#\d+\b` alternative still matched a body-leading `> Related: #99` blockquote → duplicated on every UPDATE. All 22 self-authored tests were green; the fresh-context Gate-2 sub-agent found it in the "editorial header" attack vector because IT used a `#N` body (the case my test omitted — the exact blind spot of the author). Kernel: **"I wrote a test for it" ≡ "the behavior is guarded" is as false as "I wrote it" ≡ "it works" — a self-authored test covers what I ASSUMED, not what I missed.** The real fix was positional (header is glued to the marker; first blank line ends it), not a content regex — a content regex on `#N` can't distinguish an editorial cross-ref from a body cross-ref. **What held:** the mandatory Gate-2 spawn + then mutation-verifying the new regression test RED-on-revert (reintroduce the buggy loop → `test_body_leading_xref_blockquote_not_duplicated_on_update` goes RED). The tell to reuse: after fixing a parsing bug, ask "does my test include the case that looks IDENTICAL to the thing I'm trying to exclude?" — if not, it's theater. (2026-07-10, run_9290f4ff, source:pipeline)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:pipeline -->
- [decision] **Live-probe-before-scope saved a 70-file blast radius (run_9290f4ff, full)** — PLAN assumed the docs/discussions mirror was mostly frontmatter-style, so "edit-aware sync" would be low-blast. The BUILD `--check` against the REAL tree falsified that: 35/72 files are legacy no-frontmatter format + bodies are editorialized (not verbatim live copies), so a naive "regenerate from live" flagged 70/72 as drift. Running `--check` on real data at BUILD (not trusting the plan's assumption) exposed it before any `--write` → narrowed UPDATE to "live edited-date > mirror `updated:` AND has frontmatter", added LEGACY_SKIP (never auto-reformat), bounding blast radius to the actual drift class (missing files + stale index + genuine edits). Idempotency pivot also from a live probe: `updated:` MUST derive from stable `lastEditedAt||createdAt`, never `today()`, or every run drifts. LESSON: for a sync/migration tool, run the read-only mode against production data DURING build — the plan's assumption about the corpus shape is a hypothesis, and the corpus is the cheapest place to falsify it. (2026-07-10, run_9290f4ff, source:pipeline)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:pipeline -->
- [decision] **Recall "synonym-miss gap" — research FALSIFIED the premise, recommend do-nothing (run_5d6a7e83, research)** — Came in believing headroom's adaptive-alpha hybrid pointed at a real hole (`empty_with_keywords` counted but not acted on since the vector leg was removed 2026-06-28). M3 skeptic mined the live daemon logs: of **23 real "matched nothing" events, ALL 23 are short CJK conversational follow-ups** ("有啥结论", "发版吧", "看结果"), **0 English synonym-misses**. The imagined failure ("errors"≠"failed") has ZERO recorded instances. Verdicts: recall is keyword-only + NO FTS5 porter stemming (VERIFIED — all 3 FTS5 tables lack `tokenize=porter`) BUT Porter is English-only → helps 0/23 recorded (all-Chinese) misses; the miss is ALREADY mitigated by a live agentic re-search nudge (`session_router.py:508-513`, reaches the model). **Rejected all 4 build options** (do-nothing ✅ / stemming ✗ orthogonal / synonym-table ✗ no evidence / re-add-vector ✗ reverses a sound tested decision = C042 headroom-envy). Real miss mode is short-CJK-low-signal-query, NOT synonymy — if ever revisited, the lever is CJK keyword handling in `_extract_query_keywords`, never stemming or vectors. **META: this is the 2nd headroom-derived idea this session that evidence killed (1st: comment-drift). - [decision] **Comment-drift detector hook — SHELVED v1 at Gate 1, correct outcome (run_796f8227, bugfix)** — Two gates killed a net-negative feature with ZERO code shipped: EVALUATE M3 skeptic reframed adjacency→content-linkage; Gate 1 plan-skeptic then proved even content-linkage is net-negative. **The hard constraint for any future attempt:** the feature's value IS precision (an annoying reminder is worse than none), but deterministic token-overlap ("a surviving comment shares a token with changed code") false-positives on the MODAL case — any comment naming a common identifier (config/data/result/client) fires on nearly every same-name edit → ~8 real historical cases vs hundreds of spurious reminders = inverted SNR = F004 fatigue. Hand-rolled triple-quote/comment parsing (no AST) is also defeated by ordinary Python (f/r-strings, `"""` in string literals, single-line docstrings, inline comments, reindent). **The ONLY low-FP design is AST symbol-level** (fire only when a comment references a symbol whose definition/signature changed) — a larger scope that must be re-EVALUATEd on its own, never smuggled into a bugfix. LESSON (C042/C044 family): the idea came from a headroom research report — chasing a capability because a reference repo has it, not because I feel the pain, is the "修表面 / build-because-they-have-it" kernel; quantification + Gate 1 both said stop, and stopping was the win. (2026-07-10, run_796f8227, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A safety claim verified on ONE code path is NOT verified — Gate-2 caught a 2-HIGH data-corruption vector Gate-0+Gate-1 both blessed as safe (run_748f14a7).** Widening `_ENTRY_RE` to parse emoji-prose Open-Threads bullets. Gate-0 (skeptic) correctly REFUTED my "must use a retire-scoped matcher" premise by proving Open Threads is section-evergreen-guarded — so a global widen is "safe for decay." Gate-1 verified that against `assess_decay`+`reclaim`. BOTH gates checked the SAME single path (decay) and both passed. Gate-2 (fresh adversary) found the premise was only 1/3 true: `_ENTRY_RE` also feeds `inject_entry_metadata` (would STAMP `<!-- ref -->` onto 8 curated OT bullets — verified live on real MEMORY.md, delta +8) and the `ddd_orchestrator` IMPROVEMENT path (reclaim strips with NO evergreen set). The section-guard covers decay ONLY; inject+reclaim were unguarded. **The reusable lesson: when a change touches a SHARED primitive (a regex, a constant, a parser used at N sites), "safe" must be proven at EVERY consumer, not the one the design happened to examine — grep ALL callers and check each, because the gate that blesses the first path gives false confidence about the rest.** The convergence fix vindicated the retire-SCOPED matcher Gate-1 had called "redundant": make prose-matching OPT-IN so autonomous write-paths are safe BY CONSTRUCTION (can't see prose), not by remembering to pass evergreen everywhere. Safe-by-construction > guard-you-must-remember. (2026-07-03, run_748f14a7)

- Stale test-mock drift is a silent eval-decay class: a test that patch()es a symbol the code NO LONGER calls does not fail loud — the real unmocked call runs, gets caught by a broad except, and returns status=error, so the test is ALWAYS-RED but looks like a normal failure. Tell: an assert error==passed on an LLM-judge test = the mock target drifted off the code path. Fix = repoint to the symbol the code actually imports (call-time from X import Y → patch the SOURCE module attribute). (2026-07-02, run_2453b416, auto-cultivated)

- [decision] **AutoSDE 3-pattern port — one-shot code-quality patterns into s_autonomous-pipeline** — Ported 3 patterns from AutoSDE (Amazon internal AI code-review) across runs run_7583af5f + run_6589c62b, honestly graded by ENFORCEMENT strength: ① **confidence gate as a CODE CONSTANT** (`CONFIDENCE_GATE_THRESHOLD=7` + `_blocked_findings()` in pipeline_validator.py, called at BOTH publish + completion sites, unifying 2 duplicated HIGH-only filters) — the only "true gate" of the three (P7: prose is bypassable, a hard constant in the validator holds); ② **cross-cycle finding re-judgment** (goal_cycle re-judges prior-cycle findings APPLICABLE/RESOLVED vs new diff — targets "fixed-then-regressed") — prose-layer; ③ **3 recurring failure-classes fossilized as ASSERTABLE RP rows** — RP47 test-theater (grep test for `patch(<symbol-under-change>)` or a local re-derivation of a prod formula; verdict = mutation-prove revert→RED), RP48 stale/false-comment (a diff-changed token appears in an UNCHANGED comment line), RP49 security-redaction-as-denylist (invert to allowlist; a denylist fails-open on every field added later) — prose-layer but fed to a zero-context adversarial reviewer, so closer to a gate than self-review. Honest verdict: only ① is bypass-proof; ②③ are only as strong as the reviewer that reads them (see open thread: gate skeptics don't inherit failure history). (2026-07-01, run_6589c62b)

- prewarm sessions ran the full ~11-hook post-session chain (~6.5s) for nothing — 24h prod logs: 32 prewarm spawned / 0 adopted / 28 orphan-reaped, each paying 6.5s of auto-commit/evolution/context-health. Root cause was a MISSING GUARD at the single chokepoint (LifecycleManager.enqueue_hooks): every reap/idle/TTL/eviction path funnels through it, none checked the prewarm prefix. Fix = 1 guard on session_id.startswith(PREWARM_SESSION_PREFIX) + early return; AC3 (post-adoption resume) came FREE because adopt_prewarmed_unit re-keys unit.session_id to a real id and _build_hook_context reads it live. Gate-1 skeptic's leak-check was the load-bearing step: confirmed all 11 hooks touch only workspace/knowledge (never subprocess/session-state), so skipping is safe — without that check, skipping hooks could have leaked resources. Chose id-prefix guard over a new is_prewarm flag (DDD: new mutable state on session_unit/router god-files = top regression source). (2026-06-30, run_f10075d3)
- OT01 diagnosis sharpened: "is the pipeline stuck?" was a FRONTEND render freeze, not a backend hang. Backend truth (run-get) showed all stages completed while the frontend froze on an 8-min-old snapshot at a pytest tool-call that had been auto-backgrounded. The discriminator that settled it in seconds: `ps` (the bg pytest was already gone) + run-get (stages all completed) — observe-the-live-system beat guessing. When a long pytest gets auto-backgrounded, the frontend spinner can desync from backend streaming = OT01 class; refresh the tab, don't re-run. (2026-06-30, run_f10075d3)

- A score-drift GATE must compare against a DIFFERENT-code_digest baseline, NOT the raw previous report. Comparing latest vs a same-code RE-RUN measures LLM-judge noise, not regression — proven on real data: 100.0→91.7 on identical digest 526db7a385 (an 8.3pt "drop" that is pure noise). The gate walks reports newest-first by MTIME (NOT filename — _load_history's filename sort disagrees with the gate's mtime sort when filename formats coexist) and picks the first different-digest report as baseline; fail-OPEN when no different-code baseline exists. "Score changed" ≠ "my change regressed score." (2026-06-30, run_95d9acbc)

- [decision] **Stop → COLD subprocess recycle is INTENTIONAL — do NOT "keep it warm" (KDD, verified 2026-06-30)** — The perceived regression "Stop 后再 resume 很慢" (~14-16s cold-spawn on the next message) is BOUNDED LATENCY, not breakage/data-loss, and it must be left alone. Mechanism is PIT01 (recorded): after ANY soft interrupt the Claude CLI subprocess is in corrupt turn-state; reusing it returns an instant empty `error_during_execution` (zombie) the detector kills + respawns anyway — so eager recycle (`interrupt_recycle` session_unit ~2789 / `flush_recycle` ~2599 → `_crash_to_cold_async` → COLD) just FRONT-LOADS a kill that would happen lazily otherwise. **The 15s is paid EITHER WAY**: skipping the recycle hits the zombie path next message = same ~15s PLUS a failed round-trip + 5s retry backoff, AND resurrects the exact bug PIT01 fixed. "Conditionally keep warm" fights an SDK behavior and is high-regression-risk in the session_unit/session_router god-files (source of nearly every past lifecycle regression). **IF the 15s ever must be addressed:** (1) ZERO-RISK — a frontend "正在恢复会话…" indicator during cold-spawn (display-only, never touches lifecycle); (2) REAL fix (higher risk, only if justified) — reuse the EXISTING prewarm machinery (`prewarm_channel_session` + `adopt_prewarmed_unit`, session_router ~887/963, currently Slack-only) to background-spawn a `--resume` replacement during user think-time, needing a `--resume` prewarm variant + compute_max_tabs budget gate + tab-alive gate + a test that ENTERS each path. Do NOT conditionally-keep-warm without first PROVING the zombie isn't universal. Verified this session: spec `.kiro/specs/subprocess-recycle-on-interrupt/requirements.md` exists; all five symbols confirmed present at the cited locations. (Source: Kiro parallel-session KDD, verified against live code; mechanism = PIT01/PIT48, this entry adds the decision boundary.) (2026-06-30, source:manual)

- A fail-closed GUARD on a primitive can make the COMMON path worse than the bug it fixes — if the guard fires on the normal usage pattern. run_3caef1d3: `_find_active_run` picked the project-wide newest active run with no owner scoping → `publish --stage` without `--run-id` contaminated a sibling run. I added a fail-closed guard (2+ active + no --run-id → exit 3). Gate-2 found: ALL 8 stage docs publish WITHOUT --run-id, so in a multi-run workspace (the bug's own precondition — "14 active runs") EVERY publish would now hit the guard and HALT the pipeline. The guard alone converted silent contamination into a loud stall. The fix needed BOTH halves: the guard (backstop) AND threading --run-id through the 8 stage docs (remove the trigger). Lesson: when you guard a primitive against a bad input, grep how callers ACTUALLY invoke it — if the guard fires on the documented/common call shape, you've shipped a worse regression. (2026-06-29, run_3caef1d3)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none | source:pipeline -->
- A fail-closed error must be emitted on the SAME stream the consumer's error-handler reads. run_3caef1d3: my ambiguity error went to STDOUT, but the documented orchestrator guard is `OUT=$(publish … 2>/tmp/pub.err) || { cat /tmp/pub.err; }` — it reads STDERR on failure and feeds STDOUT to `json.load(...)['artifact_id']`. So the actionable error was invisible AND would crash the guard's json.load. Gate-2 caught it by reading the consumer contract, not just the producer. Fix: match the sibling validation_failed path (stderr). Tell: a new error path must be routed to wherever the EXISTING error paths go — grep how failures are already surfaced before inventing a new channel. (2026-06-29, run_3caef1d3)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none | source:pipeline -->
- A regression-guard test that builds its OWN throwaway probe instead of asserting on the REAL fixture/SUT is VACUOUS test-theater — it proves a general framework fact, not that THIS code is fixed. run_b9ecb07a: a guard for "the `client` fixture must not run lifespan" built a `probe_app` with a tripwire lifespan and a bare `TestClient(probe_app)` — it stayed GREEN when the real fixture was reverted to the buggy `with`-form (mutation-tested), so it guarded nothing. The "mutation-proven" claim in the commit was false (CLASS A authorship trap, test layer). Gate-2 adversarial caught it. Fix: assert against the REAL thing — `monkeypatch.setattr(main.app.router, "lifespan_context", tripwire)` + invoke the actual fixture via `request.getfixturevalue("client")`; now mutation-proven RED on revert. Tell: if your guard imports/constructs a stand-in of the SUT instead of the SUT, it's theater. (2026-06-29, run_b9ecb07a)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none | source:pipeline -->
- `with TestClient(app)` runs the app's FULL lifespan (startup+shutdown); a bare `TestClient(app)` (no context manager) does NOT — Starlette only enters lifespan on `__enter__` (verified Starlette 0.50.0). A unit test that only hits a light endpoint (/health) and forces `_startup_complete=True` does NOT need lifespan; using the `with`-form makes every such test pay the real DB-init+migrations+workspace-ensure cost (main.py:693+). run_b9ecb07a: two fixtures (test_daemon_sidecar_fixes + _robustness) did this → an R7 pytest exceeded the 120s foreground timeout → Bash auto-backgrounded → empty foreground → UI tool-spinner stranded at "11m34s". Same-class scan (Gate-2) found the sibling file; fixing only one leaves the incident live. (2026-06-29, run_b9ecb07a)

- [guideline] **Gate-2 adversarial earned its keep on a trivial-profile guard: caught a HIGH false-kill rooted in an unverified runtime-state assumption (run_07fd1d8f, bash_syntax_guard).** I built a PreToolUse Bash guard that parse-checks each command with `<shell> -n` before it runs (DENY the syntactically-incomplete/hang set, fail-open otherwise) — and hardcoded `/bin/bash`, never verifying the Bash tool actually runs `zsh`. The whole EVALUATE understanding rested on that unchecked assumption (R16b / CLASS-B). The fresh-context adversarial sub-agent found it: `/bin/bash -n` rejects valid zsh syntax (`foo() { echo hi }`) → the guard would false-kill legitimate commands (the cardinal sin for a hang-prevention guard). Two transferable lessons: (1) **A guard that validates commands must validate with the SAME interpreter that executes them** — verify the exec shell, never assume. (2) **R16b applies to sub-agent claims too**: the adversary's own example (`for i in 1 2 3 { }` "is zsh-valid") was WRONG — I ran `printf|zsh -n` (exit 1, invalid) before trusting it and used the actually-valid `for i (1 2 3) { }`. Don't trust a sub-agent's runtime-behavior claim any more than your own; verify both. Honest residual: `-n` only catches PARSE-incomplete hangs; a syntactically-valid command that blocks on stdin at runtime (bare `cat`/`read`/`grep`) parses fine and is approved — a different hang class, not claimed covered. (2026-06-29, run_07fd1d8f, manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **Session-briefing assembly cut 1150ms→~280ms warm (-75%) by mtime-pre-filtering an unbounded scan (run_885eb466).** Root cause: `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` (proactive_intelligence.py:752) `json.loads`'d ALL 343 run.json (SwarmAI alone 327, 1.5MB) every assembly, then filtered by `updated_at` — but the function's semantics only ever wanted runs <24h old. Fix: a cheap `st_mtime` gate before the read, skipping files untouched >48h (2× max_age buffer). Three transferable lessons: (1) **Unbounded scan feeding bounded output (PIT90 sibling)** — reading 343 files to pick `max_items=3` directives; push the existing window-filter DOWN to a cheap stat layer instead of read-then-filter. (2) **Profile perf in warm AND cold** — the first profiling pass blamed `_get_health_highlights` (1183ms) + DDD (297ms), but those were one-time import tax (jobs/eval/ddd heavy modules); warm they're <40ms. Only `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` was a true resident cost. Not splitting warm/cold = fixing the wrong target. (3) **A prior "optimized" claim can miss the real bottleneck** — commit 56209561 claimed briefing→4ms but it stayed 1.3s; it had cut a different section, the real hot path was never touched. The long-suspected Recurrence Radar (reads 407K IMPROVEMENT.md) was measured at 6ms — innocent. (2026-06-29, run_885eb466, manual)

- [guideline] **Adding a streaming-render timer safely = 3 traps, all solved by copying useStreamingActivity's discipline (run_02e658d0).** A per-second timer in the chat render path is near the OT01 reconcile / cross-tab zones. The 3 traps: (1) anchor on the per-invocation id (toolUseId), NOT mount-time — React reuses keyed-list instances, a mount anchor shows the prior tool's time; (2) gate the setInterval on the active/pending flag — tabs are keep-mounted, an ungated interval leaves N idle timers on every completed card across every tab (the F4 hazard); (3) always clear on unmount AND on the gate flipping false. Keeping it client-side (useRef/useState only, no stored field, no MessageStore write, no SSE coupling, no module-level state) means it scopes to its TabView instance by construction and never touches the reconcile/isolation fragile zones. (2026-06-28, run_02e658d0, manual)

- [guideline] **A coefficient and its inverse MUST derive from ONE shared constant.** 2 hardcoded `3/4` inverse-truncation sites (the inverse of the old `4/3` Latin coefficient) had silently drifted — they only break when the forward coefficient is recalibrated. - [guideline] **Session-briefing perf: profile-before-fix beat the stated bottleneck** — build_session_briefing spiked to 8.4s. The user-named heavy sections were FALSIFIED by measurement: Recurrence Radar reading the 390K IMPROVEMENT.md = 5ms; _get_paused_pipeline_highlights = the PIT01-protected auto-resume engine. The REAL cost was a bare `EvalService().growth_report(7)` buried 3 calls deep in `_get_health_highlights`: it re-parsed 84 EvalHistory files every briefing (516ms) + spawned a git-log subprocess that contended on the repo lock under N-tab concurrency (the 8.4s spike). Fix: `get_eval_service()` singleton swap (kills 516ms) + a process-level TTL cache on the git result. Net: `_get_health_highlights` 826ms→4ms; warm briefing ~2.4s/8.4s → ~500ms. Lesson: profile the actual call tree before accepting any stated bottleneck (O008). (2026-06-28, run_b0ca1196)
