- [guideline] **Cheapest perf win = use the singleton that already exists** — a load-once cached singleton (`get_eval_service()`) was being bypassed by a bare `EvalService()` at a hot call site, paying the full 516ms constructor reload every call. The fix was a one-token swap, zero new machinery. When chasing per-call cost, grep for bare `Constructor()` calls of any class that ships a `get_*_singleton()` accessor. (2026-06-28, run_b0ca1196)

- [guideline] **A perf fix can also fix a stale test** — threading `workspace_root=Path(working_directory)` into `growth_report` (instead of letting the singleton resolve its global workspace) scoped the git gather to the briefing's workspace AND fixed a PRE-EXISTING test-isolation leak: 3 governance tests in test_health_alerting were failing on HEAD before this change because growth_report leaked the real workspace's 🧬 lines into a tmp-dir test. Verify-before-judge (R29) confirmed the failures pre-dated the change. (2026-06-28, run_b0ca1196)

- **Briefing cognition-admission: cut 8 feed sections from the system-prompt briefing, gated by 3 adversarial passes (run_a16d61ad).** The session briefing is COGNITION real-estate (it goes straight into the system prompt, competing with 11 context files for attention — F004), not a dashboard. Drove "什么进 briefing" by a KEEP/CUT/RECALL admission test, finalized the whole READ line in one goal run (5 commits, 8/8 DoD, 210 tests green, eval 100%). Four reusable lessons: (1) **Gate-0 (understanding) is worth a full skeptic pass even when you "know" the system** — it REFRAMED work-stream B: `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` LOOKS like a display read but is the auto-resume ENGINE (mutates resume_attempts under fcntl lock, orphan→paused, abandoned); my planned "cap to newest 10 by mtime" would have silently DROPPED a paused run with a pending resume directive. A "cheap perf cap" on a function with side effects is a data-loss bug. KILLED. (2) **Gate-1 (plan-attack) caught an assumed-wire-that-isn't-there**: work-stream C (wire CodeIntel into the live recall leg) assumed `_recall_for_query`/`unit` carry a bound project — they don't (no session→project binding in the recall path). Threading it through would be a signature ripple on the hottest path for a trigger signal that isn't even available (the C042 "build a mechanism" trap). SPLIT: the CUT half (remove codebase-map from briefing) fully delivers the PUSH→PULL intent; the WIRE half deferred to a run that first builds the binding infra. (3) **Live-data falsification before cutting beats intuition**: DDD-trust READ as 1273 `trust:high` : 2 `trust:low` (both empty-doc placeholders) → zero discrimination → the section was occupying cognition without informing. Measured, not guessed. (4) **Cross-path adversarial on the TOTAL changeset caught the field-init question that per-commit review couldn't**: a parallel layer added `_recall_keyword_misses` init in `SessionUnit.__init__` + reset in `_cleanup_internal` — the final adversary verified ALL unit-construction paths (create/resume/COLD-respawn) run __init__ and the getattr-default backstops it, so the recall#5 miss-cap can't AttributeError. Per-commit review sees one file; the cross-path pass sees the seam. (2026-06-28, run_a16d61ad, manual)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- **goal pipeline drove a 2-Run subsystem removal to 10/10 DoD in one run (run_e9b8507e, pure-filesystem recall impl).** "Run1+Run2 一起做完别停" → goal profile, 10 DoD, BUILD+TEST looped to green. Two clean commits (aaed814a read-line + 6540970e vector-removal), eval 56/56 100%, 2 adversarial passes both SHIP. Three reusable lessons: (1) **Removing a subsystem (vector) safely = reader AND writer stopped in the SAME run + golden cases retired in the same run** — stopping readers but leaving the embed writer would burn Bedrock writing vectors nobody reads (design §5.5 caught this; the adversary verified all 3 writers null-guard embed_fn=None); leaving the vector golden cases would leave the eval gate RED. (2) **A by-function script deletion swallows module-level constants that sit BETWEEN the deleted functions** — my `_onehot`/`_build_db` deletion took out `_ENTRY_MARKER` (a const between two probes) and over-removed a still-used `patch` import; the survivor-path run (`codeintel_live`/`resume_fill`/`entry_recall` positive+negative) caught both as NameError before commit. Always run the SURVIVING paths after a bulk delete, not just "it imports". (3) **When a spec changes (truncate→never-truncate, vector→keyword), the probes/tests that encoded the OLD spec must be rewritten to the NEW contract IN THE SAME run, with teeth re-verified** — `assembly_floor` INV2/INV3 (truncation order) were deleted and replaced with INV1 byte-intact + INV2 no-marker; the negative path still bites (broken-enforcer → RED). A retired-but-not-rewritten probe is either a false-green or a permanent RED. (2026-06-28, run_e9b8507e, manual)

- **Autonomous-pipeline 近期(06-24~06-28)的提速来自"少跑偏=少跑",不是单轮变快 — 已 git 核验。** 近4天 34 个 pipeline commit,grep `perf/latency/timeout/cache/token` = **0 命中**:pipeline 自身没有任何降低单轮 LLM 延迟的改动。真正压低"一个 coding task 总墙钟"的是砍掉白做的 turn,机制有四条且都核验存在:(1) **Understanding Gate + 自苏格拉底歧义扫描** (`2479fed2`+`5389c2ca`,EVALUATE→THINK 边界) → 不再 BUILD 到一半发现方向错回炉(回炉一次=几十 turn 蒸发);(2) **REPRO gate + diagnostic-challenge** (`58bb0287`) → bug 任务先复现再动手,少一轮改错地方;(3) **profile-selection 修复 goal 过度选中** (`53dd788c`,改 INSTRUCTIONS.md+evaluate.md) → 本该 trivial/bugfix 的任务不再被塞进最重的 goal 档跑多个 cycle,**直接砍总耗时**;(4) **in-band L2 escalation via AskUserQuestion** (`8952cbe7`) → 卡住时当场问,不再 auto-resume 三次后干等(补 run_6c482b10 的 DELIVERY gap)。**最大混淆因素(必须扣除):06-26 `cabf2cc0` 单分支合并**(确认 2 parents)— 在那之前仓库分裂成两条未合并并行分支,每次 build 只带一半修复、fix 在两次部署间 flip-flop;合并后 build 第一次带齐 ~200+ commits 全部累积修复。所以"突然顺了"里有相当一部分是"终于跑在统一带齐修复的 build 上",不是某个 perf commit。**认知要点:在 coding pipeline 语境里,"顺"(少跑偏)和"快"(总墙钟)不是两件事 — 耗时≈stage数×每stage turn数,砍轮次就是真提速,只是省在轮次数量而非单轮速度。把 pipeline 改动一律降级成"只让流程顺"会低估真实提速。** 边界:本结论从 commit 内容核验,未取 run 的实测 stage 耗时;要坐实须比两个同类 run 的 `REPORT.md` 的 stage总数/总turn/abandon次数(不是单 stage 秒数)。(2026-06-28, manual)

- **3 cascading gates each caught a DIFFERENT defect-class on a docs-only design (run_19fe8649, Context/Recall pure-filesystem 设计).** This was the strongest single-run evidence yet for "model proposes, OS disposes." In ONE session I produced THREE confidently-wrong root-cause framings of a cold-start slowness — (a) "Titan embed 9.6s aborts system-prompt assembly" (REFUTED: that was a *theoretical timeout ceiling* I computed, never observed; real warm embed = 0.4s), (b) "load_all = 11s" (REFUTED: isolated load_all = 3s), (c) the whole "vector is the bottleneck" causal story — all falsified only when I finally ran empirical probes (O008) instead of inferring from constants (R16b violated 3×). The design that emerged was then gated: **Gate-0** (framing skeptic) caught that recall has TWO legs (injection-leg vector is dead code; runtime-leg vector is live) — my framing had conflated them; **Gate-1** (design skeptic) BLOCKED with 3 code-verified defects (DDD-project-binding AC was architecturally impossible — production `_recall_for_query` never calls `recall_all`; deletion list missed the 3rd Titan reader `context_recall.recall_context` default `allow_embed=True`; §7 mis-classified `GS_RCHAIN_RECALL_BUDGET` as vector-independent when its probe asserts `embed_calls>0`); **Gate-2** (adversarial) caught a HIGH that would break Run 1 (deleting the `memory_embeddings` *parameter* KeyErrors `test_memory_wiring.py:32`, contradicting the "zero eval risk" claim) + 2 MED (3 vector-bound cases share fate; Run-2 "all green" DoD is unachievable for vector-premise cases → needs explicit retire-vs-rewrite rule). The durable lesson: **felt-certainty in a root-cause narrative is indistinguishable from a correct one — the empirical probe and the adversarial gate are the ONLY things that separated them. My model-layer error rate did not drop (3 wrong framings in one session); CONTAINMENT held (every design defect caught pre-ship).** Do not read a clean gated delivery as "I diagnosed well" — I diagnosed badly and the gates saved it. (2026-06-28, run_19fe8649, manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- **Grill-the-spec caught a self-contradicting requirement before it became a design (run_19fe8649).** XG proposed "recall 范围:Knowledge 只看最近1周 files." Grilling it against `find -mtime -7` data exposed the fatal contradiction: recall's PURPOSE is to surface what's NOT in the full-load context (= archived/old long-term memory), but a 1-week mtime window cuts EXACTLY that — Archives are old by definition, so the window would let recall see only what it least needs (new files already in DailyActivity). The right boundary for "怕 grep 太散" is BM25 top-N relevance (full scan, narrow return), NOT a time window. XG withdrew the constraint. Lesson: when a scoping rule is proposed, test it against the SUBSYSTEM'S PURPOSE with real data — a perf-guard (time window) aimed at the wrong axis can silently defeat the feature's whole reason to exist. (2026-06-28, run_19fe8649, manual)

- [guideline] **Gate-1 BLOCKing TWICE on the same run reversed a fix that was worse than the bug (run_17e3399c, ① briefing deliberate-pause guard).** The bug: briefing supersede-mark archived ANY paused run on pure recency, silently burying runs awaiting a human L2/judgment decision (6 lost in prod). My FIRST framing ("make briefing read-only, strip all mutations") — Gate-0 skeptic killed it: mutations 2+3 are the auto-resume ENGINE (only running→paused recovery path + emit throttle); stripping them breaks auto-resume. My SECOND plan (allowlist: archive only the crash marker) — Gate-1 killed it: (a) `continue` short-circuited the resume block → crash orphans archived before exhausting resume budget; (b) :856 is the SOLE paused→abandoned writer so empty-reason runs leak forever; (c) tests vacuous (default reason ≠ marker, no completed sibling). My THIRD plan (hand-rolled denylist) — Gate-1 killed AGAIN: substring `l2`/`block` false-matches `model2`/`roadblock` — **the exact bug class this codebase already fixed at artifact_cli:1542 with word-boundary matching**, and the hand-list diverged from the canonical vocab. CONVERGED (Plan C): REUSE `_checkpoint_reason_has_true_trigger` (single-source, word-boundary) + gate crash orphans on resume_attempts>=MAX + crash marker is explicitly not-a-true-trigger (verified `\bcrash\b` ∌ `session_crash_auto_detected`). The meta-lesson: **on a real-code fix to an entangled function, the FIRST 2-3 framings are usually wrong in ways only an adversarial reader sees — the gate firing repeatedly is the feature, not friction.** Also: Gate-2 surfaced a dead `escalat` stem in the SHARED matcher (a deliberate 'escalation' pause was unprotected); owned the fix in-flight (P4) rather than ticketing (C039) since it weakened THIS feature. 173 tests green, 0 regressions. (2026-06-27, run_17e3399c, manual)

- A test fix can itself be vacuous: repointing test_update_status to assert --status running round-trips would pass even if run-update wrote nothing, because running is the create-time default. Gate-2 caught it. When fixing a status/state round-trip test, the assertion target MUST differ from the create-time default or it proves nothing. (2026-06-27, run_ae689ce0, auto-cultivated)
- Stale-test triage has THREE outcomes, not two: STALE (update assertion), REAL-BUG (fix code), and NEEDS-COVERAGE (update setup but preserve the behavior the original implicitly covered). The Gate-0 skeptic caught that 2 of my 3 blind assertion-updates would have DELETED coverage (dup an existing guard test; lose the only paused-summary check) — triage the coverage, not just the pass/fail. (2026-06-27, run_ae689ce0, auto-cultivated)

- [guideline] **3 gates each caught a DISTINCT error class on a "pure text" change (run_48bd39cb, ② in-band L2 escalation)** — proof the gate stack isn't redundant ceremony even for a docs-only diff. Gate-0 skeptic found the SCOPE hole ("agent can't branch on session-type" → resolved by `channel=` header). Gate-1 found 3 DESIGN holes pre-code (graph fall-through would orphan; mid-stage in-band corrupts half-written state; background-job path has no `channel=` header → would hang to 600s) → forced design B (boundary-only + header-absent→checkpoint fail-safe). Gate-2 found 3 LOGIC misfires post-build (DEFER-at-evaluate wrongly in-band-eligible, contradicting Rule#11; §3a still bare-checkpointed bypassing the new block; boundary/mid-stage split by enumeration not a decidable test). None overlapped; happy-path grep (17/17 GREEN) would have shipped all 6. The lesson: adversarial review on PROTOCOL TEXT must hunt contradictions/ambiguity/misfire-when-followed, not code bugs — and it pays. (2026-06-27, run_48bd39cb, manual)

- **Gate placement is a design decision, not an afterthought: a quality gate belongs at the action's FREQUENCY-vs-COST inflection point (run_aba84c36, 2026-06-26).** The eval gate was first wired on `build` — but `build` is a high-frequency dev action (run many times a session), so the gate stalled iteration; `release` (rare, ships to others) is where "must pass Eval first" actually belongs. XG's correction "build 不能拦,只有发版才能拦" is the general rule: gate the rare-but-irreversible boundary, never the frequent-and-cheap one. Moving it = pure win (faster dev loop + same ship safety). Tell: if a gate fires on something you do dozens of times a day, it's on the wrong layer.

- [pitfall] **FTS5 external-content desync: the 'delete' command MUST bind OLD stored values, not the new ones (run_1d198980, 2026-06-26)** — Library `knowledge_fts` "database disk image is malformed" had a confident wrong diagnosis ("content table intact, just rebuild it"). The M3 skeptic (Gate 0) overturned it: the real root cause was `knowledge_store.py:189` — `upsert_chunk`'s update branch bound the **NEW** content/heading/source_file to the FTS5 `'delete'` command. External-content FTS5 needs the **OLD** stored values to correctly reverse the posting lists; binding new values decrements the wrong terms → progressive index corruption on every content update. Precedent was right there: `messages_fts` (sqlite.py:1977) uses `old.content`, and the `remove_*` methods read old values first. Rebuild alone is a band-aid — the buggy write re-corrupts it. Mutation-proven: reverting :189 reproduces the EXACT production error on the 2nd update. Two further wins this run: (1) **self-found a real bug in my own health probe by observation, not assumption** — `_fts_is_healthy` queried a no-match term ("healthprobe") that never traverses the corrupted posting lists, so it returned True on a corrupt index; a health check that misses the corruption it guards is worse than none (fixed to query an existing term + added a mutation-proven probe-detects-corruption test). (2) An **injected-corruption test was HARDER than the live corruption** and exposed that `'rebuild'` itself fails on a deeply-desynced index → forced the `drop+recreate` nuclear fallback (data-safe: content lives in `knowledge_chunks`, 27587 rows). Repair lives in `context_health_hook`, NOT the read path — recall stays read-only. Scope discipline: TranscriptStore.upsert_chunk has the same latent class (INSERT OR REPLACE without preceding 'delete') — flagged, not fixed (separate table, separate run). (run_1d198980)

- [decision] **Eval system-level decouple = data-boundary move, not code-rewrite (run_69b1c644+run_45cc58fa, 2026-06-26)** — Moving Eval out of `Projects/SwarmAI/` to top-level `SwarmWS/Eval/` decoupled it from DDD with surgical edits (3 path-builders + marker keyed on `Eval/golden_set.yaml`) instead of a rewrite. EVALUATE skeptic reading the code found the gate was already 80% decoupled via `programmatic_only`; the real work was the ship-boundary split. Migration was pure exposure-REDUCTION (all 161 were already public; moving 128 to gitignored private only reduces leak surface). `git mv` for tracked (public+EvalHistory), plain `mv` for gitignored private (git mv errors on untracked). Pattern: for "decouple X from Y", first probe how coupled they ACTUALLY are — the answer is often "less than the requirement assumes". (run_45cc58fa)

- [decision] **Git-bound eval gate: bind freshness to INPUTS not HEAD; 3-state not 2 (run_7a0ff10d, 2026-06-26)** — `ci_eval_gate.py` binds `code_digest` to the eval-relevant code+golden_set CONTENT, not git HEAD — so committing the eval report (which changes HEAD) doesn't self-invalidate the gate (the lock-file fixed-point trap). 3-state exit is what makes it shippable: exit1 (stale|red) BLOCKS, but exit2 (no gate-readable report) SOFT-warns + proceeds — without that a fresh clone / bootstrap with no report would be permanently un-shippable. Gate-2 caught the `set -e` interaction: `_x=$(cmd)` aborts the whole script on non-zero substitution BEFORE `$?` is read — wrap gate calls in `set +e/set -e`. **NOTE (run_aba84c36, 2026-06-26): the gate was later moved off `build` to the RELEASE boundary** (XG: "build 不能拦,只有发版才能拦") — build is high-frequency dev; gating it stalled iteration. Now in shared `_eval_gate()` covering cmd_release + cmd_release_hive + s_swarm-release PREFLIGHT, with a non-TTY fail-closed path and a `_EVAL_GATE_PASSED` once-per-process guard. (run_7a0ff10d → run_aba84c36)

- [pitfall] **Security redaction: allowlist not denylist; LIST and DETAIL are separate leak surfaces (run_1f588e53, 2026-06-26)** — Exposing a public/private badge on the eval golden set needed private-case content hidden. A denylist (strip 6 named fields) felt complete and LEAKED TWICE (Gate-2): missed `expected_response_contains` (answer keys) via detail, and `source` (governance refs) via the separate list-projection endpoint. Fix: invert to allowlist (fail-closed — new fields dropped by default) AND audit every endpoint returning the object, not just the visible drawer. When you reach for a denylist on security-sensitive data, that's the signal to invert it. (run_1f588e53)

The reuse-an-existing-template move (vs inventing a parallel mechanism) is what kept blast radius near-zero on a CRITICAL 177-caller validator. (2026-06-26, verified)
- EVALUATE skeptic + empirical sibling-scan resolved a deferred design fork for free (run_932c0991): PLAN deliberately deferred "presence-required vs fixture-update vs exemption" to BUILD pending an OBSERVED breakage count (R16b — don't guess). BUILD then grepped: every sibling gate test filters errors by its OWN tag (`_ug_errors`, `"REPRO gate" in e`) and the lifecycle tests monkeypatch `validate_artifact_data` to a stub. Conclusion: a DISTINCT error tag = zero fixture edits. The deferral cost one grep and avoided a guessed mass-fixture-rewrite. (2026-06-26, verified)
- Adversarial HIGH falsified by grep, not argued down (run_932c0991): Gate-2 claimed the gate was "publish-time-only = fail-open, unlike deliver mirrored into _check_depth." Instead of accepting OR dismissing by reasoning (CLASS A risk both ways), I grepped the call sites: the sibling Understanding Gate + REPRO gate are ALSO publish-time-only — only deliver is mirrored, because only deliver gates `status:completed`. The new gate has exact parity with the ACCEPTED Understanding Gate. The right response to an adversarial HIGH is an observation that confirms OR refutes it, never a verbal downgrade. (The 2 real LOWs it found — dead bool-guard, no self-report consistency — were fixed immediately.) (2026-06-26, verified)
- runtime_health coverage (run_4596411e): applying RP45/R16b paid off — by READING the shipped test patterns (test_no_cross_tab_evict, TestDumbSpawnWatchdog) before writing the harnesses, this run had ZERO wrong guesses, vs the prior run's two (wrong error string + wrong stream seam). The fastest way to drive a REAL guard path in a fault-injection harness is to mirror the project's existing test driver for that path, not to reconstruct it. (2026-06-26, verified)
- A schedule-LOCK test must guarantee the thing is GRADED, not merely PRESENT (run_4596411e, Gate-2 MEDIUM): my membership test asserted GS_RTH cases survive the biweekly filter, but a future tier:draft demotion would still 'survive' yet be silently SKIPPED in the sweep (skipped != failed) — the guard lost with no failing signal. A lock on 'X runs in CI' must assert X is non-draft/non-skipped AND scores into a dimension, not just that X is loaded. (2026-06-26, verified)

- Runtime session-health (run_f646b175): the EVALUATE skeptic delivered the run's biggest win BEFORE any code — it found that the RP41 "double-signal over-interval" wedged discriminator I was about to invent ALREADY exists and is production-tested (`resource_monitor.tree_cpu_seconds` + `session_unit._tool_hang_probe`). - Pure-logic-with-injected-deps test → MUST pair with one real-wiring test. (run_f646b175) My AC1 probe test injected a fake `rss_fetcher`, so it passed while the REAL `_fetch_rss` called a non-existent method (`get_system_memory`) reading non-existent fields → the RSS check was SILENTLY DEAD (fail-safe-passed forever). Adversarial Gate-2 found both CRITICALs. This is GUI01 (authorship blind spot) in its purest mechanical form: injecting all dependencies makes a test prove the LOGIC while leaving the WIRING (the adapter that constructs the real deps) completely uncovered. The fix pattern: for every pure-logic unit with injected I/O, add ≥1 test that exercises the real adapter against the live resource. (2026-06-26, verified)

- Reversible Context Recall (run_9de88af9): Gate-2 adversarial caught 2 CRITICAL data-leak bypasses of the AC4 privacy gate that my OWN AC4 tests missed — case-insensitivity (`memory.md` ≠ `MEMORY.md` but APFS reads the same file) and path-traversal (`../MEMORY.md`). My self-authored tests covered only the happy denial path (`recall("MEMORY.md", excluded={MEMORY.md})`), never the variants. This is GUI01 (authorship blind spot) recurring on a SECURITY gate: the most dangerous place to trust your own tests is exactly where you wrote both the guard and its test. The fix pattern that held: normalize identity (casefold basename + inode `(st_dev,st_ino)` check), never compare raw user-supplied names; a privacy gate must default-DENY (`--session-type required=True`). Re-verified all CLOSED live by resuming the same adversarial agent. (2026-06-26, verified)

- [guideline] **Researching two external references in parallel produced an executable design, not a prose summary — and they cross-validated the shape (2026-06-26, Understanding Gate design)** — Asked to evaluate the pipeline against `awslabs/aidlc-workflows` v2 and `aws-samples/sample-ai-plc`, the output that earned its keep was a DESIGN DOC + a tracked open item, NOT a "here's what they do" report. The decisive value: the two repos converged on the SAME structure from opposite ends — ai-plc gives the *implementation* (envision.md problem-only and solution-analysis.md are separate files with a `⛔ GATE` between; a dedicated `overconfidence-prevention.md` that inverts defaults from "ask only if needed" to "ask unless exceptionally clear"), and aidlc-v2 gives the *theory* (P3: same-source generate+judge converges to satisfying-the-letter, so an LLM-only-verified stage can't self-stop = the theoretical NAME for CLASS A; P6: staged decomposition beats single-shot). Convergence from two independent sources = high confidence the "diagnosis↔solution wall" shape is right, not a one-off idea. METHOD that generalizes: (1) when researching to improve our own system, the deliverable is a falsifiable design + open item, not restated facts (Research Quality Gate); (2) prefer TWO references that approach from different angles — agreement between them is the signal; (3) the design must address the SELF-DIAGNOSED gap (here Gap 1: dive-deep before build), with the failures it would have caught named explicitly (run_6adee7d5 `[DONE]` no-op). NOT a competitive-threat read — a mirror + theory-backing read (avoids COR03 self-underrating: we already shipped the artifact_id gate they're still drafting in issue #366). Design: `Knowledge/Designs/2026-06-26-understanding-gate-design.md`. (2026-06-26, source:manual)

- [guideline] **GC11 held under pressure: designed the pipeline-governance change, but REFUSED to implement it in the same long session (2026-06-26)** — The Understanding Gate changes a core pipeline quality gate. GC11 explicitly warns against designing pipeline governance in a long/fatigued session (session-level cognitive decay), and this session had already produced TWO confident-but-wrong diagnoses earlier. The discipline that worked: separate the decay-RESISTANT work (research + design doc — facts on disk, checkable) from the decay-SENSITIVE work (implementation — judgment under fatigue), do only the former now, and tag the open item + design frontmatter + §7-R4 with an explicit "implement in a FRESH session via pipeline" gate. This is the structural answer to Gap 4 (long-session decay) applied to MY OWN process, not just the product's: externalize the conclusion to a file, let a clean-context session execute against it. Self-dogfood AC recorded so the implementing run must pass its own new gate. (2026-06-26, source:manual)

- [decision] **PIT01 zombie ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED at source + PRODUCTION-VERIFIED — both poison paths, third-time trigger closure (run_f1bd4b51, 2026-06-26)** — The recurring zombie-on-reuse (CLI subprocess returns instant empty `error_during_execution` → kill+resume ~10s stall) is fixed at the SOURCE, not lazily detected on reuse. Trigger attribution was wrong twice before (AC6 tool-budget, then "user-Stop only"); this run confirmed the MECHANISM is general (any soft `client.interrupt()` that ENDS the turn leaves the CLI in corrupt turn-state, left warm for reuse) and fixed BOTH entry paths: `interrupt(autonomous=False)` (user Stop + compaction — both `return` after interrupt) AND `flush_subprocess_pipe` clean-success branch (SSE disconnect — the trigger L1055 proved was the real 7/7 source). Fix = recycle eagerly via the blessed kill path `_crash_to_cold_async(clear_identity=False)`: real kill + FD cleanup, `_lock` held, `_sdk_session_id` preserved so next send() respawns clean WITH `--resume`. EXCLUDED: `interrupt(autonomous=True)` = tool-hang watchdog ONLY (keeps STREAMING, lets the model reroute warm). **Production-verified 02:19**: user Stop → `flush_recycle` → recycled to COLD (resume id preserved) → next send spawned fresh pid → direct idle→streaming, zombie count 32 UNCHANGED (0 new). The disconnect path firing in the live test (not the interrupt path) retroactively proved Gate-1 finding-6 right: fixing only `interrupt()` would have left this exact case broken. (2026-06-26, run_f1bd4b51, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [decision] **Two pipeline gates each earned their keep on a CRITICAL module (session_unit.py, 353 callers) — Gate-1 blocked 2×, Gate-2 caught test false-confidence (run_f1bd4b51, 2026-06-26)** — A clean root-cause + existing spec is NOT license to skip rigor. Gate-1 (Skeptic+SSA) BLOCKED the plan TWICE pre-code: v1 bare `_transition(COLD)` would orphan the subprocess + FD leak; v2's durable `_needs_recycle` flag leaked through `continue_with_answer`/`permission` (bypass send() Layer-0 reset); blanket recycle would collapse the watchdog warm ladder — converged to narrowest correct scope. Gate-2 (adversarial) caught a CRITICAL the author missed: my OWN test passed `autonomous=True` manually to "verify compaction stays warm", but the real compaction caller uses DEFAULT args → it actually recycles. The test exercised a flag value production never uses = FALSE CONFIDENCE (GUI04 class: a self-authored test covers what you ASSUMED, not the real call path). Resolving it FLIPPED the design (compaction SHOULD recycle — returns after interrupt = turn ends = poisoned; code was right, design DOC was wrong). Added a test pinning the real default-arg signature. (2026-06-26, run_f1bd4b51, source:manual)

- [guideline] **health-check skill probes rot silently — 3 stale probes gave false WARNs for weeks (run_3e9c1433, 2026-06-26)** — A self-maintenance tool's own probes are a decay class: v7 file moves (`include_partial_messages` session_unit.py→prompt_builder.py), endpoint renames (`/api/mcp/status` removed → `/api/mcp`), and build-mode assumptions (PyInstaller onefile→onedir, `test -f`→`test -d`) all silently rotted probes into false WARNs — masking real issues as "expected noise". Fixed all 3; adversarial caught 2 LOWs: `GET /api/mcp` pre-filters disabled servers so enabled/total was vacuous (dead code) → report active count; `=True` exact grep is whitespace-brittle → `\s*=\s*True`. Lesson: a probe reporting the WRONG answer is worse than no probe — it trains you to ignore the signal. (2026-06-26, run_3e9c1433, source:manual)

- **The pipeline now structurally enforces the lesson this session kept teaching: diagnosis-before-build for bug fixes** (run_688b6487, goal profile, 6/6 DoD, 5 commits, 205 tests). Two improvements ship together: (1) a code-enforced **REPRO gate** in `pipeline_validator.validate_artifact_data` — a bug-class evaluation (scope=bugfix) is BLOCKED at publish unless it carries non-empty `observation_evidence` (ps/log-signal-counts/live-gauge/repro, not inference); (2) a human-spawned **Diagnostic-Challenge Gate** in evaluate.md — a fresh-context skeptic sub-agent that REFUTES a bug root cause before THINK (verdict SUPPORTED/UNSUPPORTED/ALREADY-FIXED/WRONG-LAYER). These directly target THIS session's two framing-error failures (the fabricated kernel-OOM narrative; "[DONE] no-op") — both would now be caught at EVALUATE for ~1 sub-agent's cost instead of a full pipeline + external reviewer. THE META-MOVE: rather than add another EVOLUTION.md correction entry ("be more careful"), the fix is a **mechanical gate the model cannot rationalize past** (SOUL "I am the OS not the model"). Also shipped `artifact_cli publish --quiet` (parse-proof single-line output) — the ceremony cost that cost me 3+ manual artifact_id recoveries this session was output-SHAPE, not missing plumbing (single-line success + stage auto-record already existed; I just wasn't using --stage). Dogfooded it in this run's own deliver publish: clean, zero fumble. Cross-path adversarial found 3 real holes (pollinate path ignored --quiet, bare-True passed REPRO, profile not relaxed) — all fixed; 2 HIGHs correctly NOTED-not-fixed (self-label evadability → the challenge sub-agent is the backstop, not keyword-sniffing; breaking-change verified non-breaking against 3 in-flight runs).

- **OT01 spinner-hang root cause found by 3-SIGNAL LOG TRIAGE, not by reading code — and an external review corrected my mis-framed fix mid-flight.** (run_6adee7d5, 2026-06-26). The recurring "spinner spins after backend idle" (~33 fixes, all on the force-clear *backstop*) was finally root-caused by counting THREE mutually-exclusive frontend log signals: premature-disconnect (2), stall-timer 90s (3), and silent (~109) out of 114 idle force-clears. The 109 silent = `[DONE]` arrived + `onComplete` fired but `createCompleteHandler` early-returned on a STALE `streamGen` (bumped mid-stream by reconnect — the 65s reconnect budget widened the window) BEFORE `setIsStreaming(false)` → spinner pinned, rescued only by the 30s reconcile backstop. Fix: per-tab `latestCompleteGen` marks the live completer (immune to reconnect churn, superseded only by a new send). TWO external corrections shaped this: (1) Kiro flagged my ORIGINAL requirement ("make frontend treat [DONE] as authoritative") as a NO-OP — chat.ts:276 already does that; the real defect was the stale-gen gate, not [DONE] recognition. I had started building the wrong fix. (2) Gate-2 adversarial caught my fix introduced a FAIL-OPEN (`latestCompleteGen ?? capturedGen` = always-clear when unset) → hardened to fail-safe (unset = non-authoritative no-op). META-LESSON: for a bug that recurred 33× on the backstop, the discriminator was never in the code — it was in COUNTING the runtime signals to prove WHICH of the candidate mechanisms actually fired. Inference ("frontend ignores [DONE]") was wrong; signal-counting ("109 silent, not disconnect, not stall") was right. And both my framing AND my fix had a flaw that only fresh eyes (Kiro + adversarial) caught — confidence was anti-correlated with correctness, again.

- **Adversarial Gate-2 caught a HIGH that made the entire feature DEAD in production — a self-authored unit test could not, by construction.** Dumb-spawn watchdog (run_6c482b10): the discriminator `_last_event_time <= _streaming_start_time` was meant to detect "no SDK event since spawn." But `_transition()` set the two fields via TWO separate `time.time()` calls, so `_last_event_time` was always microseconds GREATER → the branch NEVER fired in prod → the watchdog the whole pipeline was built for did nothing. All 8 unit tests passed because the fixture set the two timestamps EQUAL — it encoded the author's assumption, not production's two-call reality. This is the CLASS-A authorship trap in its purest form: "I wrote the test, so it's covered" ≠ "the test reproduces production." THE FIX THAT GENERALIZES: for any predicate over runtime-set state, add ONE integration test that drives the REAL state-producer (here: call real `_transition` and assert the predicate holds) — not just unit tests with hand-set values. Proof the test works: reintroduce the bug → integration test goes RED (verified); the equal-timestamp unit fixtures stay GREEN (blind). A fixture that hand-sets the value you're testing is structurally incapable of catching a producer that sets it differently. (2026-06-26, run_6c482b10)

- [guideline] **Idempotency teeth must be at EVERY merge/record path, not just the entry point** — run_448a4f7f autonomous-recording: the SAME failure class (counter inflation from non-idempotent counting) fired at TWO layers. Gate-1 caught dedup-on-evidence-text (live correction_ref d6d44889 was parked 3× with 3 different evidence strings → text-dedup triple-counts; fix: dedup on stable correction_ref). Gate-2 then caught `_merge_drift` summing count across drifted members but keeping only members[0].recorded_refs → a dropped ref re-records and re-inflates. A counter is only as idempotent as its LEAST-guarded write path. When adding a dedup ledger, audit every path that mutates the count (record, merge, load) — fix one, the others still leak. (2026-06-25)

- _persist_golden_set cross-process TOCTOU close (run_0fac5a91, commit c643451a): the disk-only fix narrowed but didn't close the race — two SwarmAI processes (daemon + CLI eval) could still interleave re-read→merge→rename. Closed by activating utils/file_lock.py (cross-platform flock helpers that had ZERO callers — built and never wired). Activating existing infra beat writing new lock code: fewer lines, Windows msvcrt fallback free (STEERING #2/#3). Two points that mattered: (1) the lock must span the WHOLE read-modify-write (acquire before the disk re-read, release after the rename) — locking only the write leaves TOCTOU open; extracted the body to _merge_and_write() so the lock wraps it cleanly. (2) The RED test holds the sidecar lock from a background fd and asserts persist BLOCKS until release — a behavioral test of mutual exclusion, not a "lock object exists" check. Adversarial SHIP but flagged a real MEDIUM: blocking flock acquired while holding the in-process _data_lock = head-of-line-blocking if a peer hangs. Deliberately scoped to a follow-up todo rather than tack on a timeout under fatigue — the timeout fix is itself a design decision (skip-on-timeout reintroduces the data loss; raise fails the op), not a mechanical add. (2026-06-25, run_0fac5a91)
- _persist_golden_set disk-only data-loss fix (run_fb4b42d2, commit 22828615): the merge loop iterated only self._cases, so a case added to golden_set.yaml by ANOTHER session after this process loaded it was silently dropped on the next persist (caused the 13-case corruption incl all GS_TRAJ). Before touching code, the reverse-risk had to be cleared: "won't appending disk-only cases resurrect a deleted one?" — grepped eval_service.py and confirmed delete_case is SOFT (tier=archived, stays in _cases) with NO hard-remove path, so disk-only ALWAYS = externally-added. That single invariant check is what made the fix safe AND simple (append + dedup set), and it's encoded as a loud INVARIANT comment so a future hard-delete path can't silently turn it into a resurrection bug. The bug had ZERO test coverage — a self-authored "merge-preserve" docstring claimed it already prevented this (it only preserved FIELDS on in-both cases, not whole disk-only CASES). Lesson: a function whose docstring describes data-loss PREVENTION is exactly where to demand a RED test that reproduces the loss — the prose was confidently wrong. (2026-06-25, run_fb4b42d2)
- Rule-23 retry-once worked as designed: the Gate-2 adversarial Agent spawn was rejected once (tool-layer), and instead of falling back to self-review (the CLASS A bypass), a single fresh in-turn retry succeeded and returned SHIP after independently tracing 7 attack lines + running 87 tests + diffing the RED test against HEAD~1. The strongest adversarial line (could a stale disk copy of a user-owned field clobber a programmatic update?) was only retired by the sub-agent enumerating EVERY writer of tags/notes/promoted_from and confirming none exist — proof by exhaustion, not assertion. (2026-06-25, run_fb4b42d2)

- **Self-evolution closed loop — remaining 5 items shipped (run_cf491cab, goal profile, 5 cycles)** — M4-3 score-divergence flag, M4-4 seed replayability verification, finding-1 (sibling-committed, verified not re-litigated), M5 closed-loop audit (Goodhart guard + loop-closed meta-test), M3.5 APPLY-gate redesign doc. Key lessons: (1) **Meta-review caught the highest-value defect, not the code adversarial** — the divergence banner fired on has_red() which includes rule-only chronic recurrence (live OPERATIONAL 799x), so it would fire EVERY session forever = banner-blindness. Code review can't catch this (the code is correct); only the operational "what fires in steady-state after 6 months?" lens does. Fix: has_gate_failure() — divergence is event-like (a deployed gate failed), not level-like (anything red). (2) **Pure-function-first made every milestone testable on synthetic state** — compute_score_divergence, audit_recurrence, loop_closed_meta_test are all I/O-free, so divergence/Goodhart/loop-closure are asserted without a live eval run. (3) **My own M5 test caught a Goodhart-ordering bug** — gate_failed masked logged_less because the collapsed-capture denominator must be checked FIRST (you can't trust recurrence counts you stopped logging). (4) **STEERING #7 collision discipline paid off** — finding-1 was committed by a parallel sibling session mid-pipeline (eval_service.py); I verified ownership via git, built ON it, never reverted. (5) Adversarial found 5 (2 CRITICAL: string-score crash, None-entry crash that would make Phase-3d a permanent silent no-op); meta-review found 1 HIGH (banner-blindness). All 6 fixed, 215 tests green. (2026-06-25, run_cf491cab)

Fix = single render source (run_9db9f987, commits 70b4108b+e0de50c7). (2) Parallel session: background-command no-timeout hang (the 12-min `find &`, AGENT.md "a hang IS a failure") — CLI #61568 is unfixable at the model/rule layer (Anthropic: "defense comes from outside the agent"; Claude Code provably ignores deny-rules + CLAUDE.md), so the fix is a PreToolUse `background_command_guard` hook that default-denies backgrounding (commit 8bb8b849). PIT06 in action: GracefulEscalation's base/escalated verdicts are INJECTED, so ONE shape served 3 triggers (streaming-timeout KILL→KILL_HARD, tool-hang INTERRUPT→KILL, OOM KILL→KILL_HARD). Verify-before-build found it was dead via grep, and the deadlock audit (does _spawn_lock nest with it? do callers hold it?) cleared it BEFORE coding. A dead lock that already exists at the right scope is the cheapest possible fix for a concurrency bug. (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)

All three passed the per-milestone tests — the value was in the FINAL integrated cross-path review, not the per-milestone ones. The cross-path question "do two DIFFERENT triggers race the same unit?" is what surfaced the kill()-bypass. (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)

- **Side-channel SSE events (toast-only, no store/streamState writes) must be handled BEFORE the cross-turn-bleed generation guard, not after.** The guard exists to drop stale stream content from an interrupted turn — but a session-scoped signal (recovery_exhausted) is semantically true regardless of turn generation, and is emitted post-`result` so it always looks "stale" to the guard. Handle-before-guard + immediate `return` is the correct pattern (mirrors file_changed). Verified safe: bypassing the guard trades a guaranteed missed-toast for an at-most-one (backend high-water-mark gate), self-clearing (session_start backstop), semantically-correct straggler. - **R6a (run_6ea35431): the binding constraint was NOT where the design first pointed.** The design's two-limit-split framing (§9.4) implied changing `spawn_budget`'s penalty input (`alive_count`→`streaming_count`). Gate-1 SSA BLOCKED that: `spawn_budget`'s `alive_count` penalty IS the COE05 simultaneous-peak floor (resource_monitor.py:293-299 docstring says so verbatim), and `streaming_count` can't see idle→stream transitions at spawn-time → reopens COE05. The REAL fix was far narrower: the `compute_max_tabs` COUNT CEILING (not the penalty) fired before the budget gate, so removing it from the 3 backend consumers + leaving `spawn_budget` untouched fixed the user pain (eviction on memory-abundant machines) AND preserved the OOM floor. Lesson: when a design names a mechanism to change, verify it's the BINDING constraint before touching it — `spawn_budget` already permitted the 4th tab; only the ceiling blocked it. The penalty-swap was both wrong and unnecessary. (2026-06-24)

- A bool return that collapses N outcomes (not-found/duplicate/not-safe) into False is a silent-failure generator: the caller cannot distinguish a config-drift BUG from a benign no-op, so drift gets counted as benign rejection. Return an explicit status enum the moment one False has >1 meaning (ddd_cultivation.apply_to_ddd). (2026-06-22, run_6b52f221, auto-cultivated)

- **Both pipeline gates caught different error CLASSES in the same 1-line change (P3 slot fix, run_002eca4c).** Gate 1 (skeptic) caught that the FIRST plan (P1) targeted the wrong eviction path entirely — the verified incident was a queued probe force-killing a freshly-idled tab at the wake re-check (session_router.py:924 `force=True`), not the count-full branch P1 addressed. Re-scoped to P3. Gate 2 (adversarial) then caught that the corrected 1-line fix (`force=True`→`force=False`) WIDENED a pre-existing latent race: wakers now loop on within-grace misses, exposing a lost-wakeup on the shared `_slot_available` Event that could stall a waker up to 300s. Lesson: a "trivial" 1-line flag flip on a CRITICAL concurrency primitive is exactly where adversarial earns its keep — the fix was correct in isolation but changed the loop's *frequency of looping*, turning a benign latent bug reachable. Neither gate was ceremony. (2026-06-21, run_002eca4c)

- **Pipeline v4 "BUILD is the Root Cause" (2026-05-29, commit 75c128df)** — Three structural improvements: (1) Litmus Pre-Gate (Check 8e, 30s pre-screen, PASS/BORDERLINE/FAIL with code enforcement), (2) AC Coverage Matrix (Check 8f, every PLAN AC must map to impl+test+verified, cross-reference with identifier matching), (3) TEST 3-Layer Strategy (Check 8g, BLOCK for full/bugfix when ac_driven not run, cross-artifact count validation). Also: adversarial prompts refocused on bugs not gaming (~30% FP expected reduction), DELIVER inherits REVIEW litmus weak_areas, ac_verification in STAGE_DEPTH, human_override requires reason. Two rounds adversarial per component (litmus: 17 findings, AC: 10 findings, all addressed). +1056 lines, 155 tests (was 114). Root insight: REVIEW/DELIVER were compensating for low BUILD quality — fixing BUILD quality upstream reduces downstream overhead.

- [pitfall] **Exit code for control flow, never grep output — grep masks crashes AND misses severity variations** — `prod.sh` used `grep -q "STALE"` to check staleness: (1) missed `[MISSING]` severity, (2) if python crashed, traceback doesn't contain "STALE" → grep fails → else-branch says "all good". Same pattern: any `command | grep pattern` for control flow silently passes on left-side crash when pipefail is off. Rule: use exit codes. If the tool doesn't have exit codes for your case, add them. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 1)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **Module-level mutable globals break on 2nd call — "works in isolation" ≠ "works as a library"** — `_SCRIPT_DEADLINE = 0.0` set once on first call, never reset on subsequent import-based calls. Pattern: any module-level mutable state that's set inside a function but read on every invocation will be stale on 2nd+ call. Test: call the entry function twice with different expectations. Fix: always-reset at function entry, or use function-local state. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 1)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **`sorted(path.iterdir())` sorts lexicographically on filenames — UUID-named dirs have NO chronological order** — `run_a3f7b2c1` sorts before `run_f8985057` but may be newer. Any code that needs "most recent" from a directory scan MUST read timestamps from file contents (e.g., `created_at` in run.json), not rely on directory names. Pattern existed in TWO places (`cmd_publish` and `_auto_validate_before_advance`) — same bug, different call sites. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 2)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [decision] **Physical path separation is the right architecture for safe updates** -- Both Desktop (app bundle vs ~/.swarm-ai/) and Hive (/opt/swarmai/ vs /home/swarm/.swarm-ai/) use the same pattern: code directory is fully replaceable, user data directory is never touched by updates. This survived 2 live deploys, 1 SSM update test, and 1 manual rsync. AGENT.md and SOUL.md are system-owned (rebuilt from code on startup) — users get new agent behavior automatically, which is the right default. Pattern: any system that separates "what the system does" from "what the user has accumulated" should use physical path separation, not file-level merging. (2026-04-29, Hive update architecture)

- [decision] **Layer versioning on existing detection-based migrations, don't replace** -- DB had 19+ detection-based column migrations (PRAGMA table_info). Instead of rewriting them as versioned, layered PRAGMA user_version on top: Phase 1 (legacy detection) runs only at version=0, Phase 2 (versioned) runs incrementally. Zero migration rewritten, new migrations slot in with `if current < N:`. Returning users hit 1ms fast path (single PRAGMA read). Pattern: when extending a working system, add a version gate above it — don't refactor the working code into the new scheme. (2026-04-28, run_91a6fb7e)

- [decision] **Shadow mode validates infrastructure before wiring it live** -- Recall L2/L3 (12,872 indexed chunks) was built 4 weeks ago but never triggered in production. Instead of wiring it directly (and risking low-quality recall polluting agent context), shadow mode logs results without injection. First data point: FTS5-only = 14ms/12 hits, FTS5+embedding = 1827ms/12 hits — same results, 130× slower. Two weeks of shadow data will answer "is FTS5 sufficient?" before committing to a production path. Pattern: for any infrastructure that's built-but-unvalidated, add a shadow/observability layer first. The cost (fire-and-forget task + JSONL append) is negligible; the risk mitigation is significant. (2026-04-14, run_419ac912)

- [guideline] **PermissionError is not ProcessLookupError** -- `os.kill(pid, 0)` raises `PermissionError` (subclass of `OSError`) for processes owned by other users. Catching `(OSError, ProcessLookupError)` treats alive-but-unowned processes as dead. Fix: catch `PermissionError` separately and treat as "alive". This matters for daemon conflict checks where the daemon runs as the same user but PID 1 (launchd) owns the port check socket. Pattern: any PID liveness check must handle 3 states: alive+ours, alive+theirs (PermissionError), dead (ProcessLookupError).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **boot_id beats poll frequency for restart detection** -- A 10s health poll misses a daemon restart that completes in <10s. Adding a `boot_id` (random string generated at startup) to the health response lets the watchdog detect silent restarts without reducing poll frequency to sub-second. The watchdog compares boot_id across polls — change means restart even if health never went unhealthy. This is the standard pattern for detecting process identity changes across restarts.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Single source of truth for test execution** -- pyproject.toml `addopts = "-n 4"` is the ONLY place that controls xdist worker count. conftest.py only downgrades (memory pressure) or overrides bypasses (agent passing `-n 0`). Makefile has zero `-n` logic — it delegates entirely to pyproject.toml. One config, one enforcement point, zero escape hatches. Arrived at this after 12 commits of doing it wrong (runtime injection, multiple entry points, silent fallbacks).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Loud fallbacks prevent invisible degradation** -- Every xdist override path in conftest.py prints to stderr with `⚠️` or `✓`. For 5+ days, tests silently ran serial (no xdist) because every fallback was `except: pass` or `if not hasattr: return`. Nobody noticed. The print-to-stderr pattern costs nothing and makes every test run self-documenting.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Agent behavioral constraints are system constraints** -- The agent generates pytest commands. Without an explicit rule in AGENT.md ("never pass `-n 0`"), the agent's self-healing behavior (crash → disable xdist) bypassed 12 layers of conftest defense. Constraining the agent's command generation was more effective than all the runtime guards combined.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Ship heuristic first, layer ML later** -- Evolution Optimizer built with correction-pattern heuristics (zero dependencies) and DSPy-ready interface. The heuristic handles the common case ("don't X" → remove, "use Y" → add) at near-zero cost. GEPA/DSPy is an additive enhancement, not a prerequisite. This shipped Phase 3 in one session instead of blocking on pip install dspy. (2026-04-10, run_8266f10b)

### Architecture Decision Records

**ADR: code-intel v3 internal (non-HTTP) subsystems anchor their domain-flow to a real RELATED route, not a synthetic symbol-flow** (2026-07-18, run_89e28075)
Context: 6 gap subsystems (context-memory, self-evolution, pipeline, proactive-intelligence, channels-runtime, services-jobs) are mostly internal — they have few or no HTTP routes. To make them "covered" domains they need a flow, but `finalize_v3` fail-closed requires every `flow.entry_ref` to resolve to a real `route.id` (a symbol/file-path entry_ref is rejected as dangling). Decision: each internal subsystem's flow anchors at the single most-representative REAL route that exercises it (services-jobs→`POST /api/jobs/run`, pipeline→`POST /api/artifacts/pipeline/advance`, self-evolution→`POST /api/escalations/{project}/{id}/resolve`, context-memory→`/api/memory/save-session`+`/api/recall/coverage`, proactive→`GET /api/system/briefing`, channels-runtime→`POST /api/channels/{id}/start`) + carries the subsystem's real business_rules with verified file:line anchors. Rejected alternatives: (a) synthetic `entry_type=symbol` flows with a `file::Symbol` entry_ref — REJECTED by the fail-closed gate (doesn't resolve to a route.id), and rightly so: it would let a domain claim coverage with no real entry point; (b) flowless domains + teaching `compute_subsystem_coverage` to credit `business_rules[].anchor` as evidence — viable but weakens the coverage signal (a domain could claim coverage by naming a file it never enters). Why it's hard to reverse: it sets the pattern for how EVERY future internal subsystem enters the v3 domain layer, and the route-anchor is what both the accounting gate AND `compute_subsystem_coverage` key off — changing it means re-authoring all internal domains + the coverage mechanism. The route-anchor is truthful (those routes DO exercise the subsystem) AND doubles as the accounting-backfill for the 4.8%→100% first-order gap.

**ADR: Socratic method in the autonomous pipeline = interrogate the spec/framing, NOT ask the user more** (2026-06-26, run_932c0991)
Context: Amazon PR/FAQ and the AWS sample-ai-plc / aidlc-workflows repos use Socratic, gated questioning to elicit requirements — but they are human-in-the-loop IDE assistants where "Socratic" means writing questions into a file and BLOCKING for the user's answer. SwarmAI's pipeline is autonomous. Decision: the correct port of the Socratic method to an autonomous pipeline is a *self-Socratic* micro-loop — the stage re-scans its OWN clarification output (EVALUATE WHO/WHAT/WHY/WHEN; THINK risk-probe assumptions) for ambiguity/hedge terms and self-answers each hit by reading code/DDD, escalating to the user ONLY what is genuinely unknowable. This is the same philosophy as the just-shipped Understanding Gate ("the claim must survive a skeptic's refutation") shifted LEFT and narrowed to the requirement-clarification layer — interrogate the framing before building, not after. Rejected alternatives: (a) the interactive ask-the-user grill protocol — already downgraded once because it was "almost always skipped" (think.md), and human-blocking gates become rubber stamps (aidlc #366/#367 ceremonial-gate bugs); (b) full PR/FAQ Working-Backwards — valuable but only for greenfield product framing, deferred as a greenfield-only follow-up (plan B). Why it's hard to reverse: it sets the questioning philosophy for every future EVALUATE/THINK and is validator-enforced (`_check_ambiguity_scan`), so changing it means re-teaching the gate and re-cutting the stage docs. The enforcement parallels the Understanding Gate exactly (strict-require / relaxed-exempt / distinct error tag), so the two form one coherent "diagnose-before-build" family rather than two competing mechanisms.

- [decision] **Projected-but-not-injected reference files = zero-cost shared context** -- CONTEXT.md (ubiquitous language glossary) needed to be available to the agent but NOT in every session's system prompt (wasteful for non-skill work). Solution: `_SYSTEM_REFERENCE_FILES` in `ensure_directory()` — copied to `.context/` on startup (always-overwrite from template) but not in `CONTEXT_FILES` list (no prompt injection). Same pattern as `_AGENT_MANAGED_FILES` but system-owned instead of agent-owned. Reusable for any future reference doc (CONVENTIONS.md, GLOSSARY.md, etc.) that should be on-demand, not always-on. (2026-04-30, run_d2b80cbe)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->

- [pitfall] **A read endpoint that "displays a metric" must READ the stored score, never CALL the compute function — the computer often WRITES (2026-08-04, run_d7146171):** the DDD-health-in-BrainHub run planned to surface trust/health on `GET /api/ddd/brains/{name}` by calling `compute_section_health(project_dir)`. Gate-1 (pre-code skeptic) caught that `compute_section_health` **writes** `section_health.json` as a side effect (ddd_health.py:362) — so every brain-open would mutate disk from a GET handler (concurrency race + REST violation + violates the router's own "computed live per request, no stored metric" contract). Fix: read the already-computed score via the read-only `_load_last_scores`; absent → `null` (honest "no scheduled score yet"), never trigger the write. **Durable tell: before a read/GET path calls any `compute_*`/`build_*`/`refresh_*` function to "get a value to show", grep that function for a write (`os.replace`, `.write_text`, `tmp`+rename) — if it persists, read its OUTPUT file instead of calling it.** Pairs with the Gate-1 MAJOR same run: do NOT invent a rollup/average to fill a headline number (that's a vanity metric) — surface the stored per-item scores verbatim.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **Verify WHICH endpoint owns a field before claiming to "enrich the existing X" (2026-08-04, run_d7146171):** the EVALUATE frame was "enrich the existing `health` block in `brains/{name}`". The M3 skeptic (same-model, refute-stance) found `health` lives ONLY in `_brain_summary` (the `/brains` GALLERY list) — the `/brains/{name}` DETAIL endpoint (`_brain_detail`) has no health block at all. So "enrich existing" was a WRONG-FRAME; it had to be ADDED to the detail path. **Durable tell: a field's presence in ONE endpoint of a router does NOT mean the sibling endpoint returns it — grep the specific handler you're extending (`get_brain -> _brain_detail`), not just "the router has a health block somewhere".** Same authorship-blind-spot family as the deploy-state traps: I asserted a code fact from a plausible frame instead of tracing the exact handler. Containment held via the mandatory skeptic, not self-catch.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [decision] **Universal Understanding Gate — shift diagnosis left for ALL work types (2026-06-26, run_862fa4e0, goal pipeline)** — Generalized the bug-only REPRO gate into a work-type-shaped Understanding Gate at the EVALUATE→THINK boundary (`pipeline_validator.py::_check_understanding_gate`). Strict profiles (full/bugfix/goal/standard/unknown — fail-closed) now require an observation-backed, present-tense (M1 solution-language scan), non-hedged (M2 hedge scan) `understanding` block before THINK can propose a fix. Catches the run_6adee7d5 class: a FEATURE framed as "frontend ignores [DONE]" when `chat.ts:276` already treated it as authoritative — the "fix" was a no-op a bug-only gate would never catch. Evidence form varies by work_type (code-trace / repro / characterization / premortem); bug-class back-compat via the `observation_evidence` alias (REPRO marker preserved). 4 surfaces: validator + evaluate.md + INSTRUCTIONS.md + test_understanding_gate.py (46 force-execution tests). Dev/CI-only — no daemon build. Commits 2479fed2 + 58923b50.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

- [pitfall] **The authorship trap applies to GATES too — my new gate's flagship example slipped its own check (2026-06-26, run_862fa4e0)** — Built the Understanding Gate specifically to catch the `[DONE]` no-op framing, wrote M1 to block solution-language claims, and the Gate-2 adversarial sub-agent then proved my M1 regex did NOT match "Make [DONE] authoritative" (the verb `make` wasn't in my list; the imperative pattern wanted `\w` but `[DONE]` starts with `[`). Same session, same person: I authored the gate AND the blind spot. The adversarial also found a HIGH the whole TDD cycle missed — `standard` profile (a documented rank-4 alias for full) fell through both my strict/relaxed sets → gate silently dead on the most common legacy strict profile. Both are CLASS_A authorship-trap instances: I had perfect understanding of intent and shipped coverage gaps anyway. Fixes: fail-closed default (strict unless explicitly relaxed — closes standard + all unknowns in one move) + widened M1 verbs/gerunds/imperative + `_strip_negated_verbs` (so "does not implement X" present-state claims don't false-block). Lesson reconfirmed: a parametrized test that only covers the profiles YOU listed (`full/bugfix/goal`) silently omits the one that matters (`standard`); adversarial breadth > author breadth. R1 non-negotiable, again.

- [decision] **M5 Self-Evolving Eval — pressure cases + auto-seed + noise gate (2026-06-25, 2 pipelines: run_b250caf1 + run_0305426d)** — Upgraded self-eval from testing KNOWLEDGE (ask-directly, grade the answer) to testing APPLICATION-under-pressure, and made the suite begin to grow itself. **Part 1:** 6 in-flow behavior cases (3 trap + 3 negative control, `GS_T4_*`) reusing `trajectory_capture`+`decision_rubric`, zero new infra. Trap = efficient-but-wrong framing with NO protective cue; PASS requires the agent to self-initiate the protective read AND cite the governing rule by name (not just "advise caution" — that's breadcrumb-following). Models COE10/CLASS_A/R16b. Each trap paired with a negative control where proceeding fast IS correct (guards against rewarding reflexive heavy-process, a PRODUCT non-goal). **Part 2 (swarmai 5ff52ad6):** `auto_seed_case` now emits `tier=draft` trajectory skeletons from REAL classified corrections; noise gate routes seeding through the existing `judgment_classifier` (only `counter_state=pending_confirm` CLASS_A/B/C seeds; removed the blind UNCLASSIFIED hot-path seed that dumped `GS_C_test-ses_*` junk); `eval_trajectory_capture` skips `tier=draft` so unrefined skeletons never score/spawn; briefing surfaces the draft backlog. 263 tests green. **Division of labor that worked:** machine finds WHAT to test (detects recurring class, seeds skeleton); human designs HOW (the pressure scenario) — auto-generating the full case is a tautology.

- [pitfall] **Self-review covers self-assumptions, not reality — adversarial is the only fix (2026-06-25, run_0305426d)** — Shipped a HIGH bug into REVIEW that BUILD + REVIEW + my OWN SMOKE test all passed: I claimed "draft skeletons are excluded from the eval score" and wrote a SMOKE proving it — but my SMOKE only exercised the DEFAULT path (where the `eval_method=behavior` filter drops them). It never tested the `behavior_trajectory`-tag path that BYPASSES that filter, where an unrefined skeleton WOULD spawn a real agent and fold a tautological "read-the-doc" pass into the score. `eval_trajectory_capture` had no `tier=draft` guard. The fresh adversarial Gate-2 agent's 7-vector attack found it. Lesson: a self-authored test inherits the author's blind spot — it covers what I ASSUMED, never what I missed. My confidence ("exclusion is unconditional") was the counter-signal; it was conditional. Also: **honesty must live in CODE, not prose** — the skeleton self-marked as a placeholder in its title/rubric text, but the runner never reads prose; the fix was a `tier=draft` code skip. Reconfirms why Gate 2 is non-negotiable (R1).

- **MessageStore Single-Writer (2026-06-17, 5 pipelines)** — 45 setMessages → 1 store. Key lessons: (1) Phase machine (idle/streaming) makes dangerous ops structurally impossible during streaming, not just "checked at call sites". (2) Eager store creation eliminates entire class of fallback code. (3) `endStreaming()` flush semantics — any replace() after needs _reconcileGen++. (4) Strict cross-tab isolation: NEVER use runtime state (streaming/idle) to bypass identity checks (tab ownership). The `store.phase !== 'streaming'` escape hatch CAUSED the cross-tab message leak it was meant to prevent. (5) "Read paths are also write paths" — restoring messages without seeding store IS a store consumer (COE10 bug #6).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **E2E Verification Chain + Deploy Unification (2026-06-17, 3 pipelines)** — Three-layer automated verification. Key lessons: (1) No Playwright/Cypress — regressions are logic/state-machine, not visual. (2) Fixture-based contract tests give 90% of integration confidence at 0% of cost. (3) Auto-scope detection via `git diff $deployed_hash..HEAD` makes deploy intelligent. (4) Canary as scheduled job (not manual step) provides continuous validation without human discipline. (5) daemon `.version` git hash is the keystone for deploy auto-scope — without it, can't diff.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Streaming Interruption Fix + Unified Persist (2026-06-18, 2 pipelines)** — Two bugs fixed + knowledge system unified. Key design decisions: (1) **Queue-before-force: eviction cost >> queue cost (100:1 asymmetry)** — 800K token context lost vs 60s wait. Grace period is meaningless if upstream callers immediately escalate to force=True. Grep ALL callers of bypass flags when adding protection mechanisms. (2) **Continue button always-visible > heuristic-triggered** — zero false negatives, zero complexity, matches Claude Code /continue. Users who don't need it ignore it (same as Copy button). (3) **Knowledge routing: content-type determines destination** — "换个项目还有用吗？" YES→MEMORY.md, NO→Projects/<X>/DDD. Not where the user is typing, but what the content IS. (4) **Governance boundary = target file judgment** — if it would change SOUL/AGENT/STEERING = governance (→ s_self-evolution). Everything else = knowledge (→ s_persist). Two-part detection (action+target keywords) prevents "standing rule: prefer atomic writes" false positive. (5) **One schema two consumers** — `persist_routing.py` ROUTING_TABLE consumed by both auto hooks (ddd_cultivation) and manual skill (s_persist). Deriving SAFE_APPEND_SECTIONS from the table eliminates drift. Never maintain parallel classification logic.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Session Unit Strangler-Fig Extraction (2026-06-18, 6 pipelines)** — God module tamed: 4167→2802 lines (−33%). Key decisions & lessons: (1) **Facade-first proves interface cheaply** — 125 LOC facade investment validated the boundary before moving 890+530 LOC. Wrong interface discovered at 1% of the cost of wrong migration. (2) **`self.`→`self._parent.` mechanical transformation** — zero-creativity refactoring. 176 field accesses changed with find-replace, no logic decisions. (3) **Lazy imports break circular deps** — `from .session_unit import SessionState` inside method body avoids import-time cycle. (4) **Removed `__slots__` to enable `mock.patch.object`** — testability > 64 bytes memory savings per instance. (5) **Stubs retained where external callers exist** — `_read_formatted_response` stub stays because `continue_with_permission` + 4 test files call it directly; removing forces 6-file update for zero value. (6) **Bug localization is the real win** — "which of 3 files?" replaces "which of 4167 lines?" for every session bug. Recovery path test baseline (70 tests) locked before any extraction started — STEERING #4 compliance.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Frontend Streaming State Machine (2026-06-18, 5 pipelines P1-P5)** — Explicit 11-mode reducer replaces 18 implicit boolean flags. Key decisions & lessons: (1) **Matrix testing is O(N×M) complete** — 11 modes × 20 events = 300 tests. Covers every transition including "invalid event in this mode = no-op." No boolean combination testing could achieve this coverage. (2) **Shadow mode coexistence is correct architecture** — boolean flags AND state machine updated by same single-writer (`setIsStreaming`). Both stay in sync by construction. Removal of booleans is cosmetic (P6 DEFER was correct). (3) **`pendingStreamTabs` provides React re-render trigger** — `tabMapRef` is a `useRef<Map>`, mutations don't re-render. The useState Set is the ONLY mechanism for React to know streaming started. Cannot remove without alternative. (4) **Per-tab state machine on UnifiedTab** — each tab independently tracks mode. Multi-tab isolation achieved without global reducer. (5) **Pure reducer = specification** — `streamingReducer` IS the behavioral spec. Future regression = "reducer allows this transition but shouldn't" or "reducer blocks this transition but shouldn't." Testable at <1ms per case. (6) **DEFER > cosmetic refactor** — P6 (remove 30 boolean reads) was correctly deferred. Zero behavioral gain, non-trivial regression risk, re-render trigger dependency makes it architecturally blocked. Design: `Knowledge/Designs/2026-06-18-usechatstreaminglifecycle-decomposition-design.md`. Commits: 907e2890, ef9371f7, 46d14334, 03130ddc, 7e2307f6.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **SSE Streaming Reliability — State Desync Complete Fix (2026-06-20, 2 pipelines + follow-up)** — The "streaming truncates mid-content" saga fully resolved across 4 commits. Three independent mechanisms were conspiring to kill active streams:

  **(A) Backend streaming-state endpoint lying (a0589e95 + eae7fa0a)**
  `_recover_streaming_on_disconnect` transitions STREAMING→IDLE on SSE disconnect (correct for send() path), but `streaming-state` endpoint reported `streaming=false` based on enum alone. Frontend 15s reconcile poller saw idle → force-cleared. Fix: `is_generating_after_disconnect` property uses persistent flag + `os.kill(pid, 0)` PID liveness check. Unbounded by any timer — clears only when subprocess actually dies, user sends new msg, or kill/cleanup runs. Adversarial found the ship-blocker: initial approach using `_pipe_flush_task.done()` had 30s timeout gap (same bug deferred).

  **(B) Frontend reconcile killing active states (d82dce5c + ee360da6)**
  `waiting_input` (permission prompt pending) was treated identically to `idle` — reconcile force-cleared it. Fix: `ACTIVE_BACKEND_STATES` set guard skips `waiting_input` and `streaming` states. Time-capped at 120min to prevent infinite stuck state if waiting_input SSE event was truly lost.

  **(C) Eviction killing post-disconnect generating sessions (ee360da6)**
  Same-source bug as (A): eviction's `idle_units` filter checked only `state==IDLE`, which includes sessions with `is_generating_after_disconnect=True`. High-RSS sessions (1M context) were eviction target #1. Fix: added `and not u.is_generating_after_disconnect` to filter.

  **Key Design Decisions:**
  1. **Flag+PID liveness > task-duration detection** — `_pipe_flush_task.done()` is bounded by 30s timeout. Flag persists until subprocess actually dies. PID check via `os.kill(pid, 0)` is the only truth source for "is this process alive?"
  2. **State endpoint must report REALITY not enum** — endpoint consumers (frontend reconcile, eviction) make destructive decisions based on the response. Reporting enum value when reality differs = feeding lies to decision-makers.
  3. **Scoped immunity must have BOTH trigger-scope AND time-cap** — `waiting_input` immunity is correct but unbounded immunity = no safety net. 120min cap preserves the reconcile's role as last-resort cleanup.
  4. **Same-source bugs propagate to all consumers of the lying signal** — fixing the endpoint (A) wasn't enough because eviction (C) read `state` directly, bypassing the endpoint. Rule: when fixing a "lying signal", grep ALL consumers of that signal.

  **Avoided Pitfalls:**
  - NOT keeping STREAMING state on disconnect (would break send() which needs IDLE path)
  - NOT adding a timer-based grace period (timers always have a gap; PID liveness is definitive)
  - NOT killing subprocess on flush timeout (output persists to DB for reconciliation recovery)

  Commits: a0589e95, eae7fa0a, d82dce5c, ee360da6. Files: session_unit.py, chat.py, session_router.py, useChatStreamingLifecycle.ts. Tests: 165 backend + tsc clean frontend.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [decision] **Enforcing "no instance/user-drift in the product SEED files" with a runtime PreToolUse content-gate is the WRONG LAYER — NO-GO'd at Gate-1, zero code (run_e4c8b2ae, XG: "没有合理的方案就不要硬上").** The goal was sound (seeds ship to all users; owner-name/private-project/machine-path/snapshot-as-contract must not leak in). Two pipeline gates killed the mechanism before any code: **Gate-0** re-framed the door — this class of drift enters via a **dev-time agent `Edit` of `backend/context/*.md` SOURCE**, which NO runtime ingestion judge (`ingestion_gate`/`admission_band`) ever sits on (they only guard runtime MEMORY/EVOLUTION/DDD writes; grep: zero of their callers write `backend/context/`). **Gate-1** then killed the fallback (a content-scope upgrade to `governance_file_gate`) on three independent plan-killers: (1) **self-defeating** — the anti-drift rule itself lives in a seed (AGENT.md R30), so the first edit that adds a concrete negative example ("strip names like <owner>/<customer> from seeds") NAMES the forbidden tokens and the gate would DENY the very governance edit it exists to protect; live private tokens (customer/project dir names) confirm the collision is real, not hypothetical. (2) **false-positive machine** — a short owner token (2 chars) word-boundary-DENY is indefensible, and the USER.md `- **Name:** X — call him **Y**` format doesn't match a naive `Name:` parser. (3) **false timing premise** — the "5s hang-guard" the design reasoned against doesn't even apply (the hook is solo in its `Write|Edit` matcher group → bare-registered, no `wait_for` wrapper; hook_builder.py). **The durable tells:** (a) a runtime hook catches only the in-session agent's edits — it MISSES IDE/git/external writes, so it's a partial layer, never the root for a *source-file* invariant; the structurally-complete home for "keep `backend/context/` seeds clean" is a **pre-commit / CI check on the source tree** (catches all authors + all write paths) — AND `hook_builder`'s own HOOK-ADMISSION-GATE says enforcing SwarmAI's OWN repo hygiene inside a product-wide per-command hook (every user pays) is an anti-pattern. (b) **an admission gate that keys on "content contains a forbidden token" is self-defeating for any store whose job is to DOCUMENT those tokens as examples** — the rule that teaches "don't write X" must be allowed to write "X". (c) drift-prevention for seeds is a repo-hygiene concern (CI layer), NOT a cognition-ingestion concern (judge layer) — don't conflate the two doors. If revisited: CI content-check on `backend/context/` + a fenced-example/allow-marker escape for rule-text that legitimately names private tokens + a runtime-derived reference (not a hardcoded denylist). (2026-08-20, run_e4c8b2ae, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A bare-RELATIVE `/api/...` URL is a latent packaged-app bug: it works in dev (Vite proxy / same-origin) but in the packaged Tauri app the webview origin is `tauri://localhost`, so the relative URL resolves to the ASSET protocol and never reaches the daemon.** Any fetch/`src`/`file=` that must hit the backend MUST be absolute via `getApiBaseUrl()` (returns `http://localhost:PORT` desktop, `:8000` dev, `''` hive same-origin — safe in all three). Symptom this time (run_1dea02e1): 4 Canvas renderers (Pdf/Image/Video/Audio) built `/api/workspace/file/raw?...` → pdf.js "Invalid PDF structure", `<img>/<video>` blank; the frontend log's `@tauri://localhost` stack framed it. Regressed silently by a base64→streaming-URL refactor (31b22ee8) because tests + dev mode never see the packaged origin. **Two durable tells:** (1) grep `` `/api/ `` for bare-relative literals in any surface that renders in the packaged app — route them ALL through one `getApiBaseUrl()`-prefixed helper (RP58 single-authority; here `file-viewer/utils/rawFileUrl.ts`), so a 5th consumer can't reintroduce it. (2) a jsdom/vitest test CANNOT catch this (its origin ≠ `tauri://`) — the mutation-proof guard is "assert the built URL is ABSOLUTE (startsWith the mocked base)", and the real confirmation is a packaged-app smoke or a live `curl` of the built URL shape against the daemon. Not codebase-specific: any Tauri/Electron-bundled SPA + separate backend has this seam. (2026-08-19, run_1dea02e1, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **`npm run build:all` (Tauri DMG bundle) run from a NO-GUI shell (agent/daemon/ssh — no Aqua session) produces a DMG that mounts but does NOT auto-open a Finder window → looks like "install failed" when the `.app` is actually fine.** Root: `bundle_dmg.sh`'s Finder-prettifying AppleScript (writes the `.DS_Store` that holds window size/position/icon layout) can only run with a Finder/Aqua session; headless → it takes the `--skip-jenkins` branch and SKIPS it, so the DMG has no `.DS_Store` and no auto-open-window bless flag (arm64 branch only does `bless --folder`, no `--openfolder`). The volume still mounts (often as `SwarmAI 2/3/…` if old volumes weren't detached — name collision compounds the "nothing happened" feel). **Durable tells / workarounds:** (1) to get a normal-popping DMG, run `build:all` in a REAL Terminal (has Aqua session), never in this daemon/agent shell. (2) You don't need the DMG at all to install locally — the raw `.app` product is at `src-tauri/target/release/bundle/macos/SwarmAI.app`; `cp -R` it into `/Applications` directly (then `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` — the build is adhoc-signed, Gatekeeper will otherwise block first launch). (3) verify a "new app is running" claim by the RUNNING PROCESS's binary path + mtime (`ps` → `/Applications` vs `/Volumes/…`), not by the DMG. This is Tauri-macOS-general, not SwarmAI-specific. (2026-08-19, run_1dea02e1, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **一个 payload 副作用标记(如 `history_dropped`)绝不能挂在会被中间消费者拦截/吞掉的 control-sentinel 帧上——必须骑在会被【转发】的那一帧。** 本例(run_2d2e3258):restart-fallback 把 `history_dropped` 和 `_abort` 塞进同一个 dict 单帧 yield,而 `send()` 的 `if event.get("_abort"): return` 在转发前就吞掉整帧 → `history_dropped` 从没到达 router → 它绑定的 `mark_pending_by_id` 副作用永不触发 → pending-save 沦为**静默死代码**(丢用户消息且无告警;上一版 f20760be 的修复其实从没生效)。修法(仿 `CREDENTIALS_EXPIRED` 已验证的两帧惯例):拆两帧——frame1 = 带副作用标记的 error 帧【无 `_abort`】(被转发,副作用生效),frame2 = 纯 `{_abort}` sentinel(被吞,只负责结束回合)。**Durable tell:任何"帧携带一个下游必须执行的副作用标记"的设计,先问"这一帧会被哪个中间消费者拦截/return 吞掉吗?"——会被吞的帧只能携带 control 语义,绝不能携带必须触达终点的副作用。** 这是 event-pipeline / SSE-streaming 的通用陷阱,非本 codebase 特有。(2026-08-19, run_2d2e3258, source:manual)

- [guideline] **"immune" ≠ "un-archivable" — archive≠delete is what decouples them.** The MEMORY size-valve had 3 compounding TYPE FLOORS (evergreen-section early-return + per-entry `EVERGREEN_TYPES` skip + operational-only candidate collection) that pinned the live body above the LOW watermark forever once immune-type content alone exceeded LOW ("降不到就是bug"). Reframe (XG): once recall covers `.context/*-archive*.md` (FTS5+BM25), archiving a principle does NOT lose it — so type must set eviction PRIORITY (tier), never a FLOOR. Iron law: every archive run unconditionally reduces body to ~LOW; no type is immune from ARCHIVE (age-decay's never-DELETE is a SEPARATE, preserved mechanism). Tell: when a "value floor" makes a bounded resource un-boundable, ask whether the protected thing is actually LOST when moved — if a recall-backed cold store exists, immunity-from-archive is the bug, not the safety. (2026-08-14, run_f6ab7207, source:manual)

- [guideline] **A "worklist exclusion" is only safe if it's STRUCTURAL, not incidental.** Excluding COE/Open-Threads/Standing-Preferences from per-entry size-archiving (unsafe `###`/emoji/footer shapes) is legal ONLY because all three are COUNT-CAPPED so they stay ~4K << the 25K target — the ~21K regular sections always suffice to reach LOW without them. The one uncapped worklist section (Standing Preferences) was a latent floor; capping it made the guarantee structural. Plus a LOUD `logger.critical` backstop fires if the body is ever left above LOW (never fail silently). Tell: when you exclude a subset from a bounding mechanism "because it's small," make "small" structural (a cap) + add a loud backstop for the day it regresses. (2026-08-14, run_f6ab7207, source:manual)
