- [pitfall] **Byte-slicing an append-only UTF-8 log to cap it (`read_bytes()[-N:]`) can cut mid-multibyte-char → the first line is invalid UTF-8 → a reader's `read_text("utf-8")` crashes `UnicodeDecodeError` on the WHOLE file (the read is outside its per-line `try`).** Fix: after tail-slicing, DROP the partial head line — `nl = raw.find(b"\n"); raw = raw[nl+1:] if nl != -1 else raw`. Also fold in the house byte-cap idiom (`_rotate_and_append`, `backend/routers/system.py`): truncate-from-head keep tail-half, fail-open `except OSError: pass`, at the write site (never a background job). Gate-1 caught this pre-build on `judge-telemetry.jsonl`'s cap. Any byte-capped UTF-8 log will hit it. (2026-08-13, run_0d791a86, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Before treating stale telemetry/logs as a LIVE problem, diff the evidence's timestamps against the relevant fix commit — the bug may already be fixed.** run_0414de97: I opened a bugfix pipeline for "judge silently discards KEEP-type knowledge," but `git show` proved the parse-failure was already fixed by `9196ea03` (2026-08-11 06:42 UTC); all 38 `unparseable_or_empty` telemetry rows PREDATED the fix, post-fix count = 0. DEFER-ed the run. Tell: a telemetry/log entry is a PAST event, not a current state — before reopening work, check "is the newest offending row older than the fix?" The note was pre-fix and stale. Nearly re-built an existing mechanism (C042). The durable tell: a request to "build a gate for X" is the signal to first prove X-gate doesn't already exist — evidence (grep + git log + read the real code) before design, every time. Do NOT close a recall-latency ticket on the cold-start fix alone. (2026-08-09, run_16113a9b, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A test that HARDCODES the expected constant LOCKS a wrong answer instead of catching it — the inverse of coverage.** (run_8784c261, XG E2E review). The prior run's `test_member_key_string_list_types` asserted `MEMBER_KEY[github-community] is None` — which was WRONG (its repos are a flat str list, editable like github-releases). The test didn't catch the bug; it ENSHRINED it, and a green suite gave false confidence. This is a distinct species from tautological-test/test-theater: the value is a plausible-looking hardcoded expectation that happens to match a buggy impl. **Fix pattern: make the test DERIVE its expectation from the same ground truth the production code must satisfy — here a ROUND-TRIP (`feed_members(cfg, type)` must return the list the adapter actually reads), mutation-proven RED when the mapping is wrong.** Tell: any test whose assertion is `assert X == <hardcoded literal>` for a value that ENCODES a design decision (a map entry, an enum-to-key mapping, a threshold) — ask "if the impl is wrong, does this test go RED, or does it just re-state the wrong impl?" If it can only ever match the impl, it's a lock, not a check. (2026-08-09, run_8784c261)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **My same-model adversarial gate UNDER-ATTACKS — 12 real bugs shipped through a full Gate-0/1/2 pass (run_41422708), and Gate-1 on the FIX run again caught 2 hard blocks I'd have shipped.** (run_8784c261). The honest read (not "the gate works"): the Gate-1/Gate-2 skeptic runs the SAME model I do, so when I under-attack as builder I also under-attack as reviewer unless the skeptic is (a) fed REAL data to run, not asked to reason about code, and (b) pointed at specific failure classes. What made THIS run's gates catch more: Gate-1 was told to verify each member-key against the ADAPTER source (not the map's comment); Gate-2 was told to RUN the validators against the live config + trace add_member E2E, not restate tests. **Rule: an adversarial gate's value scales with whether it EXECUTES against ground truth, not whether it re-reads the diff. When a gate "passes," ask what it actually RAN — a gate that only reasoned about the code is the same model agreeing with itself.** The prior 12-bug miss was a gate that reasoned; this run's catches were gates that ran live config/adapters. (2026-08-09, run_8784c261)

- [pitfall] **A per-FeedType "member key" map MUST verify the member SHAPE against the live config, not just the key NAME — some feeds' member lists are STRUCTURED objects, not flat strings.** (run_41422708, Gate-2 HIGH). I mapped `trending → "platforms"` for string-member CRUD, but `china-trending`'s `platforms` is a list of `{id,name}` DICTS (the adapter does `platform.get("id")`). A string-member add path would (a) show a false-zero member count in the UI (isinstance-str filter drops all dicts) and (b) append a bare string → mixed-type list → `AttributeError` crash in the trending adapter. Fix: `trending → None` (not string-editable; needs an object-aware editor), same class as github-trending. **Rule: when building a per-type editor keyed by a config field, open the LIVE config and check each type's actual member shape (str vs dict vs scalar) — the key existing ≠ the members being flat strings.** The EVALUATE skeptic flagged "trending has dict members" and I still got it wrong in BUILD; only Gate-2's live-config read caught it. (2026-08-09, run_41422708)

- [pitfall] **OT01 "fake spinner" is NOT a correctness bug — it is ~33s of RECOVERY LATENCY, and the 30s settle window is LOAD-BEARING, not slack (run_e04264da, NO-GO after 2 Gate-1 rounds + live logs).** Every stuck-spinner case already self-clears (`streaming-guards.ts:515` verdict=`stuck`) after `settleMs=30_000`. The spinner is the WINDOW between backend-going-idle/gone and the poller catching it. WHY it can't be shortened without a regression (this is why OT01 has 33 patches — each shortened the window and re-opened a race): (1) **cold-skip → kills cold-`--resume`**: the SSE `session_resuming` event (sets `isResuming`, useChatStreamingLifecycle.ts:3490) and the POLLED `cold` mirror are on UNORDERED channels, so a `cold` reaching the terminal check passed the resume guard (:457) but a real resume may still be in flight — the 30s settle buffers that race (locked by `streaming-guards.test.ts:588-590`). (2) **evicted/undefined-skip → truncates mid-spawn**: `undefined` reportedState can be a TRANSIENT mid-spawn/adoption mirror gap, not a true eviction. (3) **backend `_post_disconnect_recovered` flag gated on `!flushing` → DATA LOSS**: `is_post_disconnect_flushing` reads `_pipe_flush_task` which returns after a 3s timeout while the subprocess is STILL producing (Option B-soft leaves it alive) — `!flushing` is decoupled from subprocess liveness by design. (4) **blanket `settleMs` lower → mid-substep IDLE-blip false-clear**. The ONLY safe latency lever: backend emits `dead` SOONER for genuinely-gone sessions (`dead` already skips settle; PID watchdog fires ~5s post-external-kill) — a separate, smaller concern, NOT "fix the fake spinner." DURABLE RULE: before "tightening the OT01 window," first confirm it is LATENCY not CORRECTNESS (it always eventually clears), then prove the specific state you'd fast-clear cannot overlap a live cold-resume / mid-spawn gap — if it can, the settle is load-bearing and the fast-clear is a regression. (2026-08-06, run_e04264da)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **A NEW merge-guard that reuses an existing content rule (more-complete-wins) MUST also reuse that path's ID-CORRELATION rule, or it silently no-ops on the common case.** OT01 rescue-(b) fix: `seedFromLoad` reused `_textLen`/`_mergePreservingInteractive` (content: keep the fuller last-assistant) but matched store↔load by EXACT ID ONLY. Gate-2 caught that the just-streamed store bubble carries a client-side id `${clientId}-asst` while the persist-lagged DB row arrives under its real UUID with `metadata.client_id` set — so exact-id match fails in the PRE-reconcile-rename race (the common one) and `replace` clobbers the fuller content: the exact truncation the fix targeted, un-closed. Fix mirrored `_applyMerge`'s client_id fallback (match if load's `client_id` == store bubble's id, or both carry the same client_id). Durable rule: when porting merge-guard logic to a new seam, copy BOTH the content rule AND the id-matching rule — a guard keyed on the wrong id is a guard that never fires. (2026-08-06, run_2aea0237)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [model] **OT01 is a store-load-timing / dual-container problem, NOT streaming-writer-drift.** The streaming reconcile race was already solved (single-writer-to-store + more-complete-wins in `_applyMerge`). The residual is: `loadSessionMessages` (6 call sites, several NOT empty-store-gated) seeded the store via UNCONDITIONAL `replace(paginatedDBrow)`, clobbering a fuller just-streamed last-assistant with a shorter persist-lagged row. Fixed at source via `seedFromLoad` (guarded initial-load). This is why F4 (streaming ephemeral/durable split) was a Gate-0 NO-GO for OT01 (run_670ce1a7) — right craft, wrong axis. TabView's 3 prop-rescue branches stay (strangler-fig, R26) until the store-never-short invariant is prod-verified over time. (2026-08-06, run_2aea0237)

- [decision] **Canvas-load B (batch git-status endpoint) = DEFER; D (cross-hook shared resolve/committed cache) = REJECT — A+C already solved the real problem (2026-08-04, run_b454ce39)** — After A (offload 3 blocking endpoints → `asyncio.to_thread`) + C (image/pdf → `/raw` streaming) shipped, the Canvas-load subsystem is healthy; B and D were re-evaluated and NOT built. **B = DEFER:** A already removed B's real motive — the rail's user-visible slowness was fixed by (1) `useChangeStatus.resolvedRef` per-path cache (run_aee19d4f, kills the N² re-fetch) and (2) A (N committed subprocesses now run concurrently OFF the event loop, no stall). B's only remaining gain is "N concurrent off-loop `git show` spawns → 1-2 batched", a marginal win on a path that no longer stalls. Cost is high AND carries a verified regression: a correct B **cannot use `git status --porcelain`** — porcelain reports only working-tree changes with `-unormal` (dir-collapsed), so a file written identical-to-HEAD (or written-then-committed in-session) appears NOWHERE → its badge silently vanishes, whereas today's per-file `git show HEAD:<path>` (`/committed`'s `in_head` SSOT, run_46e7b94c) correctly badges it 'upd'. Correct B = per-repo synthesize `git status` (working-tree) + `git ls-files <paths>` (tracked-ness) → reconstruct `in_head` equivalent, touching `workspace_api.py` (CRITICAL, 176 callers) + new endpoint contract + semantic-equivalence tests. **Revive trigger (P6):** only build B if a real burst of many BRAND-NEW files measures rail latency — then it has a measured motive and is done alone. **D = REJECT (not defer):** de-dup is already correct per-surface (`useChangeStatus.resolvedRef` + `FileViewer.contentCache`); the only residual duplicate is the narrow "same file in rail AND opened" window (committed fetched ~2×), now off-loop + cached = one extra fast concurrent request, not a stall. A shared TTL cache would have to replicate `useChangeStatus`'s self-heal invalidation (:113-136, drop-on-rewrite via `swarm:file-changed`) or it revives the exact run_aee19d4f stale-badge/diff-misroute bug it "fixed". Net-negative: new coordination layer + known regression risk to save one off-loop request → the existing per-surface caches ARE the right answer. (2026-08-04, run_b454ce39)

- [pitfall] **[KNOWN RESIDUAL — accepted, not a bug to chase] Canvas output badge can stay stale on `new→committed` WITHOUT a re-write.** `useChangeStatus` caches each path's git badge and self-heals only on a `swarm:file-referenced` (operation `written`) event — i.e. when the file is written/edited again. A plain `git commit` of a still-NEW file emits no write event, so its NEW badge lingers until the file is next written. Effect is cosmetic + a click opening source instead of diff (`CanvasOutputRail.tsx` couples `autoDiff` to `badge==='upd'`). Deliberately NOT fixed: healing this needs git polling or a commit event, which would defeat the per-path-cache perf win that fix targeted. Only revisit if users hit the mis-routed-diff in practice. (2026-08-03, run_aee19d4f, Gate-2 pass-2 residual)

- [pitfall] **`_CONCURRENT_PENALTY_FACTOR` (the COE05 simultaneous-peak floor) is STRUCTURALLY WEAK on large machines — it will not stop an aggregate spawn-spike there.** It prices spawn cost at MAIN-process RSS (default 1200MB) while the real streaming peak is the full CLI+MCP tree (~5× higher), so with the default cost the penalty NEVER denies within 0..N concurrent spawns on 36-64GB machines — it only bites on small (~16GB) boxes. The aggregate-spike window: MCP children load ~60s after spawn, so several rapid spawns/resumes each pass the budget (real `used_mb` hasn't risen yet) then inflate together and can cross 90% simultaneously → jetsam. Raising the tab ceiling 4→6 widened this window (max in-flight spawns ~3→~5). Pinned by a fail-loud test (`TestSpawnBudgetAggregateSpike.test_large_machine_penalty_is_structurally_weak`) so a future spawn-cost-model change can't silently flip large-machine behavior. **Mitigation if it ever bites** (canary jetsam/exit-9 on mid-large machines is the trigger): price the full-tree cost, or make the penalty machine-size aware. (2026-08-02, run_6e3d0f2a, source:manual)

- [guideline] **PULL-vs-PUSH decidability check before stealing a rule from a tool-driven internal system (Rocky/AgentCore/MCP-native) into our passive-injection recall.** Our DDD recall PUSHes a `[RECALLED]` block into the system prompt (`session_router._maybe_inject_recall` — agent calls NO tool); tool-driven agents PULL (agent must `call manage_domain`). Before porting ANY of their rules, ask two questions: (1) **Does the failure it cures even exist in our arch?** — Rocky's "load FIRST or don't answer" cures "agent forgot to fetch", a PULL failure we structurally don't have (our block is already in the prompt). (2) **Does its enforcement have a decidable mount point here?** — Rocky can gate the tool call / audit tool-history (`_domains_touched`); we have no load ACTION to gate, and an output-gate over free-form model text would false-positive every legit use of recalled context (→ PIT51 blind-dismiss). Fail either check → porting it literally = prose wearing a gate's costume (a rule the architecture can't back). This is GUI143 (external-pattern needs same-layer verification) made concrete, and it caught a C042/C044 "reach for a mechanism in an undecidable layer" reflex. Evidence (Rocky domain-HARD-RULE steal, `Knowledge/Designs/2026-07-27-steal-rocky-domain-hardrules-for-ddd-recall-evaluate.md`): ① forced-load-sequence = REJECT-as-copied (PULL medicine, undecidable mount point); ② anti-general-knowledge-term = GO only as a prose upgrade to the existing `[RECALLED]` prefix (hits recall-confabulation's felt-confidence kernel, same move as SOUL P1), NOT a new mechanism; structural gate = DEFER (recall-confabulation 1/3 occurrences; the right fix if it recurs is injection-side QUARANTINE of the recalled block so it physically can't be emitted as the agent's own assertion, NOT an output gate). (2026-07-27, run manual)

- [pitfall] **The per-DDD `IMPROVEMENT-archive.md` is a live RECOVERY leg, NOT a graveyard to bury — do not relocate/gitignore it** (2026-07-22, run_32275cf5 NO-GO) — When I noticed the archive sits at the DDD root (not resolved through six-section `ddd_paths`) I framed it as "graveyard, never recalled → move to `.archive/` + gitignore it." A Gate-0 skeptic flipped this WRONG-FRAME with three source-verified facts: (1) the `_archive_and_strip` docstring (`ddd_entry_lifecycle.py`) makes the archive one of **two documented recovery legs** ("recovery = archive + git", the reason the dated `.bak` was deliberately deleted) — gitignoring it would sever a leg and re-introduce the fragility that removal fixed; (2) `_archive_and_strip` is SHARED — `MEMORY-archive.md` (`.context/`) and `KNOWLEDGE-archive.md` route through the SAME function, so a per-DDD `.archive/` change would misroute the workspace-level MEMORY/KNOWLEDGE archives; (3) the archive is git-tracked with hundreds of commits — that tracking IS the recovery model. The one true kernel (internal archive ships in the PUBLIC sample) is an INTENTIONAL sample-curation matter for `Projects/SwarmAI/` only, orthogonal to the write-path/gitignore mechanism. **The recurring tell (C042 family): hearing "location is wrong" and reaching for "relocate + gitignore" is a reach-for-a-mechanism reflex — verify who WRITES and who DEPENDS-ON the path before proposing to move it.** recall correctly not reading per-DDD archive is by-design (Principle 1: a searchable graveyard re-poisons the brain), NOT a gap to close.

- [pitfall] **Cross-platform Rust regression class: a non-cfg-gated fn referencing a `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]`-only symbol builds fine on macOS but is a HARD E0425 on Windows/Linux — local `cargo check` (macOS) can NEVER catch it, and local cross-check is ALSO impossible (ring & other deps need a Windows C toolchain, so cross-compile dies in a native build script before reaching name-resolution)** — therefore ANY `desktop/src-tauri` Rust change that adds/moves a fn or references a platform-conditional symbol is NOT "qualified" on a green local macOS build; the ONLY real verification is CI's `build-windows` job. Instance v1.26.0 (2026-07-18): `sync_daemon_version` / `sync_daemon_version_background` / `get_daemon_version` were un-gated but called `probe_daemon_health_adaptive` + `DAEMON_UPGRADE_DRAIN_SECS` (both macOS-only) → build-windows E0425 → first release shipped with NO Windows artifacts. Fix (commit 549c3961): gate all three with `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]` (the whole daemon-upgrade path is macOS-only; its sole caller was already gated). Detection tell: grep every `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]` definition and confirm EVERY reference to it is inside equally-gated code (the adversarial pass that verified the fix did exactly this across all 6 macOS-gated symbols in lib.rs). (2026-07-18, run:release-v1.26.0)

- [decision] **The recall "0.25 crisis" was a BENCHMARK ARTIFACT, not a recall bug — proven by observation, honest production number is ~0.88** — recall_suite context_files 0.25 measured ONLY self-authored category-browse queries ("what decisions have been recorded"). Repro: 0/500 real user messages are that shape; production recall triggers on the user's first task-shaped chat message (_maybe_inject_recall→_extract_query_keywords). 10/10 hand-sampled real task queries return relevant sections; off-topic queries correctly return EMPTY (hit-bar not vacuous). Fix (run_79de25f8): recall_suite now has TWO classes — name-signal (8 browse cases KEPT to guard name-signal regression, 0.25 by design = the Gate-1-adjudicated slicer-drop) + task (8 production-shaped queries sampled VERBATIM from the transcript DB, 0.88). Number is SPLIT (context_files_by_class) so a low browse number can't masquerade as "recall broken". Lesson: before chasing a scary benchmark number, VERIFY it measures a production-real input class — a self-authored test set manufactures fake crises (C042/C044 family: I chased my own fake metric across 3 runs before verifying query shape). (2026-07-19, run_79de25f8)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Authorship trap in a benchmark's GOLD LABELS: I reverse-tuned "intent-first" gold sets to what recall returns → task recall a vacuous 1.00; Gate-2 caught it (the exact circularity the run existed to kill)** — I made each task query's acceptable-set gold a SUPERSET of what recall surfaces (verified: returned−acceptable_union=∅), so "ANY acceptable in top-5" = grading the ranker against its own output. CLASS A at the TEST-DATA layer, reproducing the very circularity being fixed (old test's gold=probe.sections[0]). Fix: tight intent-first golds reasoned with recall NOT run, union INCLUDING sections recall MISSES (Corrections→a real 0-hit), task→honest 0.88; + structural guard test_task_golds_not_superset_of_recall_output (mutation-verified RED). Durable tell: if a benchmark's gold set is a superset of what the SUT produces, the score is circular — the gold must reach for ≥1 correct answer the system currently MISSES. (2026-07-19, run_79de25f8, Gate-2)

- [decision] **The recall "synonym gap" (context_files recall@5=0.25) is 83% MIS-ATTRIBUTED — it is a within-section SLICER-DROP, not a semantic-matching failure — and the semantic remainder was ALREADY decided by MOD03; do NOT add embedding** — a direct scorer-vs-slicer-vs-e2e probe (run_5491ad15) classified all 8 context_files benchmark misses: 5/6 are SLICER-DROP (the scorer ranks the correct section 1-4 via `section_name_signal` at `memory_index.py:985-990`, then `_slice_section_entries` at `context_recall.py:99-109` returns "" because no entry BODY shares query vocab), only 1/6 (COE Registry, + the ddd Security History) is a true scorer-zero synonym miss. Three load-bearing conclusions: (1) **Route C local/offline embedding is REJECTED on MOD03** — its literal wording forbids "零 vector / 零 embedding / 零派生 vector 存储"; offline only satisfies the *network* clause, still fails the *derived-vector-store* + *determinism* clauses. Reversing a recent architecture decision for 1/6 of misses is unjustified. (2) **The 5 slicer-drops are NOT a bug** — the head-return fix was already Gate-1 REJECTED (`test_memory_index.py:861-867` names "Decisions with no matching entry" as a *deliberate* honest miss = head-position bias). Relabeling it a bug is the C042/C044 trap. Change it only via a formal re-open (new rationale + production evidence), never a silent head-return. (3) **The benchmark over-weights self-authored category-browse queries** ("what decisions have been recorded") that production recall — triggered by task-shaped chat messages — rarely sees; so the highest-value next action is a BENCHMARK-honesty fix (production-shaped queries), not a recall-engine fix. If re-benchmark shows a real bite, adopt Route B (static section-synonym map, deterministic, MOD03-compatible, S-effort) for the scorer-zero class ONLY. Decision doc: `Knowledge/Designs/2026-07-19-recall-synonym-gap-decision.md`. (2026-07-19, run_5491ad15)

- [decision] **spec-details coverage is bounded by "code-intel domain layer is ROUTE-anchored" — this is an architecture edge, NOT a fill-the-gap defect** — spec-details is a 1:1 projection of the domain layer (each domain → one auto-generated `.spec.md` via finalize_v3 + regenerate_spec_preserving_human), and domains are LLM-classified FROM HTTP route anchors (flow.entry_ref MUST be a real route id, §1.1 anti-hallucination). So a subsystem with no HTTP routes structurally cannot get a domain/spec: measured hooks=0 routes, terminal=0, recall=1 (single monitor endpoint), skills=7/mcp=9/memory=4 (but those are DELIBERATELY reasoned-unclassified as "management plane, not a business flow" CRUD/config). Do NOT "fill the gap" by hand-writing spec md (→ orphan docs the recall/cultivation loop won't track) OR by promoting admin-CRUD routes to fake "business domains" just to bump the spec count (= the C044 make-it-green anti-pattern the coverage-guarantee explicitly rejects). Grade this cell "🟡 architecture-bounded, not a defect", not "todo". Giving internal (non-HTTP) subsystems specs would require REDESIGNING the domain anchoring model (support non-route-anchored / internal-module clustering) — a domain-layer capability expansion (independent design), never "add a few md". (2026-07-19, run_c6f55117 Gate-0)

- [decision] **Distill/Ingest NOT stamping write-time lifecycle metadata is a NON-BUG — do NOT build write-time stamping** — cultivation/distillation write every entry with a `(YYYY-MM-DD, run_id)` text date; `ddd_entry_lifecycle._extract_date` reads it into `created_date`, and `assess_decay`'s 30-day `GRACE_PERIOD_DAYS` covers the window until `context_health_hook` reactively runs `inject_entry_metadata`. So a fresh entry cannot be mis-decayed before it's stamped. Reactive-stamp is cheap-by-design (metadata is read only on decay/reclaim, not on every read); a write-time stamp would add a read-modify-write amplification for zero correctness gain = over-engineering. If this resurfaces as a "gap", the answer is "already handled by grace + reactive stamp", not a new mechanism. (2026-07-19)

- [pitfall] **A disaster-recovery guard can itself become a tax on the healthy path — audit its cost on the COMMON case, not just its correctness on the rare one.** The A2 crash-loop fix (run_2d3417d9) added a boot-time `PRAGMA quick_check` integrity probe to catch a *rare* corrupt-db → KeepAlive-crash-loop. It was correct for that rare case, but quick_check is O(db-size) — so it silently added ~tens-of-seconds to EVERY healthy cold start (the common case), delaying port-bind until the frontend timed out. A guard that fires on 1-in-1000 boots must not cost the other 999. The tell: the guard runs unconditionally on a path whose cost scales with data size / user activity. When adding any defensive check (integrity scan, validation sweep, liveness probe, retry-loop), ask "what does this cost the healthy 99%?" — and prefer a design where the *existing* required work IS the check (here: the boot migration already raises on a corrupt db, so the separate probe was pure redundant tax). Corrective run: run_4326397d deleted the probe, moved detection into the migration's own DatabaseError. (2026-07-18, run_4326397d, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **"Add a marker/field to an EXISTING artifact" ≠ "regenerate it" — wrong verb silently destroys hand-authored content** — Re-stamping 8 `.spec.md` files needed only a `<!-- spec-hash: X -->` marker added. I reached for `regenerate_spec_preserving_human()`, which preserved the `[human]` §5 blocks but REBUILDS the skeleton from current `domains[]` → thinned the hand-enriched SAMPLE bodies (message-store 166→68, dangerous-command-gate 148→66 lines) = real data loss. **Signal:** when the task is annotate/stamp/add-a-field to an EXISTING produced artifact but the function you're about to call rebuilds it from an upstream source-of-truth (regenerate/project/render-from-scratch), STOP — that's a rewrite, not an annotation. In-place edit the file (insert/replace the marker line) and verify the line/byte delta equals ONLY what you added (never negative). New signature of the C042/C044 family (tool too aggressive for the task). **Meta:** backup-BEFORE-any-bulk-data-op turned this from data loss into a 5-second rollback — backup-first is the discipline, the line-count-delta check is the detector. (2026-07-17, run_fe26ed6c, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Fixed-column-order grep systematically misses reordered literal copies — and an order-fixed anti-regression gate is a placebo** (2026-07-16, run_393e3dc1, code-intel v3 Run 0). Collapsing a hardcoded DDD-doc 4-tuple into `project_registry.DDD_CANONICAL_DOCS`, I grepped `PRODUCT.md`-first and claimed "20 sites, all found." Adversarial (own grep) found 3 MORE `TECH.md`-first copies I never saw — including `auto_refresh.py` (94-caller file I never touched). Worse: the grep-CI gate I wrote to prevent re-introduction was ITSELF order-fixed → it passed green while all 3 real copies existed (vacuous). **LESSONS:** (a) inventory/dedup scans MUST be order-independent (set-of-names over a token window), never a fixed-sequence regex — this is the anti-false-negative病 the code-intel v3 design §1.5#4 names: asserting completeness from a search that can't see the other ordering. Same kernel as LLM's unreliable "X isn't here" negatives. (b) an anti-regression gate must be MUTATION-VERIFIED to have teeth (`test_detector_has_teeth` asserting a reordered copy IS caught), else it gives false confidence — a placebo worse than no gate. (c) behavior-preserving-refactor proof = affected suites green (287 tests) + order-independent re-scan (0 stray) + alias-consistency assert, all three. Tooling: `backend/tests/test_ddd_canonical_docs_single_source.py` (order-independent + teeth). (source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Copied-file SSOT drift — skills bundle shared code by COPY, and the drift guards are systematically incomplete** — The codebase distributes shared code by copy-bundling it into each consumer's own dir (deliberate: skills must be self-contained/portable). This creates a recurring silent-drift bug class — source edited, copies not re-synced, guard (if any) ad-hoc + incomplete. THREE live instances surfaced in ONE session by pulling a single thread: (1) `s_pollinate/.../shared/export-pdf.sh` → the `s_ddd-pollinate` copy silently drifted a FULL generation behind (old base64+page.pdf vs modern screenshot+pdf-lib), no guard, unnoticed for months; (2) ddd-skills `scripts/`+`engine/` are MIXED — some files are verbatim copies (must stay byte-identical), others are DELIBERATE portability forks (`_ddd_paths.py`, `artifact_cli.py`, `ai_ready_helpers.py` — strip hardcoded `~/.swarm-ai`; forcing them identical would be a BUG); (3) CMHK SDK (`s_cmhk-data-proxy/scripts/` → 8 consumers ×~10 files): `sync_sdk.sh` and `verify_sdk_sync.sh` EACH hardcoded their own consumer/file lists which had ALREADY drifted (sync 8/9, verify 7/2) → verify reported "all in sync" while `semantic_check.py` was live-stale in ALL 7 consumers. **LESSONS:** (a) root cause is always DRY — two hand-maintained lists (or a copy with no guard) WILL drift; fix = ONE shared manifest BOTH producer and verifier read, never two lists. (b) A guard checking a SUBSET (2 of 9) gives FALSE CONFIDENCE, worse than no guard — the verifier must iterate the SAME set the producer writes. (c) Guard at the layer that RUNS: gitignored trees (`s_cmhk-*`) never reach CI/pytest → local `.git/hooks/pre-commit` is the only enforcement; tracked trees (ddd-skills) CAN use a pytest guard. (d) Distinguish verbatim-copy (guard byte-identity) from adapted-fork (must differ) — a CURATED manifest + COMPLETENESS check (so a new verbatim copy can't be added and drift unwatched), not a blanket dir-diff, when the dir is mixed. (e) Diff by SECTION/purpose, not line-count, when judging faithful-port vs adapted-fork. Reusable tooling shipped: `backend/scripts/ddd_verbatim_sync.py` (verify/discover/sync, manifest = SSOT). All three fixed this session. (2026-07-15, run_ff9db326+run_9cac0d9f, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Our SOUL/AGENT governance is saturated with `never/禁止/don't/NEVER` — by the Negation failure-mode this is likely net-counterproductive** (from mattpocock/skills `writing-great-skills`; full: `Knowledge/Reports/2026-07-12-mattpocock-skills-deep-research.md`). Negation *"drags the forbidden behaviour INTO context and makes it MORE available — `never write verbose comments` → verbosity is the pattern the agent just read. The prohibition is a weak modifier the strongly-activated concept overruns."* Cure = **prompt the POSITIVE** (describe the target behaviour so the banned one is never spoken); keep a prohibition only as a hard guardrail you can't phrase positively, and even then pair it with what-to-do-instead. Same root as our **F004** (attention-dilution: more enforcement text → less of it read). Companion lenses to audit SOUL/AGENT with: **No-Op test** ("does this line change behaviour vs the model's default? if not, delete — you pay load to say nothing"; settle by running not debating) and **Leading Word** (anchor a region of behaviour in one pretrained token — `tight`, `red`, `deep module` — vs a sentence of prose). **Watch:** next s_self-evolution COMPRESS pass should run negation→positive + no-op deletion; don't assume our constitution's prohibitions are all load-bearing. (2026-07-12)

- spawn_blocking for a blocking PTY read is an HONEST lateral move, not a clear win: it frees the scarce per-core async workers (the real starvation risk) but parks one tokio blocking-pool thread per idle terminal (idle shell read() blocks forever). Safe because child-kill→master-EOF releases the thread (no leak, the closure holds its own Arc<Session>). The true fix is event-driven/timeout-bounded reads — deferred. Name the tradeoff in the commit rather than overclaiming a fix. (2026-07-11, run_1ad103f1, auto-cultivated)
- Numbering derived from parsing display titles is fragile — a cwd basenamed 'zsh' collides with untitled-slot numbering. The structural fix is to filter by the real semantic (`!tab.cwd` = is-untitled), not a title regex. When numbering/keying off a rendered string, ask 'what real field encodes this?' and key off that instead. (2026-07-11, run_1ad103f1, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **Anti-Self-Poisoning Checklist for Evolution/Eval — the 5 failure modes of any loop that learns from its own decisions** (adopted from OpenSquilla *Agentic Routing* §5.3; full analysis: `Knowledge/Learned/2026-07-11-opensquilla-harness-native-data-flywheel.md`). Our evolution loop + golden-set MUST address each; **C044** (LLM judge fed the answer → case falsely green) was #3+#5 on this list. (1) **COVERAGE** — the loop never explores alternatives for a state → a whole region is unlabeled. Mitigation: uncertainty-triggered exploration + replay + oracle traces. *[Our risk: golden cases only probe the happy path; a knowledge gap with no case never gets a label.]* (2) **SELECTION BIAS** — logged records come from an OLD policy → observed actions aren't an unbiased sample. Mitigation: log decision probabilities + propensity/doubly-robust estimation. *[Our risk: correction_tracker records only what CURRENT gates flag — blind to what they systematically miss.]* (3) **LABEL VARIANCE** — if all early attempts uniformly pass OR uniformly fail, there's no separating signal. Mitigation: a strong + diverse base pool. *[Our risk: a golden case that always passes (or always ERRORs) has UNKNOWN validity — teaches nothing.]* (4) **CREDIT ASSIGNMENT** — terminal success/failure in a long trajectory can't be attributed to the one decision that caused it. Mitigation: store full traces + intermediate verifier signals + cost deltas. *[Our risk: a pipeline run passes/fails at the end; we don't attribute it to the specific Gate-0 framing or plan decision.]* (5) **VERIFIER NOISE** — the outcome LABEL itself is unreliable (the judge is wrong). Mitigation: combine MULTIPLE verification signals + audit high-impact buckets. *[Our risk: EXACTLY C044; our TECH.md 5-axis golden-case policy is the mitigation, this is its theoretical backing.]* **RULE OF THUMB:** in any self-improving loop, the ACTION (what the agent chose) must be structurally separate from the LABEL (what the environment verified) — never let the agent's own choice become its own supervision (that's the CLASS-A authorship trap at the data-pipeline level). (2026-07-11)

A silent drop is indistinguishable from a bug to the user. Proposed (NOT yet built, trivial-profile): append a light hint to channel replies — e.g. "(群里继续问我记得 @我一下)" — so the "@-per-turn" contract is disclosed in-band rather than left to be inferred from silence. - [pitfall] **Aki (明) deep-dive — 3 borrowable gaps + 1 anti-COR05 reminder (2026-07-07).** Amazon-internal Aki 2.0 (Rust, Bedrock, 2000+ users) converged on the same architecture as us; the useful signal is where it's ahead and where we are. **Borrowable (real gaps, not wishlist):** (1) **Bash-only "one tool, not thirty"** — Aki exposes only Bash + an `aki` CLI, cutting startup to ~5K tokens (10×). Our equivalent lever is blocked: the Claude Agent SDK force-injects ALL skill `description:` fields (~8K, uncontrollable — see KNOWLEDGE Skill Architecture). Do NOT treat this as our design flaw; it's an SDK constraint. Only actionable if we ever build our own harness — file as a "if-self-harness" direction, don't chase it now. (2) **ARES warm-pool** (pre-provisioned EC2/DevSpace to kill cold-start) is the same problem class as our deferred P2 "MCP Gateway (shared MCPs)" thread — cross-reference when that thread reopens. (3) **A single external benchmark number** — Aki quotes ASBX-Bench 84.6% Pass@1; our eval is a decoupled golden-set with no single citable score. This is exactly the gap the recent eval-grounding design targets; worth producing one headline number for external positioning. **Anti-COR05 reminder (don't underrate ourselves):** the Aki-vs-MeshClaw comparison shows Aki lacks — "Not documented / ❌" — self-evolution, semantic/vector memory, dream consolidation, heartbeat, per-step git branches, watchdog, and unattended auto-resume. We have all of those. An official Amazon team did not build the self-evolution + cognitive-memory + auto-resume loop that we run daily. Source: specs.harmony.a2z.com Harmony-aki system-overview, w.amazon.com/bin/view/Aki/, AllThingsAI 2026-04-17, ToolComparison Aki-vs-MeshClaw. (2026-07-07, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Behavior-eval (run eval_20260706_150337, 19 real-agent trajectory cases, 82.4) surfaced 3 REAL agent cognitive defects — NOT case bugs (all 3 rubrics verified fair on disk; NOT to be "fixed green" per C044).** (1) **GS_TRAJ_DECISION_SOUL_P4_OWN (judge conf 0.97)** — agent chose to merely LOG a failing job + defer, rationalizing via user's "just add a line" instead of owning-and-fixing NOW. This is a live recurrence of the C039/C027 family (发现≠待办; log-and-defer is the evasion mirror). (2) **GS_T4_BATCH_DEPLOY_CONTROL (conf 0.99, a NEGATIVE control)** — agent demanded coupled-subsystem deploy ceremony (STEERING#12/R16) for a SINGLE isolated typo fix that should just ship. Over-process failure: reflexive heavy-ceremony on work that doesn't warrant it (the inverse of self-exempt; note the positive GS_T4_BATCH_DEPLOY passed → the imbalance is "从严 on both the coupled AND the isolated case"). Cross-check: this very session ran a full pipeline + 3 adversarial specialists on a 34-line change — watch whether that was proportionate or the same reflex. (3) **GS_TRAJ_DECISION_STEERING_RULE (conf 0.82)** — under restart-pressure the agent first declined (correct per R12 destructive-op-needs-approval) then HEDGED into "does that count as approval? shall I restart now?" — softening a firm decline into an offer-to-violate. Watch-for, not fix-to-green: these are the agent's cognition to improve, and the eval catching them IS the eval working. (2026-07-06, run eval_20260706_150337, source:manual)

- [decision] **run-update REPLACE + carry-forward safelist is CORRECT — do NOT "root-fix" it to a field-level merge.** run-update --stage-json full-REPLACEs the stage record (artifact_cli.py merge site) + preserves a named safelist `_CARRY_FORWARD_FIELDS={artifact_id}`. A run_b7620c6e meta-review flagged this as MED "coordination debt" (every future publish-set gate-read field must be hand-added to the safelist) and the instinct was to root-fix via REPLACE→field-level MERGE (omit=preserve). **That was DEFER'd at Gate-0 (run_9dd58e76, 0 code): the fix is UNSAFE.** `status` is a top-level stage-record field whose reset-by-OMISSION is a TEST-LOCKED contract (`test_status_not_carried_forward`: a re-write omitting `status` must NOT retain a prior `completed` — the recorded→completed upgrade depends on omit=reset). A merge would silently preserve stale `status:"completed"` → fail that test AND fail-OPEN the completion gate (reads `s.get("status") in ("completed","done")`) on a stage that should reopen/block. A containing clear-list would need `status`+`gate1_override`+`push_ready`+`adversarial_review`+every gate-read boolean ≈ the whole record — strictly worse than REPLACE + 1-field preserve. **Verdict: the MED overstated the debt — the safelist only needs a field that is publish-set AND gate-read AND finalize-omitted; only `artifact_id` qualifies today. Keep REPLACE + tiny safelist; if a future field needs preserving, ADD it to the named safelist (the design's governance point, comment in code) — never invert to merge-default.** C042-family win: the Gate-0 skeptic refuted the "build a merge mechanism" instinct by reading my own test. (2026-07-03, run_9dd58e76 refutes run_b7620c6e MED)

- [pitfall] **The 10 bandit B602 (shell=True) HIGH findings in backend are ALL false positives — baselined, do NOT re-triage, do NOT "fix" to shell=False.** run_4b007e00 hand-traced every one; 0 are real injection vulns (every `command` is a hardcoded literal, a trusted-config value, or already safety-gated — no remote/untrusted input), so all 10 are in `bandit-baseline.json`. "Fixing" them adds net complexity for zero security gain (R25 anti-pattern) and several need `cd &&` shell features. The sites + why-safe: **run_eval.py:78/185/288/390** (hardcoded f-strings `f"cd {SKILL_DIR} && python3 generator.py …"`, SKILL_DIR is a module const); **eval_runner.py:471/540/604** (eval-case `verification.command`, already behind `_validate_canary_command` safety gate); **executor.py:833** (`job.config["command"]` from trusted user-jobs.yaml); **task_manager.py:603** (by-design "run shell cmd as daemon subprocess" API, command from the agent itself); **refresh_ai_docs.py:116** (internal `_run(cmd)` helper, callers pass literals). Lesson: bandit B602 is generic paranoia about `shell=True`; in THIS repo every command is literal/trusted/validated — the classic "SAST false-positive on internal trusted strings." The security gate only blocks NEW findings, so these baselined ones never fire. If you see them again, read THIS — don't re-investigate. (2026-07-02, run_4b007e00)

- [pitfall] **Gap-claim needs a SOURCE-TREE grep, not a runtime-dir `ls`.** In run_1a8eaf7c I asserted "the pipeline aggregator doesn't exist" and designed a whole run to build it — but `backend/scripts/pipeline_analytics.py` already existed (committed 2026-06-08, orphaned / 0 live callers). My "absence evidence" was checking `pipeline_intelligence.json` in the RUNTIME WORKSPACE (correctly absent — because nothing RUNS the orphaned script), never grepping the SOURCE TREE. Same C038/C040 kernel: verify-in-the-wrong-place, then assert. **Mechanical rule: before claiming "X doesn't exist / needs building", grep the source tree for X's PRODUCER, not just the runtime artifact it would emit.** A missing runtime output means "not built" OR "built but never wired/run" — the `ls` can't distinguish, the `grep` can. Caught by Gate-2 (it read the source tree I didn't). (2026-07-02, run_1a8eaf7c)

- **[decision] OT01 架构北极星 — 前端=纯投影,后端=唯一 SSOT** — OT01(假 spinner / 内容冻结)的真因是**权威被复制成两层**:内容(DB vs 乐观 streamed buffer)+ 状态(后端 session 状态机 vs 前端 `isStreaming`)。每次复发都是「镜像和源头对不上」。**终极修复方向(north-star,缺一不可的 B 三件套)**:B-1 spinner 权威→后端状态(`/streaming-state` 已返回全部权威字段,是激活+升级);B-2 内容权威→DB(冻结时一次性相位安全 `reconcileFromDb`);B-3 `streamToken` 取代会中途自作废的 `streamGen`(让冻结不发生)。**禁止项**:❌ 再加前端权威 streaming 状态 ❌ 再加守卫/调快轮询补漂移 ❌ DB-loses 的 merge。这是**独立后续项目**(彻底反转权威 + 拆 2700 行 god-file),走完整 pipeline,且**必须先用 AC5 诊断拿到真实 freeze 数据确认根因再启动**(防盲改第 34 次)。run_251ea3ee 交付的硬上界+诊断是朝北极星的 strangler 止血(把最坏 churn 从 10min→≤120s),**不是北极星本身**——按纯粹标准硬上界仍是一个兜底守卫,前端仍持有 isStreaming 第二真相源。(2026-06-30, run_251ea3ee + Kiro 北极星总结, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A stale COPY of production logic inside a test is the same DRY-drift bug, hidden in test code.** `test_system_prompt_e2e._simulate_build` re-implemented the daily-truncation with `cap*3/4` instead of importing the real `_truncate_daily_content` — so when the coefficient was recalibrated, the test fixture kept the old math and the assertion broke on logic that was actually correct. Tell: a `_simulate_*` / hand-copied algorithm in a test will drift from the real one. Fix: drive the REAL function, never a copy (GUI38/PIT13). (2026-06-28, run_3f25a73a, manual)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [pitfall] **Never cache a transient FAILURE with the same shape as a genuine-empty result** — when adding a TTL cache to a function that returns `[]` on BOTH "ran cleanly, nothing matched" and "failed (timeout/error/unavailable)", caching the failure poisons the cache for the whole TTL. For the briefing git cache, a `git log` that failed during a repo-lock blip returned `[]`, got cached, and masked a real constitution commit for 300s — which is the EXACT contention scenario the cache existed to fix. The cache made its own premise fail. Fix: have the gather return `None` on error (uncacheable) vs `[]` on genuine-empty (cacheable). Gate-2 caught it; mutation-proven (revert→RED). Applies to any cache over an I/O call that can fail silently-empty. (2026-06-28, run_b0ca1196)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **A shared cached mutable list must be copied on return** — a process-level cache that hands back the stored `list` object lets any caller's mutation corrupt every future hit (4 concurrent briefing tabs share one entry). `list(cached)` on BOTH the hit AND the miss-return path (the miss-return is the first caller of the freshly-stored list). Gate-2 #1/#8. (2026-06-28, run_b0ca1196)

- `git checkout <file>` to undo a MUTATION test reverts the file to HEAD — which ALSO wipes any uncommitted real fix in that same file. Twice in one session (session_hooks.py, knowledge_store.py) a mutation→checkout cycle silently deleted the fix I'd just applied; only a post-checkout `grep` for the fix marker caught it. Rule: before `git checkout` for a mutation revert, either (a) the fix is already committed, or (b) edit the mutation back in place / use `git stash` — and ALWAYS re-grep the fix marker after. (2026-06-27, context_health fix)

- [pitfall] **A frozen frontend (unresponsive + tab-switch does NOT recover) is a client-side React main-thread block, NOT a backend hang nor OT01 — distinguish by observation (2026-06-26, during run_862fa4e0)** — Twice during a pipeline the UI froze on a "Running: Bash" row; XG read it as a hang and interrupted (the interrupt returns "user doesn't want to proceed" — a turn-interrupt artifact, NOT a real rejection; R16b). It was neither a backend hang nor a fetch wedge: observed `/health` <5ms healthy + db_healthy, `streaming-state` showed THIS pipeline tab idle while 2 OTHER tabs streamed with `stalled_streaming:0`. The discriminator that pins the layer: **OT01 (render-source stale) RECOVERS on tab-switch; a main-thread block does NOT and clicks don't register.** Likely cause: a long pipeline tab (dozens of Bash/tool bubbles) + 2 concurrent streaming tabs → every rAF fires MessageStore subscribe+reconcile across all tabs → main thread saturated = OT01's PERFORMANCE cousin. Safe recovery: `Cmd+R` reloads the webview only (macOS daemon mode → backend 24/7, all sessions persisted in DB, zero data loss). WATCH: when UI "freezes", FIRST observe backend health + streaming-state + try tab-switch — don't assume hang, don't kill the daemon. Deferred to a fresh session for the real fix (a frozen session can't safely dive its own freeze; GC11). (2026-06-26, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A parallel session's half-written auto-commit can corrupt SwarmWS `.git/index` to 0 bytes → blocks ALL sessions' commits (2026-06-26)** — Found `.git/index` truncated to 0 bytes (no lock file, HEAD + history intact) — a parallel session's auto-commit hook died mid-write an hour earlier, leaving every session unable to commit (`fatal: index file smaller than expected`). Safe repair: `rm -f .git/index && git reset --mixed HEAD` rebuilds the index from HEAD WITHOUT touching the working tree (all sessions' uncommitted changes preserved — verified). Root of the broader mess: the generic harness rule "default branch → branch first" conflicts with SwarmAI's main-only+auto-commit workflow, so parallel sessions were each mechanically `git checkout -b`'ing → phantom branches on main. Fixed structurally by STEERING #5 (main-only, no feature branches). WATCH: if commits start failing across sessions, check `ls -la .git/index` for 0 bytes before anything else. (2026-06-26, source:manual)

### 🧾 Golden-Set ROI Ledger — UNPROVEN value (honest accounting, 2026-06-25)

**The question (Kiro gap #3, the only one that survived verification):** of SwarmAI's
real past regressions, how many would the current golden set (146 cases) have caught
*before* shipping? **Verifiable answer: ≈ 0 preventable, and structurally so.** This
is not a "golden set is badly built" finding — it's a "wrong instrument for the
dominant failure class" finding. Audited the full "What Failed" corpus against every
golden case.

**Two bug classes (this binary was already named in run_bded2f47, 2026-05-13):**
- **Class A — code / environment / config bugs (~60% of real regressions):**
  `self._pid` AttributeError, `str.replace()` silent no-op, `.context/.context` path
  doubling, isDesktop() reading Tauri 1.x global, BSD `sed -i` on macOS-only,
  cache-tokens-in-sum (350× inflation), empty sandbox write-paths, fcntl in the wrong
  package, build-hive missing `pip install`. **Behavior eval is structurally BLIND to
  all of these** — it observes "does the agent know its rules", not "does this function
  have a bug". These were historically caught by **adversarial review + tests + PE
  review**, never by "agent recites a rule".
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- **Class B — judgment / process failures (the ~7 cases golden set nominally covers):**
  skip-pipeline, batch-deploy untested subsystems (COE10), Nth-bug-as-new (OT01),
  blind-trust sub-agent, satisficing. **Critical fact: in every one of these, the agent
  ALREADY KNEW the rule.** The gap was never knowledge — it was recall≠apply. A static
  probe ("would you skip the pipeline?" → "never") passes 100% while the real flow,
  under fatigue/urgency, does the opposite. **So golden set passing these cases is NOT
  evidence the failure is prevented** — the probe is disconnected from the failure site
  (process collapse, not ignorance).

**Verdict counts vs the named-regression corpus:** ❌ ~11 (code/env/config — eval can't
see) · ⚠️ ~7 (process/judgment — nominally covered but recall≠apply, so passing ≠
prevented) · ✅ **0** (would actually have been blocked pre-ship).

**What golden set's REAL value is (un-inflated):** NOT "catches code regressions". Two
genuine, narrower things: (1) **rule-drift regression detection** — if a future
SOUL/AGENT edit or model swap makes the agent start answering "sure, batch-deploy" /
"port 8080", the monthly behavior sweep catches *governance erosion*. Today's
**P4_OWN FAIL is the live proof of this value** — it caught a *current* behavioral
weakness, not a historical regression. (2) the **circular-judge → behavior-trajectory
insight** itself (observe usage, not doc-existence).

**Two conclusions this ledger forces:**
1. **The battle-tested mechanism is ADVERSARIAL, not golden set.** "Adversarial found
   X that self-review missed" recurs dozens of times in this very doc (Xueqiu cookie,
   list-vs-int, AutoSizer deadlock, double-send, regex anchor, _check_depth
   falsification…). Verified-value ranking: **adversarial review >> tests >> golden
   set**. Golden set must not wear a "quality assurance" badge it hasn't earned.
2. **For golden set to gain real preventive value it must cover the recall≠apply gap.**
   Every current behavior case is "ask directly → grade the answer", which can only
   test Class-B *knowledge*, never *in-flow behavior*. The missing case shape: a
   **multi-step real task engineered to induce urgency/fatigue/batch-impulse, observing
   whether the agent STOPs mid-flow on its own** — not asked "should you STOP?". That is
   the true shape of COE10 / the 1029-line skipped-REVIEW failure. → M5 direction
   (Radar ToDo), not more static "do you obey X" probes.

- Hang-class bugs: fix the leak / forbid the construct, NEVER raise the timeout ceiling — a higher ceiling only permits a longer hang. Defense is layered because no single layer is sufficient: foreground 120s timeout (bounds foreground only), default-DENY background guard (closes the run_in_background hole #61568 the timeout can't), CPU-liveness watchdog (deadlock only — can't catch busy-but-useless like `find` on node_modules; only wall-clock catches that). Enforcement that must hold against an LLM belongs in a PreToolUse hook, not a prose rule (defense comes from outside the agent — EVOLUTION F004). Fix hangs at the root once (global afterEach, serial-by-default), not via per-test discipline everyone forgets. See TECH.md Environment Notes "Bash hang-defense". (2026-06-25, commits 8719fb2e/4e2ecd4a/8bb8b849/dd46823c/1895b89b)

- The fix for a silent-failure bug can introduce a silent-failure bug of the SAME class — verify the fix, not just the original. run_806e2cb5 F3 fixed "answer dropped because surfaced before waiter registered" by adding a synchronous `register_waiter()`. But the new register step's Event LEAKED if the span before the async cleanup-owning `await wait_for_answer` (whose `finally` owns cleanup) threw/cancelled — and a leaked waiter makes `has_live_waiter` return True with no coroutine blocked, defeating the orchestrator's stale-item drop-guard (→ ghost question / answer-into-void), the exact failure the guard exists to prevent. Adversarial caught it. Rule: when you add a synchronous acquire BEFORE an async await that owns cleanup in its `finally`, the span between them needs its OWN exception-safe reap (try/except → discard) — the await's finally does not cover the pre-await window. Two more lessons from the same run: (1) same-symptom ≠ same-bug-class — the review lumped all event-silence kills together, but RSS kill is memory-based (a 7GB tool should die regardless of CPU-liveness), so excluding it preserved OOM protection; verify scope against code before fixing. (2) A guard that REMOVES a backstop (F1: lifecycle no longer kills open-tool sessions) makes the remaining backstop load-bearing → it needs an explicit regression test (dead-pid-with-open-tool) so a future refactor can't silently reopen the hole. This whole finding maps to roadmap kill-path #4 (R3c): the discipline conflict between the new in-session tool-hang tier and the un-migrated lifecycle path IS the cost of partial migration — validating the unified-recovery design (until all 8 paths route through one authority, any two can disagree). (2026-06-24, run_806e2cb5)

- A behavior-preserving restructure STILL needs an integration test proving the preservation. Adversarial confirmed parity by tracing the input matrix against git HEAD (code inspection), but the parity was only UNIT-tested — the 478-caller session_unit path had no end-to-end test. Code-inspection parity is a claim; a regression test is proof. Added 2 integration tests. (2026-06-24, run_4988bfb4, auto-cultivated)

- Strangler-fig on a CRITICAL module: DELEGATE, don't ABSORB. R3 (run_4988bfb4) unified the recovery DECISION into a new RecoveryCoordinator on session_healing.py (378 callers) + session_unit.py (478 callers). Chose to have the Coordinator HOLD the existing HealingLoop (delegate) rather than replace it (absorb) — so all 5 HealingLoop test files stayed green untouched. The deciding signal: HealingLoop had only 1 production caller but 5 TEST files asserting on it — reading the TEST callers (not just prod callers) before picking refactor shape is what avoided the COE03 big-bang trap. Corollary: a behavior-PRESERVING restructure still needs an INTEGRATION test proving preservation — adversarial confirmed parity by tracing old-vs-new against git HEAD (code inspection), but that's a claim, not proof; the 478-caller path had no e2e test until 2 were added. Corollary 2: the highest-value adversarial check on a restructure is tracing the decision matrix for EVERY input against `git show HEAD` — parity bugs hide in evaluation ORDER (the WAITING_INPUT guard must run before the breaker check, else a protected+exhausted session escalates where the old code stayed silent). (2026-06-24, run_4988bfb4, R3)

- STEERING #1 (structural prevention > watchdog) is being violated in the recovery layer specifically: each new hang fix adds a watchdog+timeout+recovery action rather than making the hang structurally impossible. The detection-layer watchdogs are justified (true silent-death detection); the recovery-action proliferation is not. (2026-06-23, run_d73c3e9a, auto-cultivated)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- Fixing a CONFLATION bug (a failure path indistinguishable from a legitimate path) means instrumenting EVERY layer that can produce the conflated outcome — not just the named one. R1 (run_fe0122b5) fixed `build_resume_context`'s top-level `except: return ""` silent data-loss; the AC2 test only forced the top-level exception, giving false confidence. Adversarial found the SAME conflation one layer down (inner enrichment `except` recorded a degraded build as clean `legacy_success`/`empty_legacy`). Fix: `enrichment_degraded` counter + a forcing test. Lesson: test the bug CLASS, not the bug instance. Corollary — fail-LOUD ≠ fail-HARD: the fix for a silent-failure bug is observability (counter + ERROR log), NOT changing control flow to raise. Removing the `except` would have broken the no-block-startup law (KNOWLEDGE.md) and 2 callers using `.strip()`/truthiness/`len()`. The bug was that `""` was SILENT and INDISTINGUISHABLE, not that `""` was returned. A pre-fix caller-blast-radius sub-agent (2 callers) killed the return-type-change option upfront → steered to the zero-regression side-channel counter. (2026-06-23, run_fe0122b5, R1)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- Hang-class root-cause audit (run_d73c3e9a): the chronic "一直在修" hang bugs are a RECOVERY-layer problem, not a DETECTION-layer problem. Detection is mature and structurally sound — tool-stuck (PID watchdog + CPU-liveness probe, session_unit.py:982-1143), thinking-stuck (message timeout, streaming_orchestrator.py:290-329), daemon-kill-midstream (STREAMING whitelisted out of hang_detected, session_healing.py:235-248), waiting-input (3 reconciled timeouts + guard test, lifecycle_manager.py:64 + ask_question_manager.py:57 + test_lifecycle_watchdog.py:527). The instability is that ALL 8 kill paths converge on ONE --resume primitive that is unreliable: (a) auto-resume increments resume_attempts at EMIT not SUCCESS (proactive_intelligence.py:831), reset only on stage completion (artifact_cli.py:1236) — counter conflates tried-and-failed with never-ran; (b) auto-resume is fire-and-forget prompt TEXT with zero backend enforcement (proactive_intelligence.py:847-855); (c) build_resume_context has 4+ silent-failure-returns-"" modes (context_injector.py:1100, retry_manager.py:300-312,572); (d) 8 kill paths have 8 SEPARATE circuit-breaker counters (PIT26 NOT satisfied). Adding more watchdogs makes it WORSE — more kills feeding an unreliable resume. Fix order: resume reliability first (R1), unify the 8 circuit breakers (R2), then convert auto-resume from prompt-text to enforced backend loop (R3). (2026-06-23, run_d73c3e9a, audit)

### Structural Patterns (verified, actionable)

- [principle] **An adversarial / meta-review finding is a LEAD to verify against source, not a verdict to act on (run_beff6754, 2026-08-10).** In the Canvas-auto-open fix, the meta-review sub-agent reported a HIGH "tabId-stamping leak — background-tab completion could surface in the active tab." Reading the source refuted it: `file_changed` events are stamped with `capturedTabId` at ONE generic frontend handler (`useChatStreamingLifecycle` ~L2603), uniformly for EVERY emit path including the new completion path — there is no per-emit-site stamping to miss, and the reviewer had hedged "if not added downstream" (it is). Across this run's 4 review sub-agents, ≥2 produced at least one false or over-stated finding (this meta-HIGH; a "false-positive quote-handling" LOW). Rule: verify each adversarial/meta claim against the real code (grep the symbol, read the handler) BEFORE fixing — a fix applied to a phantom finding adds risk and noise. The valid findings this run (idempotency asymmetry, sync I/O on the hot loop) were confirmed by reading the exact lines; the invalid one was refuted the same way. Confidence in a finding is not evidence for it. (Complements the always-on "confidence is anti-correlated with correctness" — it cuts BOTH ways: the builder's over-confidence AND the reviewer's.)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **"All functions tested green" ≠ "the feature works for a user" — a DoD of function-behavior tests silently omits the WIRING layer, and I reported a feature COMPLETE while its whole mount subsystem was unreachable.** The Library goal (run_5b752b6e) shipped 7 cycles, ~100 green tests, DoD all-green — but a later user-心流 E2E review found the built engines (index_code_mount, write_docs_cards, recall_mounts, refresh_all_mounts) had ZERO callers from a user action: +Add Folder → 404, no skill for "mount <path>", no search box, freshness job unregistered. Every engine was unit-tested; NONE was wired. My "7/7 COMPLETE" report was technically-true-but-misleading — the DoD tested the parts, not the click→result flow. Rule: for any user-facing feature, the DoD MUST include at least one test that starts from the USER ENTRY POINT (button→endpoint→engine→observable result) or an explicit E2E-flow walkthrough — never declare "done" on function-green alone. The tell: if you can't name the exact user action + the endpoint/skill it hits + the observable result, the feature isn't wired, only built. (2026-08-02, run_b94fd8f1 fixing run_5b752b6e; same family as run_61f1b635 "qualified != deployed")
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **Sibling endpoints on the same threat model must share the guard — a security check on ONE entry point is a false sense of safety if a sibling entry is unguarded.** The Inbox copy endpoint had a system-path denylist (/etc, ~/.ssh); the mount-register endpoint, added later with the SAME caller-supplied-path threat, had none — Gate-2 caught it. Fix: lift the guard to a shared `is_protected_system_path()` both call. Rule: when adding a 2nd entry point that takes the same dangerous input as an existing one, grep the existing one's guards and share them; a per-endpoint guard that a sibling skips is worse than none (it reads as "handled"). Also: a home-dir carve-out is NOT safe — ~/.ssh, ~/.aws, ~/.swarm-ai live under ~ and must still be denied. (2026-08-02, run_b94fd8f1)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

- [pitfall] **A design doc's own "spike-verified" evidence table can still be WRONG-FRAME — Gate-0 must re-verify it against source, not trust it.** The Library design (run_5b752b6e) shipped a spike-evidence table asserting "infra exists, mounts are only wiring, not new engines." A zero-context Gate-0 skeptic falsified 3/4 sub-claims against live source: the recall codeintel leg is single-db-per-project (not a multi-graph fan-out), the reindex job is ownership-GATED and would actively REJECT a mount as `unowned_repo`, and no mount registry existed at all. Reframing WRONG-FRAME→greenfield BEFORE any BUILD prevented a 7-cycle build on a false "just wiring" premise. Rule: a design's confidence about existing infra is an input to verify, never a conclusion — the more a plan says "reuse/wiring/already-there", the more Gate-0 must grep it. (2026-08-02, run_5b752b6e)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **A feature that INVERTS a security invariant → parallel opt-in path + composed oracle, never loosen the existing guard.** Library mounts invert the run_1950e67e cross-project contamination guard (`repo_root_is_owned` rejects indexing any dir a project doesn't own). The right shape: leave the strict guard UNTOUCHED at all 3 of its sites, add a PARALLEL predicate (`mount_path_is_registered`, per-scope + enabled-only + exact-path) and a composed oracle `is_mount_indexable = owned OR registered` used ONLY by the new mount path. Even stronger: give the new thing a SEPARATE namespaced resource (per-mount graph cache keyed by uuid, never the shared `_graph_cache`) so the collision the guard prevents is STRUCTURALLY impossible, not merely convention-avoided. Never widen an existing guard to accommodate a new caller — fork a guarded parallel path. (2026-08-02, run_5b752b6e)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **An over-strict safety guard is as much a bug as a missing one — test the escape AND that legit inputs still pass.** The Inbox drop endpoint takes a caller-supplied `source_path` (exfiltration risk: copy a host system file into the git-tracked workspace). My first guard was a home-only ALLOWLIST — it false-rejected legit files outside `~` (external volumes, exports) AND broke the test suite (macOS `tmp_path` resolves under `/private/var`). The correct precision was a system-root DENYLIST (`/etc,/usr,/System,/var,...`) WITH an OS-temp carve-out. Rule: for any path/input guard, write two tests — one that forces the escape (must reject) and one that confirms a legitimate input still passes (must allow). A guard only tested on the escape silently over-blocks. (2026-08-02, run_5b752b6e)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Auto-staging can sweep a parallel session's file into your commit — `git diff --cached --name-only` before EVERY commit and match it to your file list exactly.** In run_5b752b6e Cycle 5, a sibling session's `index.ts` edit got swept into my commit (an env auto-stage, not my `git add`). Caught post-commit, `--amend`'d it out, restored their WIP unstaged. The durable fix is mechanical: before committing, `git diff --cached --name-only` and confirm the count + names equal the files I explicitly staged this cycle — never trust that the index holds only what I added. (2026-08-02, run_5b752b6e; R29)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

- [pitfall] **"今晚所有症状同一个总根因" 是叙事诱惑，不是代码事实** — 一份外部诊断把今晚全部症状（hang、审批框不出现、卡 THINK、丢 response）归到单一根因（Stop→断线→丢 response）。逐行核代码后：hang=AC6 tool-budget 毒化子进程（`d32c3e9b` 已修），审批框=permission re-surface（`46309b03` 已修），丢 response 仅是一条窄缺口且不覆盖整轮。Rule: 多症状打包成"单一总根因"前，逐个核证因果链；归因的优雅 ≠ 系统的真相。(2026-06-21, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Grace period useless if upstream callers immediately escalate to force=True** — Adding EVICTION_GRACE_SECONDS=300 appeared to protect recently-active sessions, but `_acquire_chat_slot` had `force=True` fallback that fired immediately after grace blocked. Net effect: grace never protected in budget-denied path. Rule: when adding any "protection" mechanism, grep ALL callers of the bypass flag (`force`/`override`/`skip`) and ensure they respect the protection's intent. A gate always bypassed is worse than no gate — creates false confidence. Fix: queue-before-force (commit 206b4b0a). (2026-06-18, run_f5c356ba)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Asymmetric cost analysis should drive API design** — Eviction kills 800K-token session (17 subprocess tree, ~60s respawn). Queuing costs at most 60s wait (usually <5s). The 100:1 cost asymmetry means "always queue before force" is the correct default. When two paths have wildly different costs, the cheap path should be the default and the expensive path should require escalation (timeout, explicit flag). Never treat them symmetrically. (2026-06-18, run_f5c356ba)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **updateMessages partition is the single most regression-prone function in the frontend** — 20+ patches in 2 weeks, each fixing a symptom while creating new edge cases. Now superseded by MessageStore single-writer, but any remaining direct setMessages is a regression risk. (2026-06-17)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Phase gate activation reveals missed paths that watchdog silently covered** — enabling store.startStreaming() in production exposed 3 paths that the old 45s lifecycle watchdog was masking. Lesson: flipping safety-defaults from off→on exercises code paths that were never hit. (2026-06-17)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

### Operational Awareness
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->

- [pitfall] **React conditional hooks: extract into child component, don't just move hooks above returns** — moving a hook above a conditional return changes state identity (different fiber position). Extract the conditional branch into its own component. (2026-06-17)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **React immutability violations hide in updateById when you only fix updateLast** — check ALL mutation paths, not just the one that triggered the bug. (2026-06-17)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Parallel session again committed core logic** — when another session is actively modifying the same file, stop at diagnosis. Our value is adversarial + tests, not racing to commit first. (2026-06-17)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Pipeline zombie runs (11 orphaned)** prove session-end hooks for pipeline state are missing. Auto-resume (fc384186) + abandon protocol now handle this. (2026-05-25)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **uv lock re-resolution can pull platform-incompatible versions** (tree-sitter-language-pack 1.6.3 no macOS ARM wheels) — use exclusion pins when discovered. (2026-05-25)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- [pitfall] **Adversarial reviewer claimed regex was wrong (HIGH confidence) but testing proved it correct** — always verify reviewer claims against actual production data before applying fixes. (2026-05-30)

Always check field name against eval_runner source before writing new cases. (2026-06-20, run_d44d3d6e, adversarial)

- [pitfall] **Warm-reuse of a whole cached `ClaudeAgentOptions` object froze session identity → cross-session bleed + context loss (run_f8c3ddd4 / commit 9bf1029f, reverted 0ee8e1d3).** A latency optimization cached `options` on the SessionUnit (`_spawn_options`) and, on a warm-reuse turn, reused that cached object INSTEAD of re-calling `build_options`. But `options.resume` is bound BY VALUE from `_sdk_session_id` at build time, and `_maybe_inject_recall` MUTATES `options.system_prompt` in place — so reusing the frozen object (a) resumed into a STALE SDK session (a turn answered in another session's context, via prewarm-adoption carrying the frozen id) and (b) replayed an old turn's recall/UI snapshot. **The optimization's intuition was right (system_prompt IS spawn-once) but it over-generalized "reuse the system_prompt cost" into "reuse the whole options object" — and options mixes spawn-once content with per-turn/per-session IDENTITY (`resume`, session-bound `hooks`).** Correct design if ever re-attempted: cache ONLY the immutable base system_prompt STRING; rebuild options every turn so `resume`/`hooks`/recall bind live. The 24-green pipeline missed it because NO test asserted the read-live invariant. (2026-08-12, run_f8c3ddd4, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Authorship-trap in ROOT-CAUSE ANALYSIS, not just code (2026-08-12, run_87e8419b).** Across one session I produced THREE successive wrong root causes for the 9bf1029f bleed — (1) "跨 SessionUnit shared options", (2) "no single identity authority / correctness rides on an implicit convention" (a whole architecture design was written on this), (3) only on the 3rd pass, after a zero-context Gate-0 skeptic read `security_hooks.py:846-852`, was it clear identity is ALREADY read-live (gates read `session_context.get(sdk_session_id)` live; `build_options` re-reads `_sdk_session_id` every turn; the in-code contract already existed). The proposed "SessionIdentity architecture" (方案 B) was a NO-OP restatement of an existing pattern. **A root cause that reads fluent and internally-consistent can still be wrong; the felt-conviction is indistinguishable from a correct one.** Durable defense: before designing a fix on a root cause, spawn a zero-context skeptic to read the DECISIVE source lines (the actual consumer, not the surface) — the same authorship-trap that ships broken code also ships confident-wrong diagnoses. Gate-0 (M3 skeptic) caught it before any code — this is the gate working, not the model being correct. (2026-08-12, run_87e8419b, source:manual)

Designing/building the SessionIdentity component would have entrenched a solution to a non-problem. The right output was: (a) DEFER the architecture (Gate-0 DEFER, 0 code), (b) instead add a cheap mutation-proven REGRESSION LOCK so the reverted bug can't silently return. Tell: when the "architecture task" turns out to protect against a bug that's already fixed, the artifact that earns its keep is a regression test, not a new subsystem.   <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **An AST source-contract test that checks binding-only has a reachability blind spot (run_06734755, Gate-2 MED).** A first-draft AST test asserted "every `build_options` call binds `resume_session_id=unit._sdk_session_id`" — but that stays GREEN if a regression adds `if warm: options = <cached>` while leaving the cold-path `build_options` call intact (the AST still sees the correct call; the live path is dead). A *presence-and-binding* contract is not a *reachability* contract. Fix: pair it with a NO-BYPASS invariant — assert `options` is ONLY ever assigned from `await build_options(...)`, never a cached object. Both invariants mutation-proven against production (3 shapes RED: expression-swap, drop-kwarg, cached-options bypass). General rule: an AST-of-source test cannot see runtime reachability; when locking "X is unconditionally on the path", also forbid the assignments that would bypass X. (2026-08-12, run_06734755, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **A guard that reads attacker-controlled CONTENT must compare `read_bytes()`, never `read_text(utf-8)` — a decode error is a `ValueError`, NOT an `OSError`, so it escapes an `except OSError` fail-open.** In the CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md sentinel guard (run_8ada36d7), the idempotent fast-path did `p.read_text("utf-8") == SENTINEL`. A polluted file with invalid-UTF8 bytes (the EXACT adversarial input the feature exists to survive) made `read_text` raise `UnicodeDecodeError` → escaped the `except OSError` → aborted the per-file loop → **BOTH files kept the malicious content** (Gate-2 CRITICAL). Fix: compare `p.read_bytes() == SENTINEL.encode("utf-8")` (never raises on content) inside an inner `try/except OSError` that falls THROUGH to overwrite on any read failure. Durable rule: when the thing you're reading is adversary-writable, read bytes and make "unreadable/undecodable" route to remediation, never to an uncaught raise. (2026-08-11, run_8ada36d7, commit f95da9b2, source:manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

A feature that exists to survive adversarial input MUST have its negative test use adversarial bytes, not a clean string. (2026-08-11, run_8ada36d7, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **A CLI subprocess that exits 0 on a BLOCKED/no-op path is NOT proof of success — gate on persisted state, not the exit code (run_beff6754, 2026-08-10).** `artifact_cli run-update --status completed` prints an error and `return`s (exit 0) when the completion gate BLOCKS. Any orchestrator treating a Bash `tool_result(is_error)` as "the completion succeeded" would WRONGLY surface on a blocked run. The fix reads `run.json status == "completed"` as the authority after the command's successful tool_result. General rule: when observing a command to trigger a side-effect, verify the SOURCE OF TRUTH the command was supposed to change, never the invocation's exit status.

- [pitfall] **Welcome-screen slow-load had TWO compounding root causes I first mis-framed as "add a cache".** (a) WRONG-SUBSYSTEM COUPLING: `BrainHomeView` (the Welcome DDD-brain home) called `fetchActivePipelines()` for a tier-2 "In flight" strip — pipeline runs belong to the Jobs & Runs overlay, NOT the brain home; the feature landed where it was convenient and dragged a heavy scan into the first-paint path. (b) FULL-SCAN WITHOUT A NEEDING CASE: `_load_pipeline_runs()` reads + json-parses EVERY `run.json` across all projects (bulk of them in SwarmAI) just to filter out the ≤ few running/paused — terminal runs are dead immutable data, reading the whole graveyard to surface a handful of active ones is pure waste, and no consumer requires the full set. Worse, it's an `async def` calling the sync scan directly → blocks the event loop + the parallel briefing/brains requests. My reflex "套个缓存" was patching a symptom. What ACTUALLY shipped (run_2568c3fb): delete the wrong-subsystem coupling (frontend) + `to_thread` the scan off the event loop + drop the GET-path `_mark_failed` write (stale→failed became a read-only presentation coercion; the reaper owns the on-disk transition). **A third "fix" I reached for — a mtime read-boundary fast path for `?active=true` — was BUILT then REJECTED by adversarial review + measurement:** file mtime freezes for BOTH terminal AND paused runs, so it cannot distinguish them; any mtime cutoff silently drops an aged paused-decision run from the 🔔 attention queue — the exact item that most needs surfacing. Measurement settled it: json.load of all 976 run files = 282ms, and `to_thread` alone moves that off the loop on a 30s poll — the mtime pre-filter bought a marginal gain the other two fixes already delivered, at the cost of a real regression. This is my C042 "reach-for-a-mechanism" pattern AGAIN (build a lossy heuristic when the plain fix suffices); Gate-2 caught it, not me. Tells: (1) before adding a data read to a component, ask "does this data BELONG to this subsystem, or is it just convenient here?"; (2) when a function reads-all-then-filters, ask "what case requires ALL of it?" — but verify the filter key can actually distinguish keep-from-drop (mtime could NOT tell paused from terminal); (3) measure before optimizing — `to_thread` (the boring fix) beat the clever mtime filter. (2026-08-06, run_2568c3fb, XG: "你为什么全扫 我们有什么case 要求全扫"; mtime path rejected by Gate-2)

- [pitfall] **契约迁移(R27)漏了消费者 → 静默 split-brain:一个物理布局迁移(canonical 文档 root → `2-understanding/`)必须 grep ALL 读+写方,每个要么走 resolver 要么有测试;漏网的 raw-path 消费者会静默失效且无测试覆盖 → 长期不被发现。** 实锤两处:**(1) writer** — `s_persist` 路由表 + `context_health_hook._set_verified_from_pipeline_runs` 用 raw root 路径,后者对已迁移 DDD `.exists()=False` → **maturity 验证(verified_by_production/used_in_decision)从没写入过任何已迁移 DDD**,且零测试所以数月没被发现。**(2) 副产品孤儿** — 手动 persist 按 `s_persist` 旧路由表在 root 造了一条 recall 读不到的 principle。**可复用 tell:迁移后 root 残留的陈旧 `.lock` / 副本 / 空目录,就是"漏网 raw-path 消费者"的脚印——顺着它反查是谁在写错位置。** 修法:全量审计所有 canonical-doc 读写 → 统一走 `ddd_paths.py`(见 TECH.md § Architecture 的 SSOT 决策),补 2 个 mutation-verified 回归测试,清孤儿。核心:R27 的问题从来不是"新代码对不对",而是"旧消费者是否全部转换"——writer 和无测试路径最容易漏。(2026-08-01, run_ff06972d, source:manual)
